284 — Type Soundness Proof Project

Goal: Build, from scratch in Rust, a mechanized-metatheory framework that defines typed object languages (the simply-typed lambda calculus and extensions) with their operational semantics and typing rules, and machine-checks their type-soundness proofs. It provides: a de Bruijn object-term representation with capture-avoiding substitution; an inductive rule engine with an executable type checker and small-step stepper; a small trusted logical-framework kernel (lambda-Pi / LF) that type-checks proof terms (propositions-as-types); machine-checked canonical-forms, inversion, and substitution lemmas leading to preservation and progress; and a PLT-Redex-style randomized tester that falsifies soundness for broken rules. Prove (and test) that well-typed programs never get stuck. It is a soundness-proof framework — adjacent to a theorem prover (#280) but specialized to programming-language metatheory.

Project Number: 284

Project Name: Type Soundness Proof Project

Owner: Mujir Qureshi

Document: FINAL_BLUEPRINT.md

Status: specification — locked

1. Goal

Goal. Build, from scratch in Rust, a mechanized-metatheory framework — seven tsp-* crates plus the tsp CLI — that defines object languages (STLC + extensions), represents binding via de Bruijn indices, encodes typing/reduction as inductive relations, and machine-checks type soundness (progress + preservation) through a tiny trusted lambda-Pi proof kernel, cross-validated by a PLT-Redex-style falsifier.

1.1 Precise engineering definition (what is built)

A from-scratch mechanized-metatheory framework for programming-language type soundness. Six capabilities, in dependency order:

# Capability Crate(s) Concrete artifact
1 Define object languages via a metalanguage (sorts, term/type grammars w/ binding, judgment forms, inference rules) tsp-syntax LangDef parsed from .lang; Lexer, Token, Parser, ParseError/ParseErrorKind
2 Represent object terms with binding: de Bruijn indices, shift, capture-avoiding substitution (the computational core of the substitution lemma), structural alpha-equivalence tsp-term Term, Type, shift, subst, alpha_eq, is_value
3 Represent typing `Gamma - e : T+ small-stepe --> e'as inductive relations; executable type checker + stepper +Derivation` checker tsp-rules
4 A small TRUSTED logical-framework proof kernel: a dependently-typed lambda-Pi (LF) core type-checking proof terms, with whnf + definitional equality (propositions-as-types / Curry-Howard) tsp-kernel KTerm, Sort, Ctx, infer_type/check_type, whnf, equal — the TCB
5 State + MACHINE-CHECK the soundness metatheory: canonical forms, inversion, weakening, the substitution lemma, then preservation (subject reduction) + progress tsp-meta progress_statement, preservation_statement, Proof { goal, term }
6 A PLT-Redex-style randomized + exhaustive tester generating well-typed terms, falsifying progress/preservation for a deliberately broken rule (fast counterexample search complementing the proofs) tsp-test Generator, Shrinker, check_progress/check_preservation, TestResult
  • Curry-Howard discipline (load-bearing): a Prop is a KType; a Proof is a KTerm that check_type accepts at that KType. "Proof valid" ≡ infer_type/check_type succeeds using whnf + definitional equal for conversion. Nothing else is trusted.
  • Dual-guard design: every soundness claim is checked twice — once symbolically by the kernel (tsp-meta), once empirically by the falsifier (tsp-test). A sound language → kernel accepts the proof term AND tester yields only Pass. A broken typing rule → proof fails to check AND tester returns a shrunk CounterExample.

1.2 System boundaries

  • In scope: STLC + extensions (naturals, booleans, products, sums, references; System F as a stretch); de Bruijn binding; inductive typing + small-step semantics; an executable type checker/stepper; a lambda-Pi proof-CHECKING kernel; the four core soundness lemmas + the two theorems as kernel-checked proof terms; a Redex-style property tester with shrinking.
  • Trusted computing base = tsp-kernel only. infer_type/check_type/whnf/equal — target ≤ 1500 lines, audited line-by-line. Everything outside the kernel (parser, infer, step, generators, lemma encodings) is untrusted: a bug there is caught by the kernel, never trusted by it.
  • Trust ordering: tsp-syntax/tsp-rules/tsp-test (untrusted, may be buggy) ⟶ produce KTerm proof terms ⟶ tsp-kernel (trusted) is the final arbiter. A false theorem can only escape via a kernel bug — hence the size + audit discipline.

1.3 NON-goals (explicit, with sibling-project boundaries)

NON-goal Why out of scope Where it lives
A general-purpose interactive theorem prover with tactics This kernel is proof-CHECKING only, specialized to PL metatheory — no tactic engine, no goal state, no proof automation sibling #280
Automated proof SEARCH / SMT We check supplied proof terms; we never search for them sibling #278
A full dependently-typed programming language The lambda-Pi core is a minimal LF for encoding judgments, not a surface language —
Extraction / compilation of proofs to executable code No code-generation backend —
Concurrency or effects Beyond a references stretch (with a store + store typing) gated §1.7
A GUI CLI + embeddable crates only —
  • Proof-CHECKING, not proof-search is the defining stance: tsp-kernel answers "is this KTerm a proof of this KType?" — it does not produce proofs. Compare #280 (tactics) and #278 (search/SMT).
  • Object languages are textbook calculi (TAPL / Software Foundations), not a real language. The TCB is deliberately tiny.

1.4 Intended deployment model & users

  • Deployment: (a) embeddable Rust crates — tsp-term/tsp-syntax/tsp-rules/tsp-kernel/tsp-meta/tsp-test as library dependencies; (b) the tsp binary (crate tsp-cli, std) with four subcommands:
tsp check      <lang.lang> <proof>   # run a LangDef + its soundness proof through the kernel
tsp test       <lang.lang>           # randomized soundness (Generator + check_progress/check_preservation)
tsp typecheck  <lang.lang> <term>    # object-level infer:  Gamma |- e : T
tsp step       <lang.lang> <term>    # object-level small-step reduction:  e --> e'
User Use of the framework
PL researchers Mechanize metatheory of a new calculus; audit the TCB; reuse the kernel as a checkable proof backend
Students of TAPL / Software Foundations See progress/preservation, the substitution lemma, canonical forms made executable + machine-checked
Language designers Prototype a LangDef, run tsp test for fast falsification, then tsp check for a real proof before committing a rule
  • no_std + alloc for tsp-syntax, tsp-term, tsp-kernel → embeddable in constrained hosts; only tsp-cli requires std.

1.5 Phase-0 deliverable — the seven tsp-* crates

The Phase-0 (## 12) skeleton stands up the LOCKED seven-member workspace, each as a compiling crate shell with its core type shells + error types:

  1. tsp-syntax — metalanguage front end: parse a LangDef (sort decls, term/type grammars w/ binding annotations, judgment forms, inference rules) + object terms; Lexer, Token, Parser, ParseError/ParseErrorKind. no_std + alloc.
  2. tsp-term — object-term representation + binding: Term (de Bruijn), Type, shift, subst (capture-avoiding), alpha_eq (structural), values / closed-term predicates. no_std + alloc.
  3. tsp-rules — inductive relations: Judgment, Rule (premises + conclusion), Derivation, the Derivation checker check_deriv, the executable type checker infer/check, the small-step stepper step (with evaluation contexts). Depends on tsp-term + tsp-syntax.
  4. tsp-kernel — the TRUSTED logical-framework proof kernel: KTerm (lambda-Pi: Var, Sort, Pi, Lam, App), KType, Ctx, the bidirectional infer_type/check_type, whnf, equal. Proof = a KTerm; Prop = a KType. The TCB. no_std + alloc.
  5. tsp-meta — the metatheory layer: encode object judgments (typing, step) as kernel inductive signatures (propositions-as-types); the lemma library — canonical_forms, inversion, weakening, the substitution lemma — and the Preservation + Progress theorems, all as kernel-checked proof terms. Depends on tsp-rules + tsp-kernel.
  6. tsp-test — a PLT-Redex-style falsifier: a Generator of well-typed object terms, a Shrinker, and the progress/preservation property checks (TestResult). Complements the proofs by fast counterexample search. Depends on tsp-rules.
  7. tsp-cli — the tsp binary: subcommands check, test, typecheck, step. std.
  • Phase-0 smoke test (gate): tsp typecheck derives Lam(Bool, Var 0) : Bool->Bool; tsp step reduces (Lam x. x) true --> true. CI + Makefile + Dockerfile green.

1.6 What later phases unlock

Phase (heading) Unlocks
1 (## 13) de Bruijn Term/Type, shift, capture-avoiding subst, alpha_eq, is_value — the computational core the substitution lemma mirrors
2 (## 14) metalanguage: LangDef parser + object-term parser + ParseError diagnostics
3 (## 15) inductive Rule engine; executable infer/check (STLC); Derivation + check_deriv
4 (## 16) small-step semantics: reduction rules, values, evaluation contexts/congruence, step; (optional) determinism of step
5 (## 17) the proof-kernel core: KTerm/Sort/Ctx, bidirectional infer_type/check_type (TRUSTED), whnf + definitional equal (beta, optionally eta)
6 (## 18) propositions-as-types encoding of object judgments as LF inductive signatures; induction/elimination principles; a Derivation as a kernel proof term
7 (## 19) structural lemmas machine-checked: canonical_forms, inversion, weakening, the substitution lemma (the crux, mirroring tsp-term::subst)
8 (## 20) Preservation (subject reduction): `
9 (## 21) Progress: `
10 (## 22) type soundness corollary (well-typed terms never get stuck); extend object language (naturals, booleans, products, sums); re-establish progress + preservation
11 (## 23) Redex-style harness: Generator, check_progress/check_preservation, Shrinker → minimal CounterExample; cross-validate tester vs proofs on the sound language
12 (## 24) stress extension (references w/ store + store typing OR System F) + the deliberately-BROKEN-rule demonstration caught by BOTH kernel AND tester
13 (## 25) validation: conformance suite, kernel fuzzing (never accepts an ill-typed proof term, never panics), benchmarks vs targets — the "done" gate

1.7 Deferred / stretch features (gated)

Gated behind PhaseDeferred("FINAL_BLUEPRINT section X.Y") — present as type shells from Phase 0, activated only at the cited section:

Feature Gate Touches
System F (Type::All, Type::TyVar; type abstraction/application; parametricity stretch) PhaseDeferred("FINAL_BLUEPRINT section 24") tsp-term, tsp-rules, tsp-meta
References (store + store typing; Type::Ref) PhaseDeferred("FINAL_BLUEPRINT section 24") tsp-rules (step over a store), tsp-meta (store-typing preservation)
Parametricity (relational, over System F) PhaseDeferred("FINAL_BLUEPRINT section 24") tsp-meta (stretch only)
eta in definitional equality PhaseDeferred("FINAL_BLUEPRINT section 17") tsp-kernel::equal
Big-step semantics (alongside small-step) PhaseDeferred("FINAL_BLUEPRINT section 16") tsp-rules

1.8 Estimated timeline

Milestone Estimate
Phase 0 skeleton (7 crates compile + smoke test green) 6–14 weeks
STLC progress + preservation machine-checked (Phases 1–9) several months
Full coverage (extensions + dual-guard broken-rule demo + validation, Phases 10–13) months beyond that

1.9 Recommended language: Rust (edition 2021, MSRV 1.78) + rationale

  • Decisive: the kernel is a small, hot, recursion-heavy type checker where a single soundness bug compromises every proof — memory safety + exhaustive enum matching on Term/Type/Derivation/KTerm shapes is non-negotiable for an auditable TCB.
  • de Bruijn → usize: shift/subst are total functions the borrow checker keeps honest; no aliasing in the term graph.
  • Auditable TCB boundary: a clean crate boundary (tsp-kernel, no_std + alloc, ≤ 1500 lines) makes the trusted base small and reviewable in isolation.
  • Property-based testing idiomatic: the Redex layer (tsp-test) maps cleanly to proptest/quickcheck-style generation + shrinking.
  • Rejected alternatives: an existing proof assistant (defeats the from-scratch learning goal); OCaml/Haskell (viable but weaker systems/tooling story here, and laziness complicates the strict kernel); C/C++ (memory-unsafe term graphs in the TCB are unacceptable).

1.10 Required prerequisites

Reader/implementer should know: lambda calculus (untyped + simply-typed); type systems (STLC, products, sums, references, System F); operational semantics (small-step, values, evaluation contexts, stuck terms); progress + preservation / subject reduction; de Bruijn indices (nameless terms, shift, capture-avoiding substitution); the logical-framework / lambda-Pi (LF) idea (dependent function type Pi, Type:Kind, bidirectional checking, definitional equality / whnf); Curry-Howard / propositions-as-types (implication = Pi, forall = Pi, conjunction = pair, a derivation = a proof term). References cited throughout: Pierce TAPL; Software Foundations; PLT Redex (Felleisen-Findler-Flatt); Harper-Honsell-Plotkin LF + Twelf; Loeh-McBride-Swierstra LambdaPi; de Bruijn 1972; the POPLmark challenge.

2. Why Build This

2.1 Thesis

A type soundness proof is the only artifact that lets a language designer say well-typed programs cannot go wrong and mean it as a theorem, not a hope. This project builds the machinery end-to-end: define a calculus, give it semantics, state progress + preservation, and machine-check the proof against a tiny trusted tsp-kernel. The payload is not STLC (textbook); it is owning every layer between "here is an inference rule" and "a lambda-Pi checker accepts a proof term that this rule is sound" — the layer real verification efforts (CompCert, sel4) stand on and that 99% of engineers treat as a black box.

2.2 Unique systems knowledge taught

# Capability Where built Why it transfers
1 Binding under de Bruijn indices — shift/subst/alpha_eq as total functions tsp-term (Phase 1) every compiler, interpreter, optimizer, SMT backend manipulates binders; capture bugs are silent + catastrophic
2 Capture-avoiding substitution as the load-bearing primitive tsp-term::subst, mirrored by Phase 7 substitution lemma off-by-one in shift cutoff ⇒ lemma is false but looks proved; teaches index discipline the borrow checker can't catch
3 Inductive relations as data — Judgment/Rule/Derivation + check_deriv tsp-rules (Phase 3) "rules of inference" become a checkable tree, not prose; foundation of every type system + operational semantics
4 Executable vs. relational semantics, reconciled — infer/step agree with the inductive Rules tsp-rules (Phases 3–4) the gap between "the checker I ran" and "the spec I proved" is where most soundness bugs hide
5 A trusted proof kernel — bidirectional infer_type/check_type over lambda-Pi tsp-kernel (Phase 5) propositions-as-types made concrete: a Prop is a KType, a Proof is a KTerm; you build the thing you usually trust blindly
6 whnf + definitional equal (conversion / beta, opt. eta) tsp-kernel (Phase 5) dependent type checking is normalization-driven equality; the algorithm behind Coq/Agda/Lean conversion
7 Curry-Howard operationally — a Derivation ⇒ a kernel proof term tsp-meta (Phase 6) logic = types; implication = Pi/Arrow, ∀ = Pi; demystifies "propositions as types"
8 The anatomy of progress + preservation — induction on derivations, canonical_forms, inversion, weakening, substitution lemma tsp-meta (Phases 7–9) the canonical safety argument; once internalized, every type-system extension is a known recipe
9 Property-based falsification vs. proof — Generator/Shrinker/TestResult tsp-test (Phase 11) proofs say why it's safe; PLT-Redex-style testing says here's the 5-node counterexample in milliseconds. Two different epistemics, both required
10 TCB minimization as an architecture goal — small, audited tsp-kernel checks everything else whole workspace the gravest failure is a kernel that accepts an ill-typed proof term (silently validates a false theorem); teaches what to trust and why

2.3 The dual-guard insight (the project's spine)

Two independent oracles judge soundness, and a real bug must trip both:

              broken typing rule  --------+
                                          |
   tsp-kernel  --> proof term fails check |   <-- "WHY it's unsound" (foundational)
   tsp-test    --> shrunk CounterExample  |   <-- "an EXAMPLE of unsoundness" (empirical, fast)
                                          |
        agreement on the SOUND language; divergence flags a meta-bug
  • A correct language ⇒ tsp-test yields only TestResult::Pass(n) and tsp-kernel accepts the proof.
  • Phase 12's deliberately broken rule must be caught by both: the proof no longer type-checks and a counterexample is found + shrunk. If only one fires, the harness itself is suspect.
  • Engineering lesson: verification and validation are complementary, not redundant — a passing test suite never implies a theorem, and a theorem never localizes a regression to a minimal input.

2.4 Historical + architectural context (cite consistently)

Reference What it establishes What we borrow
POPLmark challenge mechanizing metatheory (esp. binding + System F) is hard; binders are the bottleneck de Bruijn Term, structural alpha_eq, the substitution lemma as the crux (Phase 7)
Pierce, TAPL STLC, progress/preservation, references, System F — the canonical proofs the exact theorems Phases 8–10 + 12 state and check
Pierce et al., Software Foundations progress/preservation can be fully machine-checked, pedagogically the from-scratch, machine-checked goal — but with our own lambda-Pi kernel, not Coq
Harper-Honsell-Plotkin (LF) + Twelf the logical framework: judgments-as-types, rules as constants tsp-kernel's lambda-Pi core + Phase 6's LF-style inductive signatures
Loeh-McBride-Swierstra (LambdaPi) a minimal dependently-typed checker is ~a few hundred lines the bidirectional infer_type/check_type + whnf/equal design
Felleisen-Findler-Flatt, PLT Redex randomized + exhaustive semantics testing finds bugs proofs miss tsp-test's Generator + Shrinker (Phase 11)
CompCert / foundational verification a small trusted base checking a large development is the only scalable trust story TCB-minimization: everything reduces to trusting tsp-kernel
de Bruijn 1972 nameless terms eliminate alpha-renaming bookkeeping the entire tsp-term representation

Why a tiny TCB matters (the load-bearing claim): soundness of the whole development reduces to soundness of infer_type/check_type. A 200–600-line audited kernel is reviewable by one person in an afternoon; a 50k-line one is not. This is the CompCert/LF discipline distilled — shrink what you must trust until trust is achievable.

2.5 Engineering muscles built

  • Recursion-heavy total functions on enum trees with exhaustive match — no unreachable!() in the TCB.
  • Index arithmetic correctness — shift(d, cutoff) / subst(j, s) invariants; the place soundness is won or lost.
  • Bidirectional type checking — when to infer (synthesize) vs. check (against an expected type); annotation discipline.
  • Normalization + conversion — whnf strategy, beta/eta in equal, termination of the checker on well-typed input.
  • Spec-vs-implementation fidelity — keeping executable infer/step (tsp-rules) in lockstep with the inductive Rules the proofs reason about.
  • Adversarial input handling — Phase 13 fuzzing: the kernel must never accept an ill-typed KTerm and never panic on malformed/adversarial proof terms.
  • Property-based test design — size-bounded well-typed generation, shrinking to a minimal CounterExample, classifying which property failed via Witness.
  • API/TCB boundary design — a clean module seam isolating the trusted tsp-kernel from the untrusted-but-checked tsp-meta/tsp-rules/tsp-test.

2.6 Cross-links to portfolio (per spine)

Project Relationship Boundary
#280 interactive theorem prover sibling on the proof axis #280 is a general prover with tactics + proof search; 284 is proof-term checking only, specialized to PL metatheory. 284 is #280's trusted core, scoped down
#278 SMT / automated proof search complementary 284 checks hand/structured proofs; #278 searches for them. Explicit NON-GOAL here
Compiler / parser projects upstream consumers tsp-syntax's LangDef + object-term parser is a microcosm of a real front end; binding discipline is shared
Type-checker projects direct kin tsp-rules::infer is a textbook type checker; 284 additionally proves it sound

Reuse: de Bruijn + subst/shift (tsp-term) and bidirectional checking (tsp-kernel) are the exact primitives those projects need — 284 builds the verified version.

2.7 Calibration value

  • Smallest TCB in the portfolio (tsp-kernel target: 200–600 LOC) — the cleanest possible demonstration of what to trust and why.
  • The bug that proves the design: Phase 12's broken rule is a falsifiable, reproducible artifact — a reviewer can watch both guards fire. Few specs ship their own counterexample.
  • Theorem-grade rigor on a textbook object language keeps the metatheory in focus (the transferable skill) without drowning in a real language's surface syntax — maximal signal per line of trusted code.
  • Definition of done is binary (Phase 13 gate): the soundness proofs check, known-broken variants are rejected, kernel fuzzing finds no unsound acceptance + no panic, and benchmarks (kernel check time, test throughput) meet targets. No partial credit — either it's machine-checked or it isn't.

What Mastery Looks Like

A practitioner has mastered this project when they can demonstrate all of the following competencies, each anchored to a named phase:

  • de Bruijn index discipline (Phase 1 — tsp-term): Given a lambda term with nested binders, correctly trace how shift(t, +1, cutoff) and subst(t, j, s) transform every free-variable index by hand, predict whether a proposed cutoff value is off-by-one, and explain why an off-by-one breaks the substitution lemma rather than just the computed term.
  • Inductive-relation literacy (Phase 3 — tsp-rules): Read a set of inference Rules and construct a valid Derivation tree for a given judgment; identify which Rule is violated in a malformed tree that check_deriv rejects; and articulate why the executable infer is not trusted but the Derivation checker is.
  • Proof-kernel design (Phase 5 — tsp-kernel): Explain bidirectional type-checking (infer_type/check_type), trace why equal must call whnf rather than compare KTerms structurally, and audit the ≤800-LoC TCB to confirm that K-Conv is the only rule that could silently accept a false proposition.
  • Propositions-as-types encoding (Phase 6 — tsp-meta): Encode ∀ e T. (⊢ e : T) → (value(e) ∨ ∃ e'. e → e') as a KTerm Pi-type, map each logical connective to its LF constructor, and submit the term to check_type — interpreting KernelError::Mismatch to locate the mis-encoded connective.
  • Substitution-lemma crux (Phase 7 — tsp-meta): Reconstruct why the substitution lemma's proof term mirrors tsp-term::subst case-for-case (P1, ADR-12), identify which proof case uses weakening, and explain how an accepted proof term for the wrong statement (e.g., a shift off by one in the encoding) would not be caught by check_type alone — requiring the Phase 11 cross-validation to surface it.
  • Dual-guard contract (Phase 12 + 13 — tsp-meta + tsp-test): Introduce a deliberately broken typing rule, predict which step case it mis-handles, drive tsp-test's Generator/Shrinker to a minimal CounterExample, and simultaneously show the preservation_statement proof fails check_type — explaining why a framework that catches the bug with only one guard is untrustworthy.
  • TCB minimization as an architecture goal (whole workspace): Given a proposed "convenience" addition to tsp-kernel (e.g., an inversion helper), evaluate whether it enlarges the trusted base, whether it can instead live in tsp-meta with the kernel checking its output, and how the cargo-deny + line-count CI gate enforces the boundary automatically.
  • Performance-correctness ordering (Phase 13 — benchmarks): Explain why the whnf memo optimization (§31.5) is gated behind differential testing rather than shipped unconditionally, identify the CPU cycle breakdown (~55% whnf, ~25% equal), and interpret a criterion regression report to determine whether a kernel change is safe to merge.

3. Architectural Overview

A seven-crate Rust workspace forming a layered pipeline from an untrusted front end (parse a language definition + object terms) down through two parallel assurance paths — tsp-meta machine-checked proofs validated by the trusted tsp-kernel (lambda-Pi / LF), and tsp-test empirical falsification. Data, not callbacks, flows between layers: judgments and derivations are values; proofs are KTerms; the kernel is the single arbiter of truth.

3.1 Canonical Architecture Diagram

                    tsp-cli   (tsp: check / test / typecheck / step)
                        |  language definition (.lang) + proof script        |  object program
                        v                                                    v
  UNTRUSTED  =====> tsp-syntax (parse LangDef + object terms)  --------->  tsp-term (de Bruijn Term/Type; shift;
                        |  LangDef + Terms                                    capture-avoiding subst; alpha_eq; is_value)
                        v                                                          |
                    tsp-rules (typing Gamma|-e:T + small-step e-->e' as inductive Rules; executable infer + step;
                        Derivation + check_deriv)                                  |
                        |  judgments as data                                       |
                        +-------------------------------+--------------------------+
                        v (machine-checked proofs)      v (empirical falsification)
                    tsp-meta (Progress, Preservation,   tsp-test (Redex-style: Generator of well-typed terms;
                      canonical-forms / inversion /       check_progress + check_preservation; Shrinker -> CounterExample)
                      weakening / substitution lemmas
                      as propositions-as-types)
                        |  Proof { term : goal }                              <----- empirical, NOT a proof
                        v
  =================  tsp-kernel (lambda-Pi / LF core: infer_type / check_type type-checks proof terms; whnf +  ===
  TRUST BOUNDARY      definitional equality 'equal'; the TRUSTED computing base — small + audited)              TCB
  =================                                                                                            ===

3.2 Data Flows (LOCKED)

Edge Producer -> Consumer Payload Form
1 tsp-cli -> tsp-syntax .lang file + proof script + object program byte stream / &str
2 tsp-syntax -> tsp-term parsed object terms Term, Type (de Bruijn)
3 tsp-syntax -> tsp-rules LangDef { sorts, grammar, judgments, rules } data
4 tsp-term -> tsp-rules term reps + shift/subst/alpha_eq/is_value function calls
5 tsp-rules -> tsp-meta Judgment, Rule, Derivation (judgments-as-data) data
6 tsp-rules -> tsp-test infer, step, Rule set function calls + data
7 tsp-meta -> tsp-kernel Proof { goal: KTerm, term: KTerm } proof terms
8 tsp-test (self) Generator -> well-typed Term -> check_progress/check_preservation -> Shrinker TestResult
  • Proof obligation: tsp-meta emits Proof { term, goal }; "valid" iff check_type(ctx, term, goal) accepts (edge 7) — the ONLY definition of proof validity.
  • Empirical edge 8 is NOT a proof: a Pass(u64) is evidence, a CounterExample(Term, Witness) is a falsification; neither is consumed by the kernel.

3.3 Trust Boundary (TCB)

Region Crates Status If buggy
Trusted computing base tsp-kernel ONLY audited; target <= ~1500 LOC GRAVEST: accepts ill-typed proof term -> false theorem silently validated
Checked-by-the-kernel tsp-meta untrusted; every Proof re-checked by kernel proof simply fails check_type -> caught
Untrusted input LangDef, proof scripts, object programs adversarial bad rule -> proof fails to check AND/OR tester finds counterexample
Supporting (not in TCB) tsp-syntax, tsp-term, tsp-rules, tsp-test, tsp-cli untrusted; bugs surface as failed kernel checks or test disagreement none can validate a false theorem; only the kernel can
  • Invariant TCB-1 (load-bearing): infer_type/check_type NEVER accept an ill-typed KTerm and NEVER panic on adversarial input. Fuzzed in Phase 13.
  • Trust is asymmetric: a tsp-meta lemma is believed only because the kernel re-derives it from first principles; the kernel trusts nothing below it.
  • Dual-guard: a deliberately broken typing rule (Phase 12) must be rejected by BOTH the kernel (proof no longer checks) AND tsp-test (shrunk counterexample) — independent failure detectors.

3.4 Persistence Boundary (NONE)

  • No database, no on-disk state, no caches, no network. Pure batch transform: (.lang + proof + program) -> {Ok / KernelError / TypeError / TestResult}. Files are read-only inputs; the only outputs are exit codes + diagnostics on stdout/stderr. Determinism: same inputs (and same RNG seed for tsp-test) -> same verdict; reproducibility is a conformance requirement (Phase 13).

3.5 Component Responsibilities (one box = one crate)

tsp-syntax — The untrusted metalanguage front end. In: &str of a .lang file (sort declarations, term/type grammars with binding annotations, judgment forms, inference rules) and object-term text. Out: LangDef { sorts, grammar, judgments, rules } + Term/Type values, or ParseError { ParseErrorKind: UnexpectedToken | UnknownSort | BadBinder | MalformedRule }. Components: Lexer, Token, Parser. no_std + alloc. Invariant: every emitted Term is well-scoped (de Bruijn indices in range for declared binders) and every Rule references only declared sorts/judgments — a parser bug can only yield a malformed program/proof that a downstream check rejects, never a false theorem. Built in: Phase 0 (shells), Phase 2 (full LangDef + object-term parser + diagnostics).

tsp-term — Object-term representation + binding; the computational core the substitution lemma mirrors. In: AST from tsp-syntax. Out: Type (Base | Arrow | Prod | Sum | Ref | All | TyVar), Term (Var(usize) | Lam | App | TmTrue | TmFalse | If | Zero | Succ | ...), and the total functions shift(t,d,cutoff), subst(t,j,s) (capture-avoiding [s/j]t), alpha_eq(a,b) (structural), is_value(t). no_std + alloc. Invariant SUBST-1 (crux-adjacent): shift/subst implement the exact de Bruijn discipline the machine-checked substitution lemma encodes — capture-avoidance is automatic from correct cutoff arithmetic; an off-by-one in shift breaks the substitution lemma proof in tsp-meta. alpha_eq is plain structural equality because de Bruijn nameless terms quotient alpha-equivalence by construction. Built in: Phase 0 (Term/Type shells), Phase 1 (shift/subst/alpha_eq/is_value).

tsp-rules — Inductive relations + executable judgments. In: LangDef (rules) + Term/Type (+ tsp-term substitution functions). Out: Judgment { form, args }, Rule { name, premises, conclusion }, Derivation { rule, subderivations, conclusion }; check_deriv(d, rules) -> Result<(), DerivError> (is the derivation tree a valid instance of the rules?); infer(ctx, e) -> Result<Type, TypeError> / check (executable type checker, STLC then extensions); step(e) -> Option<Term> (small-step; None = value or stuck). Invariant RULES-1: check_deriv accepts a Derivation iff each node matches some Rule whose premises are recursively valid — it is the executable, data-level analogue of the kernel's proof-term check, and infer agrees with the typing relation it encodes. Invariant RULES-2: step realizes the small-step relation e --> e' via evaluation contexts / congruence; (optional) determinism. Built in: Phase 0 (Judgment/Rule/Derivation shells), Phase 3 (infer/check + check_deriv), Phase 4 (step + evaluation contexts).

tsp-kernel — THE trusted base: a lambda-Pi (LF) proof-term checker, deliberately tiny + audited. In: a KTerm and (for check_type) an expected KType in a Ctx. Out: infer_type(ctx,t) -> Result<KTerm, KernelError>, check_type(ctx,t,ty) -> Result<(), KernelError>, whnf(t) -> KTerm, equal(ctx,a,b) -> bool. KTerm = Var | Sort(Sort) | Pi | Lam | App; Sort = Type | Kind with Type : Kind. no_std + alloc. Curry-Howard: a Prop is a KType, a Proof is a KTerm that check_type accepts at that KType; conversion uses whnf + definitional equal (beta, optionally eta). Invariant KERNEL-1 (the project's foundation): infer_type/check_type are sound — they accept a KTerm at a type only if that typing genuinely holds in lambda-Pi; soundness of the entire metatheory reduces to this one checker being correct. Invariant KERNEL-2: total + panic-free on all KTerm inputs (no overflow, no unbounded recursion that escapes a depth guard) — fuzzed adversarially in Phase 13. Smallness is a design constraint, not an accident: nothing above it is trusted, so the audit surface must stay minimal. Built in: Phase 0 (KTerm/KType/Ctx shells), Phase 5 (bidirectional infer_type/check_type + whnf + equal).

tsp-meta — The metatheory layer; untrusted, every output re-checked by the kernel. In: Rule/Judgment/Derivation from tsp-rules + the kernel API. Out: object judgments encoded as kernel inductive signatures (propositions-as-types, LF-style) with their induction/elimination principles; the lemma library canonical_forms, inversion, weakening, and the substitution lemma; progress_statement(lang) -> KTerm and preservation_statement(lang) -> KTerm; Proof { goal: KTerm, term: KTerm } for each. Invariant META-1: every theorem ships as a Proof whose term check_types at goal — tsp-meta asserts nothing the kernel has not re-validated. Invariant META-2 (crux): the substitution lemma (Gamma,x:S |- e:T and Gamma |- v:S imply Gamma |- [v/x]e:T) is proved by mirroring tsp-term::subst/shift exactly; it is reused by Preservation at the beta/elimination step, while Progress consumes canonical_forms. Built in: Phase 6 (propositions-as-types encoding + derivation-as-proof-term), Phase 7 (structural lemmas + substitution), Phase 8 (Preservation), Phase 9 (Progress), Phase 10 (type-soundness corollary + re-establish on the extended language), Phase 12 (extend to references/System F + broken-rule: proof fails to check).

tsp-test — A PLT-Redex-style falsifier; empirical, never a proof. In: infer/step + the Rule set from tsp-rules. Out: Generator (size-bounded well-typed Term generation), Shrinker, check_progress(e) -> bool / check_preservation(e) -> bool, and TestResult = Pass(u64) | CounterExample(Term, Witness) (Witness = which property failed + how). Invariant TEST-1: the Generator emits only well-typed closed terms (so a failure is a genuine soundness violation, not a generation bug), and check_progress/check_preservation directly test the locked theorem statements. Invariant TEST-2 (dual-guard agreement): on a sound language tsp-test yields only Pass; on a deliberately broken rule it must produce a CounterExample shrunk to a minimal Term, agreeing with the kernel's rejection. Built in: Phase 11 (Generator/Shrinker + property checks + cross-validation against the proofs), Phase 12 (catch the broken rule), Phase 13 (throughput targets + tester-vs-proof agreement gate).

tsp-cli — The tsp binary; orchestrates, holds no metatheory logic. In: command-line args + files. Out: subcommands check (run a LangDef + its soundness Proof through the kernel), test (randomized soundness via tsp-test), typecheck (object infer), step (object step); exit code + diagnostics. std (only crate using std: argv, file IO, stderr). Invariant CLI-1: a non-zero exit on any KernelError/TypeError/DerivError/CounterExample; check succeeds only when the kernel accepts every Proof. Invariant CLI-2: deterministic for fixed inputs + RNG seed (no hidden state, per 3.4). Built in: Phase 0 (skeleton + smoke test: typecheck of Lam(Bool, Var 0) : Bool->Bool; step of (Lam x. x) true --> true), wired progressively as each lower crate lands.

4. System Constraints

Hard, quantified bounds the build is measured against. Soundness of tsp-kernel is non-negotiable (a soundness bug invalidates every proof); the object-language scope is bounded (STLC + naturals/booleans/products/sums/refs; System F a stretch). Single-process, single-threaded; no network, no persistent state. Targets below are the gate, not aspirations — Phase 13 (## 25) benchmarks against this table.

4.1 Constraint table (LOCKED targets)

# Constraint Target / Budget Bound type Verified by Failure if breached
C1 TCB size — tsp-kernel audited LOC (KTerm,infer_type,check_type,whnf,equal,Ctx) < 1500 LOC (excl. tests/comments) HARD design tokei on tsp-kernel/src, CI gate TCB un-auditable → soundness un-reviewable
C2 Kernel soundness: never ACCEPT ill-typed proof term 0 false accepts ABSOLUTE Phase 13 fuzz + conformance silent false theorem (gravest class)
C3 Kernel check time, STLC soundness proof term (Preservation+Progress) p50 < 100 ms, p95 < 1 s, max < 5 s HARD perf tsp check bench, criterion timeout / unusable proof loop
C4 Single proof-term size (Proof.term, KTerm node count) typ. < 10^4 nodes; cap 2·10^6 nodes HARD node counter in infer_type entry OOM / pathological recursion
C5 Derivation tree size (Derivation, check_deriv) depth < 256; nodes < 10^5 HARD check_deriv precheck stack overflow on adversarial tree
C6 Object-term size (Term) — parse + infer + step nodes < 10^4; binder depth (de Bruijn) < 1024 HARD parser counter + infer guard shift/subst blowup, index overflow
C7 Tester throughput (Generator + check_progress + check_preservation) >= 10^4 terms/s (min), >= 10^5 terms/s (STLC, size<=20) SOFT perf tsp test bench falsification too slow to be useful
C8 Shrink budget per counterexample (Shrinker) <= 2000 reduction steps OR <= 100 ms wall HARD Shrinker step counter shrink hangs, no minimal CounterExample
C9 Resident memory ceiling, all subcommands < 512 MiB (kernel check), < 256 MiB (test run) HARD RSS sampler in CI OOM-kill, non-portable
C10 Determinism — proof same Proof -> identical accept/reject ABSOLUTE replay test, 2 runs byte-identical non-reproducible verdict
C11 Determinism — tester same seed -> identical run (terms, verdict, counterexample) ABSOLUTE seeded replay test non-reproducible falsification
C12 Startup — global init zero global mutable init; < 5 ms to first verdict dispatch HARD no static mut, no lazy_static in TCB hidden state -> non-determinism
C13 Recovery — adversarial KTerm infer_type/check_type never panic; return KernelError ABSOLUTE fuzz (cargo-fuzz), 0 panics crash = DoS / undefined verdict
C14 whnf / equal termination on well-typed input terminates; step cap 2^20 before KernelError::Diverged HARD reduction counter nontermination hangs the checker
C15 Total tsp binary size (release, tsp-cli) < 8 MiB stripped SOFT CI artifact size bloat, slow cold start
C16 Cold tsp check end-to-end (parse .lang -> kernel verdict) wall < 2 s (STLC), < 10 s (extended/refs) SOFT e2e timer regression vs C3 budget

4.2 The TCB constraint (C1, C2) — load-bearing

  • Trusted base = tsp-kernel only. tsp-syntax/tsp-term/tsp-rules/tsp-meta/tsp-test/tsp-cli are untrusted: a bug there yields a wrong proposition or a rejected proof, never a falsely accepted one — the kernel re-checks every Proof.term at its goal KType.
  • < 1500 LOC (C1) is a design constraint, not a measurement after the fact: every kernel addition is budgeted. whnf+equal (conversion) are the largest sub-budget (~400 LOC); infer_type/check_type (bidirectional) ~600 LOC; KTerm/Ctx/Sort + de Bruijn ops ~300 LOC; errors ~100 LOC.
  • C2 dominates all perf targets: correctness before speed. If C2 conflicts with C3/C7, C2 wins (slower, sound).
  • TCB excludes: parsing, generation, shrinking, CLI, the statements progress_statement/preservation_statement (untrusted producers — the kernel still type-checks their output KTerm).

4.3 Determinism & purity (C10–C12)

  • infer_type, check_type, whnf, equal, shift, subst, alpha_eq, infer, step, check_deriv are pure total functions of their inputs — no clock, no RNG, no I/O, no global state. -> C10.
  • de Bruijn representation removes name choice -> alpha_eq is structural -> identical verdict regardless of source naming. Off-by-one in shift breaks the substitution lemma (C2 risk), not determinism.
  • Tester randomness is confined to Generator/Shrinker, seeded explicitly (--seed); seed in -> run out is a pure function. -> C11. Default seed is fixed + logged.
  • No lazy statics in the TCB (C12) — laziness would introduce first-touch order dependence; the strict kernel forbids it (one rationale for Rust over Haskell).

4.4 Resource & size discipline (C4–C9, C14, C15)

  • All sizes counted on KTerm/Term/Derivation node counts (not bytes) — the relevant cost driver for recursive checking.
  • Recursion is bounded, not unbounded: check_deriv and infer_type carry an explicit depth budget (C5/C4) and convert overflow to KernelError/DerivError rather than blowing the native stack (C13). Large terms may use an explicit work-stack to keep RSS under C9.
  • whnf/equal carry a reduction-step cap (C14): well-typed object-language proofs normalize fast; the cap guards adversarial KTerm fed via fuzzing (C13) — divergence -> KernelError::Diverged, never a hang or a false accept.
  • usize de Bruijn indices: binder depth bound (C6, < 1024) keeps shift/subst arithmetic far from usize overflow; an index >= context length is a KernelError/TypeError, never UB.

4.5 Operating envelope & assumptions

Assumption Value Note
Concurrency single process, single thread no shared state; refs stretch adds a store (data), not OS threads
Persistence none inputs = .lang + proof script + object program; verdict to stdout
Platform Linux/macOS x86-64 + aarch64; Rust 2021, MSRV 1.78 no_std + alloc for tsp-syntax/tsp-term/tsp-kernel; std only in tsp-cli
Input trust inputs are untrusted/adversarial kernel must withstand hostile KTerm (C13); parser must reject (ParseError) not crash
Numeric Zero/Succ unary naturals are object terms, not host ints size counts toward C6 (deep Succ chains bounded)
Object scope STLC + nat/bool/prod/sum/ref; System F = stretch bounded by design — not a real language; expanding scope must preserve C1–C3
Network / FS / time none in TCB reproducibility (C10/C11) + tiny audit surface

4.6 Non-negotiable vs. negotiable

  • Non-negotiable (ABSOLUTE): C2 (no false accept), C10/C11 (determinism), C13 (no kernel panic). These define correctness; failing any one fails the project.
  • Hard budgets: C1, C3–C6, C8, C9, C12, C14 — CI gates; a breach blocks the Phase 13 "done" gate until fixed or formally re-budgeted.
  • Soft targets: C7, C15, C16 — performance/ergonomics; regressions tracked, tolerated short-term, not release-blocking.
  • Trade-off rule of record: when budgets collide, ordering is soundness (C2) > determinism (C10/C11) > safety/no-panic (C13) > hard perf (C3) > throughput (C7) > size (C15).

Mandatory before/during build. Each entry: what to extract, where it lands in the tsp-* workspace, what to ignore. Read order: Books §5.1 (TAPL ch.8–13 → tsp-term/tsp-rules/tsp-meta) → Papers §5.2 (LambdaPi + LF → tsp-kernel) → Specs §5.4 (normative rules) → Refs §5.3 (study, do NOT copy).

5.4 Specifications (normative)

These are the ground truth the implementation and proofs must conform to. Reproduce them in §11 Domain Model / the relevant phases; deviations are bugs.

S1 — STLC statics + dynamics (normative; TAPL ch.9, B1). Typing Γ ⊢ e : T, call-by-value small-step e ⟶ e'. tsp-rules MUST implement exactly these; tsp-meta proves soundness of these.

Types     T ::= Bool | Nat | T -> T | T x T | T + T | Ref T        (* + All.T, TyVar n for System F *)
Terms     e ::= x | \:T. e | e e | true | false | if e e e | 0 | succ e | ...   (de Bruijn: x = Var n)
Values    v ::= \:T. e | true | false | nv          nv ::= 0 | succ nv

Typing  Γ ⊢ e : T
  ───────────────── (T-Var)        Γ,x:T1 ⊢ e:T2            Γ ⊢ e1:T11->T12  Γ ⊢ e2:T11
  Γ ⊢ x : Γ(x)                ──────────────────── (T-Abs)  ───────────────────────────── (T-App)
                              Γ ⊢ \:T1. e : T1->T2          Γ ⊢ e1 e2 : T12

  ─────────────── (T-True)   ──────────────── (T-False)    Γ⊢e1:Bool  Γ⊢e2:T  Γ⊢e3:T
  Γ ⊢ true : Bool            Γ ⊢ false : Bool              ───────────────────────────── (T-If)
                                                            Γ ⊢ if e1 e2 e3 : T
Small-step  e ⟶ e'   (evaluation contexts E ::= [] | E e | v E | if E e e | succ E | ...)
  (\:T. e) v ⟶ [v/0] e   (E-AppAbs/beta)     if true e2 e3 ⟶ e2   if false e2 e3 ⟶ e3
                                E[e] ⟶ E[e']   when e ⟶ e'   (E-Ctx / congruence)

S2 — System F extension (stretch; TAPL ch.23, B1). Add to S1:

T ::= ... | All. T | TyVar n            e ::= ... | /\. e | e [T]     (de Bruijn type vars)
        Γ,*  ⊢ e : T2                   Γ ⊢ e : All. T12
  ───────────────────── (T-TAbs)        ───────────────────────── (T-TApp)
  Γ ⊢ /\. e : All. T12                  Γ ⊢ e [T2] : [T2/0] T12       (/\. e) [T] ⟶ [T/0] e

S3 — References extension (stretch; TAPL ch.13, B1). Store μ, store typing Σ; judgments thread Σ. Locations l; Σ ⊢ μ well-typed store. Preservation gains store-typing growth (Σ' ⊇ Σ).

T ::= ... | Ref T          e ::= ... | ref e | !e | e := e | l        v ::= ... | l
  Γ|Σ ⊢ e:T            Γ|Σ ⊢ e:Ref T        Γ|Σ ⊢ e1:Ref T  Γ|Σ ⊢ e2:T     Σ(l)=T
  ───────────────────  ──────────────       ──────────────────────────────  ─────────────── (T-Loc)
  Γ|Σ ⊢ ref e:Ref T    Γ|Σ ⊢ !e : T         Γ|Σ ⊢ e1:=e2 : Unit             Γ|Σ ⊢ l : Ref T

S4 — LF / lambda-Pi typing rules (the tsp-kernel SPEC; LF P2, LambdaPi P3). This is the trusted core's normative definition; infer_type/check_type/whnf/equal must realize exactly these. Type : Kind. Conversion uses equal (beta, optionally eta).

Sorts s ::= Type | Kind                  KTerm t ::= Var n | Sort s | Pi A B | Lam A b | App t u
Contexts  Γ ::= · | Γ, A                 (de Bruijn list of KTerm types)

  Γ(n) = A                  ──────────────── (K-Sort)        Γ ⊢ A : Type    Γ,A ⊢ B : s
  ──────────── (K-Var)      Γ ⊢ Type : Kind                  ─────────────────────────── (K-Pi)
  Γ ⊢ n : A                                                  Γ ⊢ Pi A B : s

  Γ ⊢ A : Type    Γ,A ⊢ b : B            Γ ⊢ t : Pi A B    Γ ⊢ u : A
  ─────────────────────────── (K-Lam)    ────────────────────────────── (K-App)
  Γ ⊢ Lam A b : Pi A B                   Γ ⊢ App t u : [u/0] B

  Γ ⊢ t : A    Γ ⊢ B : s    equal(Γ, A, B)        (* conversion: well-typedness up to definitional equality *)
  ─────────────────────────────────────────── (K-Conv)
  Γ ⊢ t : B

Conversion/normalization obligations (per P6/P8): whnf reduces head beta-redexes to weak-head normal form; equal(Γ,a,b) decides definitional equality (beta; eta optional) — MUST be sound (never equate distinct types) since (K-Conv) is the load-bearing rule whose unsoundness silently validates false theorems.

S5 — Soundness statements (normative targets; W&F P4, B2). tsp-meta must produce KTerm Props matching these, accepted by tsp-kernel:

Progress      ⊢ e : T  ⟹  is_value(e)  ∨  ∃ e'. e ⟶ e'            (closed e)
Preservation  ⊢ e : T  ∧  e ⟶ e'  ⟹  ⊢ e' : T
Soundness     ⊢ e : T  ⟹  e never reaches a stuck non-value      (corollary, Phase 10)
Subst lemma   Γ,x:S ⊢ e:T  ∧  Γ ⊢ v:S  ⟹  Γ ⊢ [v/x]e : T          (crux; mirrors tsp-term::subst)

Conformance: every spec rule above has a corresponding Rule in tsp-rules and a checked clause in tsp-meta; the Phase 12 deliberately broken rule is a single edit to S1/S2/S3 that BOTH the kernel (proof fails) and tsp-test (shrunk CounterExample) must catch.

6. Design Principles

Non-negotiable rules derived from the spine's design tensions and the KEY ALGORITHMS/FACTS. Each is referenced by name in later phases (## 12–## 25). Violations are stated as concrete failure modes, not abstractions. Ordering ≈ load-bearing-first; DP-1 is supreme — when principles conflict, the smaller/auditable TCB wins.

ID Principle One-line invariant
DP-1 The kernel is the TCB — keep it tiny only tsp-kernel is trusted; ≤ 800 LoC, audited
DP-2 Proofs are terms, checked not searched tsp-meta emits Proof.term; kernel only check_type
DP-3 Propositions-as-types (Curry-Howard) Prop = KType, Proof = KTerm; no separate logic engine
DP-4 de Bruijn or capture bugs binding via indices; shift/subst total; alpha_eq structural
DP-5 The substitution lemma mirrors subst exactly Phase-7 proof shape ≡ tsp-term::subst shape
DP-6 Bidirectional, definitional equality infer/check split; conversion via whnf+equal only
DP-7 Prove AND test — the dual guard every soundness claim hits kernel + tsp-test
DP-8 A broken rule must fail both guards broken rule ⇒ proof rejected ∧ shrunk CounterExample
DP-9 Determinism: same proof → same verdict check_type, step, Generator(seed) reproducible
DP-10 Errors, never panic — never accept an ill-typed proof total Result; default = reject
DP-11 Bounded object-language scope, unbounded rigor textbook calculi; zero proof gaps
DP-12 One source of truth for each fact rules in Rule data; infer/step derived, not duplicated
DP-13 Closed-world inversion via exhaustive match every Term/KTerm/Type arm handled; no wildcard escape
DP-14 Layered trust, acyclic deps crate graph DAG; trust flows toward tsp-kernel
DP-15 Conversion terminates or check fails closed whnf fuel-bounded; exhaustion = KernelError, never loop

6.1 DP-1 — The kernel is the TCB — keep it tiny

  • Statement. tsp-kernel (KTerm, Sort, Ctx, infer_type, check_type, whnf, equal) is the entire trusted computing base. Soundness of the whole project reduces to: "the kernel never accepts a KTerm at a KType it does not inhabit." Everything in tsp-syntax/tsp-rules/tsp-meta/tsp-test is untrusted — its output is re-checked by the kernel.
  • Target. TCB ≤ 800 LoC (tsp-kernel excl. tests/comments); kernel public surface = the 4 functions above only. No I/O, no unsafe, no allocation beyond alloc boxing in the TCB path.
  • Tradeoff. Tiny kernel ⇒ more encoding burden pushed up into tsp-meta (proof terms get larger; see DP-3). Accepted: bytes of proof term are cheap, lines of trusted code are catastrophic.
  • Operational implication. Phase-13 (## 25) fuzzes only the kernel; CI gate fails if tsp-kernel LoC exceeds the budget or any new pub fn appears. Code review for kernel changes is mandatory and line-by-line.
  • Violation → break. Add a "convenience" inductive-elimination helper inside tsp-kernel with an unchecked index arithmetic bug → it silently accepts a false progress_statement proof → every downstream theorem is vacuously "proved." The GRAVEST failure class (spine): an unsound TCB validates a false theorem with no symptom.

6.2 DP-2 — Proofs are terms, checked not searched

  • Statement. tsp-meta constructs explicit Proof { goal: KTerm, term: KTerm }; the kernel runs check_type(ctx, &p.term, &p.goal). There is no tactic engine, no proof search, no SMT (non-goals: #280 tactics, #278 search). Verdict ∈ {Ok(()), Err(KernelError)}.
  • Tradeoff. Proof terms are written/generated by hand or by deterministic builders (more authoring effort) instead of discovered by search (less effort, larger TCB, nondeterminism). Accepted per DP-1 and DP-9.
  • Operational implication. Phases 7–10 (## 19–## 22) deliver proof terms, not proof scripts. Acceptance = "kernel accepts the term"; a phase is not done until check_type returns Ok.
  • Violation → break. Bolt on a backtracking search that "finds" a proof and trusts its own success flag without re-running check_type → search bug = accepted non-proof. Folding search into the trusted path also blows the DP-1 budget.

6.3 DP-3 — Propositions-as-types (Curry-Howard)

  • Statement. Logical structure is encoded as types, not as a bespoke proposition datatype:
implication  A => B     ≡  Pi(A, B)        (KTerm::Pi, B not depending on the witness)
forall x:A. B           ≡  Pi(A, B)
conjunction  A /\ B      ≡  Sigma/pair encoding via Pi (Church-style) 
derivation tree          ≡  a KTerm proof term
"e is well-typed: |-e:T" ≡  inhabitant of the encoded Has-Type(...) KType
  • Object judgments (Has-Type(ctx,e,T), Step(e,e')) become LF-style inductive signatures (Phase 6, ## 18); their induction/elimination principles are kernel terms.
  • Tradeoff. Single uniform mechanism (one check_type) vs. ergonomic special-purpose logic. Accepted: uniformity shrinks the TCB (DP-1) and removes a second trusted checker.
  • Operational implication. progress_statement/preservation_statement return KTerm (a KType), never a Rust bool or AST of a separate logic.
  • Violation → break. Introduce a parallel enum Prop { Imp, Forall, … } with its own evaluator → two TCBs, two ways to be unsound, and equal/whnf no longer cover proposition equality, so conversion in proofs silently diverges from the logic.

6.4 DP-4 — de Bruijn or capture bugs

  • Statement. Object terms use de Bruijn indices (Term::Var(usize)); binders carry no names. shift(t, d, cutoff) and subst(t, j, s) are total functions; capture-avoidance is automatic given correct shifting; alpha_eq is then plain structural equality. Same discipline in the kernel (KTerm::Var(usize), Ctx as a de Bruijn list).
  • Invariant (must hold ∀ t): indices < depth(t) are bound; ≥ depth are free relative to context; shift only adjusts free indices crossing a binder (idx >= cutoff).
  • Tradeoff. No name-capture class of bugs and trivial α-equality vs. unreadable raw terms. Mitigated by the named-surface parser in tsp-syntax (names exist only at the I/O boundary).
  • Operational implication. Phase 1 (## 13) property tests: alpha_eq reflexive/symmetric/transitive; shift(shift(t,a,c),b,c) == shift(t,a+b,c); round-trip parse→deBruijn→print.
  • Violation → break. Wrong cutoff (> cutoff instead of >= cutoff) in shift → a variable that should stay bound gets renumbered → subst captures or frees the wrong index → the substitution lemma (DP-5) is false, and since the proof mirrors it, the kernel rejects a true preservation proof (best case) or the tester reports a phantom counterexample (worst case).

6.5 DP-5 — The substitution lemma mirrors subst exactly

  • Statement. The substitution lemma — Gamma, x:S |- e:T ∧ Gamma |- v:S ⇒ Gamma |- [v/x]e : T — is the crux (used at every beta/elimination step of Preservation, DP). Its machine-checked proof in tsp-meta (Phase 7, ## 19) must be structurally isomorphic to tsp-term::subst: same recursion, same index/cutoff arithmetic, same binder-crossing shift.
  • Tradeoff. Coupling proof shape to code shape (refactor subst ⇒ refactor the lemma) vs. catching off-by-ones immediately. Accepted: the spine names an off-by-one in shift as the lemma-breaker.
  • Operational implication. Code review checklist pairs each subst/shift clause with its lemma case. Phase 7 acceptance: kernel checks the lemma term; DP-7 cross-checks it against tsp-term::subst on random terms (substitution commutes with typing empirically).
  • Violation → break. subst shifts s by the wrong amount under a binder but the lemma proof "assumes" the correct shift → either the kernel rejects the (now-mismatched) proof, or — if the proof is wrong in the same direction — a false lemma type-checks and Preservation is unsound.

6.6 DP-6 — Bidirectional, definitional equality

  • Statement. The kernel checker is bidirectional: infer_type(ctx,t) -> KTerm synthesizes; check_type(ctx,t,ty) checks against an expected KType. Type equality is definitional/conversion — equal(ctx,a,b) compares weak-head normal forms (whnf) up to beta (and optionally eta), never syntactic ==. The executable object checker infer/check in tsp-rules follows the same infer/check split.
  • Tradeoff. Bidirectional cuts annotation burden and makes inference syntax-directed (good for DP-13 exhaustiveness) but requires the explicit annotations the spine already bakes into Term::Lam(Type, …). Definitional equality is the only sound way to compare dependent types but needs a terminating whnf (DP-15).
  • Operational implication. Phase 5 (## 17) implements infer_type/check_type in bidirectional style; check_type(t, ty) delegates to equal(ctx, infer_type(t), ty) at the leaves. No path compares KTerms by derive(PartialEq) for type-equality decisions.
  • Violation → break. Use syntactic equality instead of equal → (λx.x) Bool and Bool judged unequal → a valid proof whose goal contains an un-reduced redex is rejected; conversely, comparing un-normalized forms can also accept mismatched types if a buggy fast-path short-circuits, breaking DP-10.

6.7 DP-7 — Prove AND test — the dual guard

  • Statement. Every soundness property is defended twice: (a) a kernel-checked Proof in tsp-meta, and (b) empirical falsification in tsp-test via Generator + check_progress/check_preservation. The two are independent implementations of the same spec and must agree on the sound language (only TestResult::Pass).
  • Tradeoff. Duplicated effort (proof + property test) vs. defense-in-depth: the proof guards against all inputs but trusts the encoding; the tester guards against encoding/spec mistakes the proof can't see (a wrong progress_statement that is trivially provable). Neither alone is sufficient.
  • Operational implication. Phase 11 (## 23) cross-validates: tsp-test on the proven-sound language yields zero counterexamples; a divergence (test finds a counterexample on a "proved" language) is a stop-the-line bug indicating the encoded statement ≠ the tested property.
  • Violation → break. Ship Preservation with a vacuous goal (e.g. True) — the kernel happily checks it (DP-2 satisfied locally) — but with no tester, the vacuity goes unnoticed and "soundness" is meaningless. The dual guard turns a silent vacuity into a visible disagreement.

6.8 DP-8 — A broken rule must fail both guards

  • Statement. The Phase-12 (## 24) deliberately broken typing rule is the headline integration test: it must be caught by both guards — (a) the kernel: the soundness proof fails to check (check_type returns Err), and (b) the tester: a CounterExample(Term, Witness) is found and Shrinker-minimized.
  • Tradeoff. Maintaining an intentionally-unsound variant alongside the sound one (extra fixtures, risk of confusion) vs. a high-signal end-to-end proof that both guards actually bite. Accepted: a guard never exercised on a true negative is untrustworthy.
  • Operational implication. Phase 13 (## 25) conformance suite includes known-broken language variants; each must be rejected by the kernel and falsified by the tester, with the shrunk counterexample recorded as a golden fixture.
  • Violation → break. A broken rule that the tester catches but the kernel still "proves" means the encoding is too weak to express the falsified property (the proof and the property drifted apart) — exactly the DP-7 failure made concrete. If neither guard fires, the framework cannot distinguish sound from unsound languages and the entire project's claim is void.

6.9 DP-9 — Determinism: same proof → same verdict

  • Statement. check_type, infer_type, whnf, equal, object infer/step, and the Generator (given a fixed seed) are deterministic: identical inputs → identical outputs/verdicts, run-to-run, machine-to-machine. No reliance on HashMap iteration order, addresses, wall-clock, or thread scheduling in any verdict-producing path.
  • Tradeoff. Forgoes nondeterministic optimizations (e.g. parallel speculative reduction) in the TCB vs. reproducible proofs and shrink-stable counterexamples. Accepted: a flaky verifier is worse than a slow one.
  • Operational implication. Generator seeds are logged in TestResult; a CounterExample is replayable from its seed. CI re-runs the conformance suite twice and diffs verdicts.
  • Violation → break. Use HashMap for the Ctx and let iteration order leak into equal → the same proof passes on one run, fails on another → "the proof checks" becomes non-reproducible, destroying the audit trail and DP-1's guarantee.

6.10 DP-10 — Errors, never panic — never accept an ill-typed proof

  • Statement. All trusted functions are total over their input type and return Result<_, KernelError> / Result<_, TypeError> / Result<_, DerivError>; they never panic!, never unwrap, never index out of bounds, never infinite-loop on adversarial input. The default verdict is rejection: any ambiguity, exhaustion (DP-15), or unhandled shape ⇒ Err, never Ok.
  • Tradeoff. Pervasive Result plumbing and explicit bounds checks vs. fail-closed safety. Accepted: a panic in the kernel is a denial-of-service and (if recovered) potential unsoundness.
  • Operational implication. Phase 13 (## 25) fuzzes the kernel on adversarial KTerms with the assertion: no panic AND no Ok for an ill-typed term. #![deny(clippy::unwrap_used, clippy::panic, clippy::indexing_slicing)] on tsp-kernel.
  • Violation → break. A bare vec[idx] in Ctx lookup panics on a malformed de Bruijn index instead of returning Err(KernelError::UnboundVar) → adversarial proof term crashes the verifier (DoS); worse, a catch_unwind that maps panic→Ok would accept the ill-typed term.

6.11 DP-11 — Bounded object-language scope, unbounded rigor

  • Statement. Object languages are deliberately small textbook calculi (STLC + naturals, booleans, products, sums, references; System F as a stretch — see non-goals). Scope is bounded; rigor is not — within scope, zero proof gaps, zero admit/sorry, zero trusted-by-assertion lemmas.
  • Tradeoff. No real-world language features (no general recursion soundness, no effects beyond the references stretch, no GUI) vs. fully closing every metatheory obligation. Accepted: the value is a complete soundness proof of a small calculus, not a partial proof of a big one.
  • Operational implication. Phase 10 (## 22) re-establishes progress + preservation for each extension before the language grows further; an extension is not "added" until both theorems re-check (DP-7) for it.
  • Violation → break. Add references but skip re-proving Preservation with the store + store typing → the framework claims soundness for a calculus whose hardest case (aliasing through the store) is unverified — precisely where TAPL's references chapter shows naive rules go wrong.

6.12 DP-12 — One source of truth for each fact

  • Statement. Each rule/judgment is defined once as Rule/Judgment data in tsp-rules; the executable infer/step and the Derivation checker check_deriv are derived from or validated against that data — never an independent re-spelling. The LangDef parsed by tsp-syntax is the canonical rule set.
  • Tradeoff. Indirection (rules-as-data interpreted) over hand-written-per-rule code paths vs. eliminating the "two definitions drift" bug class. Accepted: drift between a checker and "the rules" is unfalsifiable without this discipline.
  • Operational implication. Phase 3 (## 15) acceptance: a Derivation accepted by check_deriv against rules has a conclusion that infer reproduces; mismatch is a bug in one of the two, surfaced immediately.
  • Violation → break. Hard-code the If typing rule in infer but maintain a separate textual rule for the kernel encoding → fix a bug in one, forget the other → the proof is about a different type system than the one the type checker implements; "preservation" no longer describes the running checker.

6.13 DP-13 — Closed-world inversion via exhaustive match

  • Statement. Inversion and canonical-forms reasoning rely on exhaustive match over closed enums (Term, Type, KTerm, Sort, Derivation): every constructor arm is handled explicitly; no _ => … wildcard in verdict-producing kernel/checker code. Adding a constructor is a compile error until every match site is updated.
  • Tradeoff. Verbose matches and churn when the language grows (DP-11 extensions touch many sites) vs. the compiler proving case-completeness — the mechanized analogue of the inversion lemma's exhaustiveness.
  • Operational implication. #![deny(non_exhaustive_omitted_patterns)] where applicable; canonical-forms (Phase 7, ## 19) and progress (Phase 9, ## 21) lean on this so a missing value-shape is caught at compile time, not by a runtime counterexample.
  • Violation → break. A _ => Err(...) wildcard in is_value/infer "absorbs" the new Succ constructor when naturals are added → canonical forms for Nat is silently incomplete → progress proof has an un-covered case the kernel can't see because the Rust match hid it.

6.14 DP-14 — Layered trust, acyclic dependencies

  • Statement. The crate graph is a DAG matching the architecture diagram; trust flows toward tsp-kernel. tsp-kernel and tsp-term are no_std + alloc and depend on nothing project-internal that is untrusted. tsp-meta depends on tsp-rules + tsp-kernel; tsp-test on tsp-rules; tsp-cli (only std) sits at the top. No cycles, and nothing untrusted is a dependency of the kernel.
  • tsp-cli (std) ──> tsp-meta ──> tsp-kernel (TCB, no_std+alloc)
    • └──────> tsp-rules ──> tsp-term (no_std+alloc)
    • > tsp-test ─────────────> tsp-rules └──> tsp-syntax (no_std+alloc)
    • > tsp-rules / tsp-syntax / tsp-term
  • (arrows = "depends on"; trust increases right-to-left toward tsp-kernel)
  • Tradeoff. Strict layering forbids convenient back-edges (e.g. kernel calling the object stepper) vs. keeping the TCB free of untrusted code (DP-1). Accepted absolutely.
  • Operational implication. Phase 0 (## 12) wires the workspace; a cargo-deny/graph check in CI rejects any new edge into tsp-kernel or any dependency cycle.
  • Violation → break. Let tsp-kernel call into tsp-rules::infer "to reuse the type checker" → the untrusted object checker is now inside the TCB; a bug in infer becomes a kernel unsoundness, collapsing DP-1's ≤800-LoC audit boundary.

6.15 DP-15 — Conversion terminates or check fails closed

  • Statement. whnf and equal operate on lambda-Pi terms where ill-formed inputs can diverge under naive reduction. Reduction is fuel-bounded (a step budget threaded through whnf); exhausting fuel returns KernelError (conversion undecided ⇒ reject), never an infinite loop and never a default-accept. This upholds DP-10 in the one place the kernel could otherwise hang.
  • Tradeoff. A finite fuel may reject a well-typed term whose normalization is unusually long (false negative) vs. guaranteeing the verifier always halts. Accepted: a halting verifier that occasionally needs a raised budget beats one that hangs on adversarial proofs; fuel is tunable and logged.
  • Operational implication. Fuel budget is a named constant; Phase 5 (## 17) tests whnf termination on adversarial KTerms (e.g. self-application (λx. x x)(λx. x x) encodings) ⇒ Err, not loop. Benchmarks (## 25) record typical fuel consumption so the budget has margin.
  • Violation → break. Unbounded whnf on an adversarial proof term that encodes a non-normalizing redex → the kernel spins forever (DoS) during equal; under a timeout-and-retry wrapper that defaults to Ok, it would accept an unconvertible pair, violating DP-6 and DP-10 at once.

6.16 Principle interaction matrix

How the principles reinforce (and occasionally trade against) each other:

Tension Resolved by Rule
Tiny TCB (DP-1) vs. expressive logic encode in types (DP-3), not in trusted code DP-1 wins
No search (DP-2) vs. authoring effort deterministic proof builders in tsp-meta (DP-9) DP-2 wins
Proof completeness vs. encoding mistakes independent tester cross-checks (DP-7) both required
Termination (DP-15) vs. accepting all valid proofs fuel-bounded, fail-closed (DP-10) DP-10 wins
Small scope (DP-11) vs. real-world coverage unbounded rigor inside scope scope bounded
Layering (DP-14) vs. code reuse into kernel duplicate small helpers rather than import DP-1/DP-14 win
Exhaustive match churn (DP-13) vs. velocity compiler-enforced inversion completeness DP-13 wins

Conflict-resolution order (highest precedence first): DP-1 (tiny TCB) ▸ DP-10 (fail-closed) ▸ DP-14 (layered trust) ▸ DP-3 (props-as-types) ▸ DP-7 (dual guard) ▸ all others. When a proposed change improves one principle at the cost of a higher-precedence one, the higher-precedence principle is dispositive and the change is rejected at review.

7. System Invariants

System-wide properties that MUST hold at all times. Violation = correctness defect, not a tuning knob. Each invariant: statement → why → enforced where → detection → telemetry → recovery → debugging. Severity: GRAVEST = silent unsoundness of the trusted computing base (TCB); CRITICAL = soundness of a derived layer; MAJOR = liveness / ergonomics. Ordered by load-bearing weight.

# Invariant Severity Locus (crate)
INV-1 Kernel soundness — check_type/infer_type accept t:ty only if t genuinely inhabits ty GRAVEST tsp-kernel
INV-2 Kernel totality — no panic / non-termination divergence on any KTerm input GRAVEST tsp-kernel
INV-3 shift/subst preserve well-formedness + are capture-avoiding (de Bruijn laws) CRITICAL tsp-term
INV-4 alpha_eq is an equivalence relation, consistent with subst CRITICAL tsp-term
INV-5 check_deriv(d) ⟺ d is a valid instance of rules (sound + complete) CRITICAL tsp-rules
INV-6 infer agrees with the typing relation (executable checker ⟺ inductive Has-Type) CRITICAL tsp-rules
INV-7 Substitution-lemma proof term type-checks against its KType CRITICAL tsp-meta
INV-8 preservation_statement + progress_statement proof terms type-check CRITICAL tsp-meta
INV-9 Generator emits only well-typed closed Terms (failure ⇒ real soundness bug) CRITICAL tsp-test
INV-10 A deliberately broken rule is rejected by kernel AND falsified by tester (dual-guard) CRITICAL tsp-meta+tsp-test
INV-11 whnf/equal decide definitional equality soundly (no over/under-conversion) GRAVEST tsp-kernel
INV-12 TCB boundary minimality — only tsp-kernel is trusted; no soundness logic leaks out CRITICAL workspace
INV-13 step is well-defined (deterministic) and type-respecting on closed terms MAJOR tsp-rules
INV-14 Shrinker preserves the falsified property (minimal counterexample still fails) MAJOR tsp-test

7.1 INV-1 — Kernel soundness (THE load-bearing invariant, GRAVEST)

  • Statement. check_type(ctx,t,ty)=Ok(()) (and infer_type(ctx,t)=Ok(ty)) holds iff t is a well-typed KTerm inhabiting KType ty under ctx per the lambda-Pi typing rules. The accept-set is exactly the inhabitation relation — no extra terms, no missing ones.
  • Why. A Prop is a KType; a Proof is a KTerm the kernel accepts at that KType (Curry-Howard / propositions-as-types). If the kernel accepts even ONE ill-typed proof term, it silently validates a FALSE theorem (e.g. a bogus "preservation" proof) — every downstream guarantee collapses. This is the gravest failure class in the spine.
  • Enforced where. tsp_kernel::infer_type / check_type only. Bidirectional discipline: infer_type synthesizes (Var,Sort,App,Pi), check_type switches mode at Lam and falls back to infer_type + equal elsewhere. NO other crate may decide proof validity.
  • Detection.
    • Soundness (no false accepts): fuzz INV-2's adversarial KTerms + a hand-curated ill-typed corpus (tsp-kernel/tests/ill_typed/); every member MUST yield Err(KernelError). Cross-check against an independent reference checker (a second, naive implementation kept in tests/ only) — accept-sets MUST coincide on the corpus.
    • Completeness (no false rejects): every proof term tsp-meta emits MUST be accepted; a regression here is caught by INV-7/INV-8.
  • Telemetry. tsp check --kernel-stats emits: kernel_accept_total, kernel_reject_total, kernel_check_us (p50/p99), conv_calls (equal invocations), whnf_steps_total, max_ctx_depth, proof_term_nodes. Histogram of KernelError kind.
  • Recovery. None at runtime — soundness is binary. On suspected breach: freeze the TCB, revert tsp-kernel to last audited commit, re-run the full conformance + fuzz gate (Phase 13) before any further proof is trusted.
  • Debugging. Minimize the offending KTerm (shrink à la INV-14 but over proof terms); dump the failing rule application via KERNEL_TRACE=1 (logs each infer_type step: rule, sub-goal ctx⊢sub:?, equal checks). Diff against the reference checker to localize the divergent constructor.
  • Audit obligation. tsp-kernel ≤ 800 LOC (target ~500), zero unsafe, zero deps beyond core+alloc. Every line is on the trust path; reviewed line-by-line at each Phase 13 gate. See INV-12.

7.2 INV-2 — Kernel totality / no-panic (GRAVEST)

  • Statement. For every KTerm (well- or ill-formed, adversarial, deeply nested), infer_type/check_type/whnf/equal return a value (Ok/Err/normal form) — never panic!, unwrap on None, index out of bounds, stack overflow, or infinite loop.
  • Why. A panic in the TCB is a denial-of-service AND a soundness hole: a checker that crashes on a malformed proof has not rejected it. Totality is what makes "rejected" trustworthy. Non-termination of whnf on an open/ill-typed term would hang tsp check forever.
  • Enforced where. Result-typed signatures everywhere in tsp-kernel (no panicking paths); de Bruijn lookups via checked ctx.get(i) -> Result<_, KernelError::UnboundVar>; whnf reduces only well-typed redexes — on ill-typed input it returns Err before looping (normalization guaranteed only on typed terms, so type-check gates reduction). Recursion depth bounded by an explicit fuel/depth counter (KernelError::DepthExceeded at e.g. 2^16) to convert stack overflow into a clean error.
  • Detection. cargo fuzz target fuzz_kernel (Phase 13): arbitrary KTerm via structured Arbitrary impl; harness asserts the call returns (catches panics) and completes under a wall-clock budget (catches loops). #![forbid(unsafe_code)] + clippy lints unwrap_used,expect_used,indexing_slicing deny-listed in tsp-kernel.
  • Telemetry. Fuzzer: kernel_fuzz_execs, kernel_fuzz_crashes (must be 0), kernel_fuzz_timeouts (must be 0), max_observed_depth.
  • Recovery. Any crash/timeout fixture is checked into tsp-kernel/fuzz/corpus/ as a permanent regression; build fails until fixed.
  • Debugging. Run the saved crash input under RUST_BACKTRACE=full; for loops, bisect with the depth counter lowered to surface the diverging whnf redex.

7.3 INV-3 — shift/subst preserve well-formedness, capture-avoiding (CRITICAL)

  • Statement. For closed-under-context Terms, the de Bruijn laws hold (lightweight, EBNF-style):
shift(t,0,c)            = t                              -- zero shift is identity
shift(shift(t,a,c),b,c) = shift(t,a+b,c)                -- shifts compose
subst(shift(t,1,j), j, s) = t                            -- subst undoes the shift at j
shift(subst(t,j,s), d, c) = subst(shift(t,d,c'), j', shift(s,d,c))   -- shift/subst commute

where indices ≥ cutoff are adjusted and s is shifted under each binder it crosses. No free index of t is ever captured by a Lam binder introduced during subst.

  • Why. subst/shift are the computational core the substitution lemma mirrors exactly (INV-7). An off-by-one in shift does not crash — it silently captures a variable, making subst unsound; the substitution-lemma proof would then encode a false equation and INV-1 would (rightly) reject it, OR worse, a parallel bug hides it. Capture-avoidance must be structural, not heuristic.
  • Enforced where. tsp_term::shift(t,d,cutoff), subst(t,j,s). subst increments j and re-shifts s by 1 on descending through each Lam.
  • Detection. Property tests (proptest, tsp-term/tests/debruijn_laws.rs) over random Terms assert all four laws above + is_value stability under shift. Differential check vs a named-variable reference substitution (in tests only). The Phase 1 smoke test pins step((λx.x) true) --> true (a subst of true for index 0).
  • Telemetry. Dev-only counters subst_calls,shift_calls,max_subst_depth; not in release.
  • Recovery. None — a failing law is a hard bug; fix shift/subst and re-run.
  • Debugging. Shrink the failing Term pair; print pre/post index maps; compare with the named reference to see which binder mis-shifted.

7.4 INV-4 — alpha_eq equivalence, consistent with subst (CRITICAL)

  • Statement. alpha_eq is reflexive, symmetric, transitive; and respects substitution: alpha_eq(a,b) ⇒ alpha_eq(subst(a,j,s), subst(b,j,s)) and alpha_eq(s,s') ⇒ alpha_eq(subst(t,j,s), subst(t,j,s')). Because terms are de Bruijn, alpha_eq is structural equality (a == b as trees) — so equivalence is by construction and the lemma is that structural eq is the right notion (no name noise to quotient).
  • Why. Typing and reduction quotient terms by alpha; if alpha_eq disagreed with structural de Bruijn equality, infer/step/check_deriv could distinguish provably-equal terms, breaking INV-5/INV-6. Consistency with subst is what lets the substitution lemma (INV-7) treat [v/x]e as well-defined.
  • Enforced where. tsp_term::alpha_eq (a structural == over Term).
  • Detection. Property tests: the three equivalence laws + the two subst-congruence laws; plus alpha_eq(a,b) ⟺ (a==b) to confirm de Bruijn collapses alpha into syntactic identity.
  • Telemetry. None (pure, cheap).
  • Recovery. None.
  • Debugging. Counterexample shrinking to the smallest distinguishing Term pair.

7.5 INV-5 — check_deriv sound + complete vs the rules (CRITICAL)

  • Statement. check_deriv(d, rules) = Ok(()) iff d:Derivation is a well-formed derivation tree: its rule names some Rule r ∈ rules, d.conclusion is an instance of r.conclusion under one substitution σ, d.subderivations are in 1-1 order with r.premises each instantiated by the same σ, and each sub-derivation recursively check_derivs. No extra/missing premises; no σ mismatch.
  • Why. A Derivation is the data witness that becomes a kernel proof term (Phase 6). If check_deriv accepted an ill-formed tree, tsp-meta would encode a non-derivation as a proof and INV-1 would face a malformed input — the two checkers must agree on "what is a valid derivation."
  • Enforced where. tsp_rules::check_deriv returning Result<(),DerivError> (DerivError: UnknownRule,ConclusionMismatch,PremiseArityMismatch,SubstClash).
  • Detection. Positive corpus: every derivation produced by infer (when it records its tree) MUST check_deriv Ok. Negative corpus tsp-rules/tests/bad_derivs/: wrong rule name, dropped premise, inconsistent σ — each MUST Err. Property test: check_deriv(build_deriv(infer(e))) == Ok for random well-typed e.
  • Telemetry. deriv_check_total, deriv_reject_total, DerivError kind histogram.
  • Recovery. None — binary.
  • Debugging. DerivError carries the offending node path (e.g. root.premise[1].premise[0]) + the failing σ binding; dump with DERIV_TRACE=1.

7.6 INV-6 — infer agrees with the typing relation (CRITICAL)

  • Statement. The executable checker is sound and complete w.r.t. the inductive Has-Type relation: infer(ctx,e)=Ok(T) ⟺ ∃ derivation of Gamma ⊢ e : T (with Gamma ≙ ctx); and infer(ctx,e)=Err(_) ⟺ no such T exists. Types are unique (STLC), so infer is a function computing the unique T.
  • Why. infer is the practical front door (tsp typecheck) and the term generator's oracle (INV-9). If infer disagreed with the relation that the proofs reason about, the tester could feed "well-typed" terms that the metatheory does not actually cover — making INV-9/INV-10 meaningless.
  • Enforced where. tsp_rules::infer/check; the relation is the rules table that check_deriv validates against.
  • Detection. Round-trip property: infer(e)=Ok(T) ⇒ check_deriv(reconstruct_deriv(e,T)) = Ok; and infer(e)=Err ⇒ no Rule conclusion unifies. Cross-validate against tsp-test: every Generator output e has infer(e)=Ok. Phase 0 smoke: infer(Lam(Bool, Var 0)) = Ok(Arrow(Bool,Bool)).
  • Telemetry. infer_total, infer_err_total, TypeError kind histogram, infer_us p99.
  • Recovery. None — disagreement is a bug in infer or the rule table; reconcile before proceeding.
  • Debugging. Bisect the subterm where infer and the relation diverge using the reconstructed derivation tree.

7.7 INV-7 — Substitution-lemma proof term type-checks (CRITICAL, the crux)
