  • NOT trusted: a tester that wrongly reports Pass cannot create a false theorem (only the kernel can); its job is fast falsification, not certification.

11.7 Type-membership summary (what is trusted)

                     tsp-cli (std)          [untrusted: orchestration, I/O, pretty-print]
                         |
   tsp-syntax -----------+------------- tsp-test  [untrusted: parser & falsifier;
   [untrusted: parser]   |               outputs are re-checked / non-certifying]
                         |
   tsp-rules  [untrusted: infer/step/check_deriv are algorithmic oracles,
               re-validated against the kernel-encoded relations]
                         |
   tsp-meta   [untrusted statement+proof BUILDER; produces (goal, term) the kernel must accept]
                         |
   tsp-term::{shift, subst, alpha_eq}   <== TCB (substitution lemma mirrors these)
   tsp-kernel::{KTerm, Sort, Ctx, infer_type, check_type, whnf, equal}  <== TCB (the load-bearing checker)
  • TCB size budget: kernel ≤ ~1500 LoC; shift+subst+alpha_eq ≤ ~150 LoC; combined audited surface ≤ ~1700 LoC (quantified gate in ## 25).
  • Everything above the line is checked by the line: a bug there yields a REJECTED proof or a failed test, never a silently accepted false theorem.
  • Single load-bearing invariant restated: infer_type/check_type never accept an ill-typed KTerm. All other correctness is reducible to this.

12. Phase 0 — Skeleton, workspace, and the "typecheck identity, step an application" smoke test

Bootstrap-only phase: zero metatheory, zero proofs. Goal is a compiling 7-crate workspace + one green end-to-end smoke test wiring tsp-cli -> tsp-syntax -> tsp-term -> tsp-rules. Type shells + error enums + no_std/alloc gating + CI/Makefile/Dockerfile + reference-oracle fixture scaffold. Everything later replaces stubs; nothing here is load-bearing for soundness.

0.1 Objectives

  • Cargo virtual-manifest workspace, 7 members tsp-syntax tsp-term tsp-rules tsp-kernel tsp-meta tsp-test tsp-cli; resolver "2", edition 2021, MSRV 1.78 pinned in rust-toolchain.toml.
  • Declare every LOCKED core type as a shell (variants present, bodies todo!()/minimal) so signatures compile and the dependency DAG is exercised end-to-end.
  • no_std + alloc gating on the 5 core crates (tsp-syntax tsp-term tsp-rules tsp-kernel tsp-meta); tsp-test/tsp-cli are std. Boundary enforced now → cheap later, retrofitting no_std is costly.
  • One smoke test proving the spine: infer STLC Lam(Bool, Var 0) : Arrow(Bool,Bool); step((λx:Bool.x) true) == Some(TmTrue).
  • CI (fmt + clippy -D warnings + build + test + MSRV) green; make/Dockerfile reproducible; TAPL / Software-Foundations reference-oracle fixture format frozen.

0.2 Detailed Implementation Steps

Theory anchors: ADR-01 (Rust + MSRV 1.78), ADR-02 (seven-crate workspace), ADR-22 (zero-dep TCB), ADR-23 (CI pipeline), ADR-24 (MSRV pin), B5 (no_std + alloc setup), INV-12 (TCB boundary minimality).

Workspace layout (paths LOCKED):

/Cargo.toml            # [workspace] members=[7]; resolver="2"; [workspace.package] edition/rust-version
/rust-toolchain.toml   # channel="1.78.0"; components=[rustfmt,clippy]
/Makefile  /Dockerfile  /.github/workflows/ci.yml  /deny.toml
/crates/tsp-{syntax,term,rules,kernel,meta,test,cli}/{Cargo.toml,src/lib.rs|main.rs}
/fixtures/{typing,steps}/*.txt   # reference-oracle expected outputs
Crate Modules (files) Shells declared this phase Dep edges
tsp-term term.rs ty.rs ops.rs Term,Type,shift,subst,alpha_eq,is_value —
tsp-syntax lexer.rs token.rs parser.rs error.rs LangDef,Production,JudgmentForm,Token,ParseError{Kind} tsp-term
tsp-rules judgment.rs rule.rs deriv.rs infer.rs step.rs error.rs Judgment,Rule,Derivation,check_deriv,infer,step,Context,TypeError,DerivError tsp-term, tsp-syntax
tsp-kernel kterm.rs ctx.rs check.rs whnf.rs eq.rs error.rs KTerm,Sort,Ctx,infer_type,check_type,whnf,equal,KernelError —
tsp-meta encode.rs lemmas.rs theorems.rs progress_statement,preservation_statement,Proof tsp-rules, tsp-kernel
tsp-test gen.rs shrink.rs props.rs Generator,TestResult,Witness,check_progress,check_preservation tsp-rules
tsp-cli main.rs cli.rs tsp bin: check test typecheck step (parse args, dispatch; bodies stub except smoke path) all
  • Atom: alias pub type Atom = String; in tsp-syntax (interner deferred to Phase 2). Re-export for tsp-term/tsp-rules to avoid duplicate defs.
  • Core-crate lib.rs head: #![no_std] + extern crate alloc; + use alloc::{boxed::Box, vec::Vec, string::String};. Box<Type>/Box<Term>/Box<KTerm> need alloc — gated now.
  • infer smoke wiring (only path implemented; rest todo!()): Var i -> ctx[i]; Lam(ty, body) -> Arrow(ty, infer(ctx.push(ty), body)); TmTrue/TmFalse -> Base("Bool"); App(f,a) -> match infer(f){Arrow(s,t) if infer(a)==s => t}.
  • step smoke wiring: beta App(Lam(_,b), v) if is_value(v) -> Some(subst(b,0,v)); is_value: Lam|TmTrue|TmFalse|Zero true. subst/shift minimal correct-enough for identity (subst(Var 0, 0, s)=s); full discipline in Phase 1.
  • tsp-cli: clap-free hand-rolled arg dispatch (keep std-only dep surface tiny); typecheck/step subcommands drive the two smoke assertions through tsp-rules.
  • All non-smoke fns return unimplemented!() or a placeholder Err(..) so the crate compiles and integration tests can target individual edges.

0.3 Deliverable

cargo build --workspace + cargo test --workspace green from a clean checkout and inside docker build. make ci runs fmt/clippy/build/test. tsp typecheck and tsp step execute the smoke path on a hard-coded STLC term and print Bool -> Bool / true. fixtures/ populated with ≥4 frozen oracle files. README "build" section reproducible on MSRV 1.78.

0.4 Acceptance Criteria

  • cargo build --workspace & cargo +1.78.0 build --workspace: exit 0, 0 warnings (-D warnings).
  • cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets -- -D warnings and cargo fmt --check: exit 0.
  • Smoke test asserts infer(&empty, &id_bool) == Ok(Arrow(Box(Base("Bool")),Box(Base("Bool")))) AND step(&app_id_true) == Some(TmTrue); passes.
  • no_std proven: cargo build -p tsp-term --no-default-features builds for thumbv7em-none-eabi (or cargo check with a no_std smoke target); accidental std import fails CI.
  • Workspace member count == 7; cargo metadata dependency DAG matches the table (no extra edges, no cycles). cargo build --workspace wall time < 30 s cold on CI runner.

0.5 Conformance Fixtures

Format: one record per file, term | TAB | expected. Cited oracle: TAPL Ch. 9 (STLC) / Software Foundations Stlc.v.

File Input (object syntax / AST) Expected Source
fixtures/typing/id_bool.txt Lam(Bool, Var 0) Bool -> Bool TAPL §9.2
fixtures/typing/app_true.txt App(Lam(Bool,Var 0), TmTrue) Bool TAPL §9.3
fixtures/typing/ill_typed.txt App(TmTrue, TmFalse) Err(TypeError) (not callable) malformed
fixtures/steps/beta_id.txt App(Lam(Bool,Var 0), TmTrue) --> TmTrue TAPL E-AppAbs
fixtures/steps/value_stuck.txt TmTrue None (value, no step) malformed-ish

Malformed/negative: ill_typed.txt → infer returns Err, harness asserts is_err(); value_stuck.txt → step==None. A test runner reads each file, calls infer/step, diffs against the expected column (string-eq after canonical pretty-print).

0.6 Pitfalls

Pitfall Root cause Symptom / debug
no_std crate silently pulls std a dep w/o default-features=false, or use std:: builds on host, fails thumbv7em target → cargo tree -e features; gate in CI early
de Bruijn off-by-one in stub subst subst(Var 0,0,s) vs Var 1 confusion before Phase 1 lands real shift smoke step returns wrong term/panics → keep stub literally subst(Var 0,0,s)=s only
Box-recursive enums won't #[derive(Copy)] Term/Type/KTerm are heap-recursive derive only Clone,PartialEq,Eq,Debug; reaching for Copy = compile error
todo!() stubs detonate in CI a non-smoke path executed by an over-eager test restrict tests to wired edges; #[ignore] placeholders for future phases
Workspace edition/MSRV drift per-crate edition= instead of [workspace.package] inheritance one crate on 2018 → clippy lint divergence; inherit via edition.workspace=true
Atom=String defined twice both tsp-syntax and tsp-term declare it type-mismatch across crate boundary → single definition + re-export

0.7 Performance Concerns

  • Not a perf phase, but lock the baselines: cold cargo build --workspace < 30 s, incremental < 3 s on CI. Regression here signals a fat dependency crept in.
  • Keep dependency surface near-zero (no clap/serde/proptest yet) — small TCB-adjacent crate graph keeps later tsp-kernel audit honest; each crate must justify its existence.
  • Box<T> indirection on every Term/KTerm node is the deliberate representation; pointer-chasing cost is acknowledged and revisited only if Phase 13 benchmarks miss kernel-check-time targets.

0.8 Edge Cases

  • Empty Context / empty Ctx (de Bruijn index 0 into empty env) → infer must Err, not panic — covered by the closed-term smoke assertions.
  • step on a value (TmTrue) → None (not Some(same)); distinguishes "stuck/value" from "reduces". Fixture value_stuck.txt.
  • alpha_eq is structural on de Bruijn, so Lam(Bool,Var 0) ≡ Lam(Bool,Var 0) trivially; no name-mangling case yet (Phase 1).
  • Unicode in object source: deferred; Phase 0 fixtures are ASCII AST literals, parser arrives Phase 2.
  • Zero-member or duplicate-member workspace manifest → cargo metadata check fails fast in CI.

0.9 Failure Analysis

Failure Blast radius Containment
Smoke infer green but encodes wrong rule masks future inversion/canonical-forms bugs cross-check against fixtures/typing/* oracle, not just the inline assert
no_std gate not actually enforced core crate becomes un-no_std-able later; expensive retrofit dedicated thumbv7em build job is a required CI check, not informational
CI green locally, red on clean runner unpinned toolchain / cached artifacts Dockerfile + rust-toolchain.toml make the build hermetic; CI uses --locked
todo!()/unimplemented!() reachable in shipped tsp user runs un-wired subcommand → panic unwired subcommands print not yet implemented (phase N) and exit nonzero, never panic!

GRAVEST-class note: the kernel-unsoundness failure mode (kernel accepts an ill-typed proof term) is not reachable this phase — tsp-kernel is shell-only. No proof is checked until Phase 5; Phase 0 cannot give false soundness assurance.

0.10 Operational Notes

  • Makefile targets: make build|test|fmt|clippy|ci|docker|smoke (smoke runs only the two assertions for fast inner loop).
  • Dockerfile: FROM rust:1.78-slim; cargo build --workspace --locked; multi-stage → final image runs tsp. docker build . is the reproducibility oracle.
  • .github/workflows/ci.yml jobs: fmt, clippy, build-test (stable + MSRV matrix), nostd (thumbv7em). cargo-deny (deny.toml) guards licenses + the small dep graph.
  • TCB tracking starts now: a make tcb-lines placeholder (tokei crates/tsp-kernel/src) prints the trusted-base line count, target reported each phase; Phase 0 baseline ≈ shell size, real budget asserted from Phase 5.
  • Toolchain pinned (1.78.0) so clippy/fmt output is byte-stable across contributors; bumping MSRV is an explicit, reviewed change.

13. Phase 1 — Object terms and binding (de Bruijn, shift, capture-avoiding subst)

The binding layer (tsp-term) is the computational core the substitution lemma (Phase 7) mirrors line-for-line. An off-by-one in shift silently breaks subst, which silently breaks the machine-checked substitution lemma — so this phase locks down the index discipline first, against ~30 property-based laws, before any rule engine or kernel exists.

1.1 Objectives

  • Implement Term/Type (de Bruijn, nameless) per the LOCKED Core Types — Var(usize) is a nameless variable; binders (Lam) carry no name.
  • Implement total functions shift(t, d, cutoff), subst(t, j, s), alpha_eq(a, b), is_value(t) — no_std + alloc, zero panic!/unwrap on well-formed input.
  • Establish shift/subst algebraic laws (commutation, composition, distribution) as proptest invariants — these ARE the substitution lemma's computational content. Closed-term + free-variable analysis (is_closed, free_vars) used by Phase 9 progress (progress is stated for closed terms).
  • Deliverable: a binding layer whose laws hold over ≥10⁴ random terms, so Phase 7 reflects subst by construction rather than re-deriving it.

1.2 Detailed Implementation Steps

Theory anchors: P1 (de Bruijn 1972 — normative source for shift/subst/alpha_eq), ADR-03 (de Bruijn binding choice), ADR-12 (shift/subst total functions), INV-3 (shift/subst preserve well-formedness), INV-4 (alpha_eq is an equivalence relation), B1 ch.5–7 (shift algorithm TAPL 6.2.1, subst TAPL 6.2.4).

Crate tsp-term (no_std, extern crate alloc). Modules under tsp-term/src/:

File Contents Key items
lib.rs re-exports, #![no_std], crate-level Atom = u32 (interned base-type id) pub use term::*; pub use ty::*;
ty.rs Type enum (8 variants per spine) + Display Type::Arrow, Prod, Sum, Ref, All, TyVar(usize)
term.rs Term enum (de Bruijn) Var(usize), Lam(Type, Box<Term>), App, TmTrue/False, If, Zero, Succ
shift.rs shift, internal shift_above recursion crosses binders → cutoff += 1
subst.rs subst, subst_top (beta helper) reuses shift
alpha.rs alpha_eq (structural) derived-PartialEq-equivalent, validated
value.rs is_value, is_closed, free_vars BTreeSet<usize> (no_std-friendly)

Signatures added (exactly the spine; &Term -> Term, persistent, no in-place mutation):

pub fn shift(t: &Term, d: isize, cutoff: usize) -> Term;   // adjust free vars ≥ cutoff by d
pub fn subst(t: &Term, j: usize, s: &Term) -> Term;        // capture-avoiding [s/j]t
pub fn alpha_eq(a: &Term, b: &Term) -> bool;               // structural de Bruijn equality
pub fn is_value(t: &Term) -> bool;
pub fn subst_top(s: &Term, t: &Term) -> Term;              // beta: shift(subst(t,0,shift(s,1,0)),-1,0)
pub fn is_closed(t: &Term) -> bool;                        // free_vars(t).is_empty()
pub fn free_vars(t: &Term) -> BTreeSet<usize>;             // de Bruijn-level free indices

shift algorithm (TAPL 6.2.1, ↑^d_c): recurse; at Var(k) → if k < cutoff keep (bound), else Var((k as isize + d) as usize) (free, shifted); at Lam/binder-introducing node → recurse on body with cutoff+1; If/App/Succ recurse on subterms with same cutoff (d is isize, shifts can be −1 in beta). subst algorithm (TAPL 6.2.4, [j ↦ s]t): at Var(k) → if k == j { s.clone() } else { Var(k) }; at Lam(ty, body) → Lam(ty, subst(body, j+1, &shift(s, 1, 0))) — j+1 and shift(s,1,0) together give capture-avoidance (re-index the target under the new binder; lift s's free vars over it); other nodes recurse with same j, same s.

Beta substitution subst_top(s, t) = shift(&subst(t, 0, &shift(s, 1, 0)), -1, 0): lift s, substitute at 0, lower the result — the exact step Phase 4 step and Phase 7's substitution lemma invoke.

alpha_eq: structural recursion comparing constructors + indices + Type annotations; de Bruijn makes alpha-equivalence syntactic identity, so this equals derived PartialEq — kept as a named function to document intent and so the property test below proves the derived eq is sound under shift/subst (Type annotations on Lam participate: a-equivalence is annotation-sensitive in Church-style STLC). is_value: Lam(_,_) | TmTrue | TmFalse | Zero ⇒ true; Succ(t) ⇒ is_numeric_value(t) (nested Zero/Succ only); else false; App, If, free Var ⇒ not values.

1.3 Deliverable

tsp-term v0.1: cargo build -p tsp-term --no-default-features (no_std) succeeds; cargo test -p tsp-term green including the proptest law suite (≥10⁴ cases, seedable). cargo doc renders every public fn with its index-convention contract. No dependency on any other tsp-* crate (leaf of the DAG).

1.4 Acceptance Criteria

Criterion Target
Build (no_std + alloc) clean, 0 warnings under -D warnings
Property laws (§1.5) all pass, 10⁴ cases each, 0 shrunk failures
panic!/unwrap/expect in src 0 (grep-enforced in CI)
shift(t, 0, c) == t holds ∀ t, c (identity law)
Capture-avoidance dedicated fixtures pass (§1.5)
Coverage of term.rs/shift.rs/subst.rs ≥ 95% lines
Worst-case subst/shift on depth-d term linear in nodes, no exponential blowup

1.5 Conformance Fixtures

Property laws (proptest, tsp-term/tests/laws.rs) — the substitution-lemma kernel:

Fixture / law Statement Source
law_shift_id shift(t,0,c) = t identity
law_shift_compose shift(shift(t,d1,c), d2, c) = shift(t, d1+d2, c) when no var crosses below c composition
law_shift_swap shift(shift(t,d,a), e, b) = shift(shift(t,e,b'), d, a') (TAPL ex.) commutation
law_subst_shift shift(subst(t,j,s), d, c) = subst(shift(t,d,c'), j', shift(s,d,c)) the substitution lemma's core (Phase 7 reflects this)
law_subst_subst subst(subst(t,j,s1),k,s2) = subst(subst(t,k', s2), j, subst(s1,k,s2)) (j≠k side cond.) substitution composition
law_alpha_refl alpha_eq(t,t) reflexivity
law_value_stable is_value(t) ⇒ is_value(subst_top(v,t))? counterexamples documented (subst can break value-ness — expected) edge doc

Golden cases (tsp-term/tests/golden.rs):

  • id_term: Lam(Bool, Var 0) (the K=I term from Phase 0 smoke). is_value ⇒ true, is_closed ⇒ true, free_vars ⇒ {}.
  • beta_step: subst_top(TmTrue, Var 0) = TmTrue; subst_top(TmTrue, Lam(Bool, Var 1)) = Lam(Bool, TmTrue) (capture-avoidance: outer s lifted over inner binder, not captured).
  • shift_under_binder: shift(Lam(Bool, App(Var 0, Var 1)), 1, 0) = Lam(Bool, App(Var 0, Var 2)) (bound Var 0 untouched; free Var 1→Var 2).
  • open_term: Var 3 ⇒ is_closed = false, free_vars = {3}.

Malformed / negative cases: capture_regression — a hand-computed wrong result (e.g. subst_top(TmTrue, Lam(Bool, Var 1)) = Lam(Bool, Var 1), i.e. NO substitution) must be rejected by alpha_eq, guarding against forgetting j+1/shift(s,1,0); negative_index — shift(Var 0, -1, 0) would underflow usize and must be unreachable by construction (only subst_top produces d=-1, only after subst removed index 0) — subst_top never underflows on closed/valid inputs, and a direct shift(Var 0,-1,0) is a documented caller-contract violation.

1.6 Pitfalls

# Failure mode Root cause Debug
1 Variable capture forgot shift(s,1,0) when descending under Lam in subst capture_regression fixture fails; print indices at each binder depth
2 Off-by-one at binder used j instead of j+1 (or cutoff not cutoff+1) under Lam law_subst_shift shrinks to a 2-binder term; compare against TAPL hand-trace
3 usize underflow on shift −1 subst_top applied to a term still mentioning Var 0 (not yet removed) wrap in debug checked_sub; assert index 0 absent before the -1 shift
4 Type annotation ignored in alpha_eq structural recursion skips Lam's Type field Lam(Bool,Var 0) wrongly equals Lam(Nat,Var 0); add annotation compare
5 is_value accepts non-canonical Succ Succ(App(..)) treated as value use is_numeric_value helper, not is_value, in the Succ arm
6 Exponential blowup s.clone() deep-copied at every Var(j) hit in a term with many occurrences acceptable for textbook terms; note Rc<Term> as future opt (kept out of TCB path)
7 cutoff confused with d swapped argument order at a shift call site name args at call sites; law_shift_id catches shift(t, c, 0) mistakes

1.7 Performance Concerns

  • shift/subst are O(|t|) node-walks; subst clones s per occurrence → O(|t|·|s|) worst case. Textbook terms (|t|<10³) ⇒ sub-microsecond; not on any hot path that dominates kernel time. Persistent (immutable) representation chosen over mutation: simpler to reason about, matches the kernel's value semantics; Box<Term> heap nodes acceptable, Rc sharing deferred (would complicate the audited path with no proof benefit yet).
  • free_vars returns BTreeSet<usize> (no_std, deterministic ordering) — O(|t|·log f), fine; a depth-d term is O(d) boxes and the generator (Phase 11) bounds size, so no unbounded growth here. Target: 10⁴-case proptest suite completes < 5 s on CI (seedable for reproducibility).

1.8 Edge Cases

  • Open terms: shift/subst must work on terms with free vars (used mid-recursion under binders), not just closed terms. Var 0 at top level before subst_top lowering is the only place d=−1 shift is valid; index 0 must be absent post-subst.
  • Identical indices, different binder depth (Var 1 inside one Lam vs two): alpha_eq distinguishes via structure, not raw index. shift with cutoff exceeding all indices ⇒ identity (no free var ≥ cutoff).
  • Numeric values: Succ(Succ(Zero)) is a value, Succ(Var 0) is not (open) — the boundary for Phase 9 canonical forms (Nat values); If with a non-value scrutinee is not a value regardless of branches.
  • Empty/degenerate: a bare Var(usize::MAX) must not overflow in free_vars (a large free index is legal; only shift by positive d near MAX is a documented contract limit).

1.9 Failure Analysis

  • Silent unsoundness propagation: a wrong subst produces some Term (no crash), so the bug surfaces only when Phase 7's substitution lemma proof either fails to check (good) or — worse — type-checks against a mis-stated goal. Mitigation: laws in §1.5 are gating; Phase 7 reflects subst rather than re-implements it, so a single source of truth. Class: capture (gravest) is caught by capture_regression + law_subst_shift; class: off-by-one by composition laws; class: underflow by the subst_top contract test + debug checked_sub.
  • Detection ordering: property failures shrink to minimal terms automatically (proptest) — fast root-cause vs. hand-debugging large derivations later; no network/IO failure surface (pure functions).

1.10 Operational Notes

  • Pure, deterministic, no_std + alloc — embeddable; the same Term flows from tsp-syntax parse → tsp-rules infer/step → tsp-test generator. No global state, thread-safe (Send+Sync via owned Box). CI: cargo test -p tsp-term, cargo test -p tsp-term --no-default-features (no_std build), grep -rn 'unwrap\|expect\|panic!' src/ ⇒ must be empty, proptest seed pinned via PROPTEST_RNG_SEED for reproducible failures.
  • Versioning: Term/Type are public API consumed by 5 downstream crates — additive only after v0.1 (new Term variants for products/sums land in Phase 10 behind the same enum, requiring exhaustive-match updates everywhere; #[non_exhaustive] deliberately not used so the compiler forces every match arm — exhaustiveness is a soundness aid).
  • Auditability: this crate is outside the TCB (the kernel is the TCB) but is the model the TCB's substitution lemma must match; keep it small (< ~400 LOC) and law-covered so the correspondence is reviewable by inspection.

14. Phase 2 — The metalanguage (LangDef parser)

tsp-syntax: front end turning .lang source (sort decls, term/type grammars w/ binder annotations, judgment forms, inference rules) + object-term source into typed ASTs (LangDef, Term) with precise ParseError. no_std + alloc. Hand-written lexer + recursive-descent + Pratt parser — no parser-generator dep (TCB-adjacent code stays auditable; spec RULE: small/audited boundary). Two surfaces: the metalanguage (defines a language) and the object-term surface (programs in that language).

2.1 Objectives

  • Parse a complete STLC .lang into LangDef { sorts, grammar, judgments, rules } (canonical fields) — one pass, no backtracking past a fixed lookahead of 2.
  • Parse object terms into tsp-term::Term/Type against the grammar; bind names → de Bruijn indices using a scope stack (resolution, NOT in tsp-term).
  • ParseError { kind: ParseErrorKind, span, msg } for every failure; recover at statement boundaries to report ≥1 error/file before abort.
  • Deliverable gate: load stlc.lang, parse the smoke term Lam(Bool, Var 0) from concrete \x:Bool. x, round-trip-equal under alpha_eq.

2.2 Detailed Implementation Steps

Theory anchors: B3 ch.1–5 (JudgmentForm/Rule vocabulary — PFPL judgment/inference-rule terminology), ADR-18 (TAPL S1 as normative oracle for rule syntax), ADR-17 (parse errors as Result, never panic), INV-12 (parser is untrusted; a parse bug yields a wrong language, caught by kernel, never unsound).

Files under tsp-syntax/src/ (per §10 repo layout):

file contents
token.rs pub enum Token (Ident, Sort kw, : . -> ( ) [ ] |- ==> , λ/\, ;, Nat lit, true/false/succ/zero/if/then/else, EOF); Span{lo,hi:usize} byte offsets
lexer.rs pub struct Lexer<'a>; next() -> Result<(Token,Span),ParseError>; longest-match; |- & -> & ==> as single tokens; comments -- to EOL skipped
error.rs pub enum ParseErrorKind { UnexpectedToken, UnknownSort, BadBinder, MalformedRule } (LOCKED 4 variants); pub struct ParseError{kind,span,msg:String,expected:Vec<&'static str>}
ast.rs Production{ lhs:Atom, alts:Vec<Alt> }, Alt{ shape:Vec<Sym>, binders:Vec<BinderAnn> }, Sym::{Term(Atom),Type(Atom),Lit(Token),Nonterm(Atom)}, BinderAnn{ pos:usize, scope:usize }, JudgmentForm{ name:Atom, arity:Vec<Atom> }; re-export Rule from tsp-rules shape
parser.rs pub struct Parser<'a>{lex,peek:[Tok;2],scopes:Vec<Atom>}; parse_langdef(&mut)->Result<LangDef,ParseError>; parse_term(&mut,&LangDef)->Result<Term,ParseError>
lib.rs pub fn parse_lang(src:&str)->Result<LangDef,Vec<ParseError>>; pub fn parse_term(src:&str,l:&LangDef)->Result<Term,Vec<ParseError>>

Algorithms: Lexer is a byte-cursor DFA (keyword set via perfect-hash on first char then eq) emitting (Token,Span); BadBinder never raised here (lexer is token-level only). Pratt for object types: -> right-assoc bp 10, +(Sum) 20, *(Prod) 30, Ref/atom prefix, parens reset; for terms: application left-assoc (juxtaposition, bp 100), \/λ lambda prefix grabs Ident ':' Type '.' body. **LangDef parse** statement grammar (EBNF below): sort S; → sorts.push; grammar S ::= alt | alt ; with bind(n) annotations → Production+BinderAnn; judgment J : S * ... ; → JudgmentForm; rule Name: prem ... / concl ; → Rule{premises,conclusion} (/ separates premises above the line from conclusion). **Name→de Bruijn**: a scopes stack — entering a binder pushes the var name, the body resolves Var(scopes.rposition(name)), exit pops; an unbound ident at a Type position → UnknownSort, at term position w/ no binding → UnexpectedToken (no free vars in closed object terms). **Recovery**: on error, sync to next ;/EOF, push ParseError, continue; lib.rs returns Err(vec) if non-empty.

EBNF (metalanguage surface):

langdef    ::= { stmt } ;
stmt       ::= "sort" Ident ";"
             | "grammar" Ident "::=" alt { "|" alt } ";"
             | "judgment" Ident ":" sortseq ";"
             | "rule" Ident ":" { judg } "/" judg ";" ;
alt        ::= { sym } [ "bind" "(" Nat ")" ] ;
sym        ::= Ident | "(" sym ")" | terminal ;
sortseq    ::= Ident { "*" Ident } ;
judg       ::= Ident "(" termlist ")" ;          (* e.g. Has-Type(G, e, T) *)

Object-term surface (STLC core):

term ::= "\\" Ident ":" ty "." term | app ;
app  ::= app atom | atom ;
atom ::= Ident | "true" | "false" | "zero" | "succ" atom
       | "if" term "then" term "else" term | "(" term ")" ;
ty   ::= ty "->" ty | "Bool" | "Nat" | "(" ty ")" ;   (* -> right-assoc *)

2.3 Deliverable

parse_lang(stlc_src) → LangDef w/ sorts {Type,Term}, ≥1 grammar production per term/type former, judgment Has-Type(ctx,e,T), Step(e,e'), and the STLC rules as Rule data (T-Var,T-Abs,T-App,E-App1,E-App2,E-AppAbs). parse_term("\\x:Bool. x", &lang) → Lam(Type::Base("Bool"), Box::new(Term::Var(0))). Exposed via tsp-cli indirectly (Phase 0 smoke now sources its term from this parser).

2.4 Acceptance Criteria

metric target
stlc.lang parses Ok, sorts.len ≥2, rules.len ≥6, 0 errors
smoke term parse_term("\\x:Bool. x") alpha_eq Lam(Bool,Var 0) == true
right-assoc A->B->C parses as Arrow(A,Arrow(B,C))
app left-assoc f x y == App(App(f,x),y)
error span every ParseError.span.lo ≤ hi ≤ src.len(); points at offending token start
multi-error malformed file w/ 3 bad stmts yields exactly 3 ParseError, parser does not panic
throughput ≥5 MB/s lex+parse on object terms (criterion); LangDef parse < 1 ms for a 200-line .lang

2.5 Conformance Fixtures

fixture expected
tests/fixtures/stlc.lang Ok(LangDef); golden Debug snapshot of sorts/judgments/rule names
tests/fixtures/terms_ok.txt (one term/line) each → golden de Bruijn Term Debug
tests/fixtures/ty_assoc.txt A->B->C, (A->B)->C → distinct golden Types
bad_binder.lang Err, kind BadBinder (bind(5) past alt arity)
unknown_sort.lang Err, kind UnknownSort (grammar references undeclared sort Foo)
malformed_rule.lang Err, kind MalformedRule (missing / separator)
unexpected_tok.txt object "\\x. x" (no :Type) → Err kind UnexpectedToken, span at .
unbound.txt "\\x:Bool. y" → Err UnexpectedToken (free var y), span at y

Golden files checked into repo; cargo test diffs Debug output byte-for-byte.

2.6 Pitfalls

  • -> vs - > mis-lex (greedy longest-match ordering) → app/type parse explodes w/ cryptic span; assert lexer emits Arrow for "->" via a unit-tested token stream of A->B. de Bruijn off-by-one at binder entry/exit (scope pushed after vs before parsing the annotation type) → smoke term yields Var(1), silently wrong but type-checks later as a different program; fix by binding the name AFTER parsing :Type, BEFORE the body, mirroring tsp-term::shift cutoff semantics, and snapshot-test the literal index.
  • Pratt binding-power inversion (-> wrongly left-assoc) → A->B->C becomes Arrow(Arrow(A,B),C), type errors look like user bugs; caught by the ty_assoc.txt golden. Recovery infinite loop: syncing to ; when the current token IS the erroring ; makes 0 progress and hangs; always advance ≥1 token before re-entering the loop.
  • UnknownSort vs UnexpectedToken confusion: a typo sort in grammar must be UnknownSort (semantic), a stray ) must be UnexpectedToken (syntactic) — mixing lookup-failure with parse-failure is the root cause; fixtures assert the exact kind. Span byte vs char offset on multibyte λ (indexing &str[lo..hi] on a non-boundary) panics the error printer; spans are byte offsets, the printer uses char_indices/get(lo..hi).

2.7 Performance Concerns

  • Lexer single-pass, zero-copy &'a str slices for idents (no String alloc per token); peek buffer fixed [Token;2], no Vec churn.
  • Recursive-descent depth = expr nesting; deep (((...))) risks stack — cap nesting at 256, return UnexpectedToken("nesting too deep") (DoS guard, since CLI takes untrusted .lang).
  • Atom interned (string→u32) so LangDef comparisons/lookups are integer; intern table built once per parse_lang.
  • Target: 5 MB/s object-term parse; LangDef < 1 ms / 200 lines; allocations O(AST nodes), no quadratic re-scan.

2.8 Edge Cases

  • Empty file → Ok(LangDef{empty vecs}), not an error; trailing comment / no final newline → lexer EOF clean. Unicode λ and ASCII \ both lex to the lambda token (alias).
  • Shadowing \x:A. \x:B. x → inner x ⇒ Var(0), resolves to the nearest binder (scope-stack rposition); succ succ zero (no parens) → prefix succ grabs one atom each ⇒ Succ(Succ(Zero)).
  • Type-only juxtaposition A B (no constructor) → UnexpectedToken (no implicit type application in STLC core). Numeric literal 3 (if enabled) desugars to Succ(Succ(Succ(Zero))); out-of-range/huge literal → UnexpectedToken w/ "literal too large".

2.9 Failure Analysis

failure blast radius guard
Parser accepts malformed .lang (silent) Phase 3 rule engine gets garbage Rules → bogus type checker → unsound demo golden snapshots + every fixture asserts kind; fuzz §2.10
Wrong de Bruijn index emitted feeds tsp-term/tsp-rules; substitution lemma mirrors indices → masks a real off-by-one round-trip alpha_eq test; property: parse∘pretty == id
Panic on adversarial input CLI crash, no diagnostic parser returns Result only — #![forbid(unsafe)], no unwrap on input-derived data; fuzz target asserts no panic
Span corruption error printer panics on second-order failure byte-offset invariant lo≤hi≤len, codepoint-safe slicing

This crate is NOT in the TCB (the kernel is) — a parse bug yields a wrong language, caught downstream by Phase 11 tester / Phase 13 conformance, but never silently unsound proofs (those are the kernel's burden).

2.10 Operational Notes

  • cargo test -p tsp-syntax: unit (lexer token streams) + golden (fixtures) + property (parse(pretty(t)) ≡α t via proptest). cargo fuzz target fuzz_parse_lang + fuzz_parse_term: feed random bytes, assert never panics, errors well-formed; run in CI nightly, corpus checked in.
  • Pretty-printer (pretty.rs, dev-only, behind cfg(test)) round-trips terms for the property test — NOT a public surface.
  • ParseError::Display renders file:byte_lo: kind: msg; expected [..]; tsp-cli maps byte offset → line:col for humans.
  • Snapshot updates gated behind INSTA_UPDATE=1 (or manual review) so golden drift is an explicit, reviewed action.

15. Phase 3 — Inductive rule engine (type checker + derivation checker)

Crate tsp-rules. Two complementary views of the STLC typing relation Gamma |- e : T: (a) declarative — a set of Rule data + a Derivation tree validated by check_deriv; (b) algorithmic — the executable infer/check. The phase's load-bearing invariant: infer agrees with the typing relation (infer accepts e iff a valid Derivation exists). Depends on tsp-term (Phase 1) + tsp-syntax (Phase 2). step is Phase 4 — only its step: Term -> Option<Term> signature is stubbed here.

3.1 Objectives

  • Reify STLC typing as Rule data (T-Var, T-Abs, T-App, T-True, T-False, T-If, T-Zero, T-Succ) — premises + conclusion as Judgments over Term/Type.
  • Executable bidirectional checker: infer(ctx, e) -> Result<Type, TypeError> (synthesize) + check(ctx, e, T) -> Result<(), TypeError> (analyze) on de Bruijn Term.
  • Derivation tree + check_deriv(d, rules) -> Result<(), DerivError>: each node's (premises, conclusion) unifies against a named Rule, subtrees recurse, leaves close.
  • Agreement theorem (testable): infer(ctx,e)=Ok(T) ⟺ ∃ valid Derivation with conclusion Has-Type(ctx,e,T). Soundness + completeness of infer w.r.t. the rules.
  • Context = de Bruijn typing env (Vec<Type>, index 0 = innermost binder), threaded through binder crossings consistently with tsp-term::shift.

3.2 Detailed Implementation Steps

Theory anchors: B1 ch.9 (STLC typing rules T-Var/T-Abs/T-App/T-True/T-False/T-If — normative source for Rule data), B3 ch.6–7 (PFPL statics/dynamics vocabulary), P7 (bidirectional infer/check discipline — Pierce-Turner or Dunfield-Krishnaswami), ADR-10 (executable artifacts beside relations), ADR-12 (infer as untrusted oracle, not TCB), INV-5 (check_deriv sound + complete), INV-6 (infer agrees with typing relation).

File (tsp-rules/src/) Contents
lib.rs no_std+alloc (CLI re-exports under std); pub re-exports; depends tsp-term, tsp-syntax
judgment.rs Judgment { form: Atom, args: Vec<Term> }; Rule { name: Atom, premises: Vec<Judgment>, conclusion: Judgment }; builtin STLC rules() -> Vec<Rule>
context.rs Context(Vec<Type>); get(i)->Option<&Type>, push(T)->Context, len(); de Bruijn indexing (innermost = index 0)
infer.rs infer(ctx,e)->Result<Type,TypeError>; check(ctx,e,T)->Result<(),TypeError> (bidirectional)
deriv.rs Derivation { rule: Atom, subderivations: Vec<Derivation>, conclusion: Judgment }; check_deriv(d,&[Rule])->Result<(),DerivError>
error.rs TypeError, DerivError (variants below)
step.rs step(e: &Term) -> Option<Term> STUB (todo!) — body in Phase 4

infer (syntax-directed, total, no backtracking), one clause per typing rule (8.3): Var i looks up ctx.get(i), Err(UnboundVar) if out of range (T-Var). Lam(T1,body) pushes T1 and infers the body's type T2, yielding Arrow(T1,T2) (T-Abs). App(f,a) infers f's type, requires it to be an Arrow(T11,T12) (else Err(NotAFunction)), checks a against T11, and yields T12 (T-App). TmTrue/TmFalse yield Bool directly (T-True/T-False); Zero yields Nat (T-Zero); Succ(n) checks n against Nat and yields Nat (T-Succ). If(c,t,e) checks c against Bool, infers t's type Tt, checks e against Tt, and yields Tt (T-If). check(ctx,e,T) is infer followed by a structural type comparison, Err(Mismatch) on divergence.

Type has no binders → equality is structural == (derive PartialEq on Type). Var i uses ctx.get(i); Lam pushes its annotation T1 (no shift of ctx needed — de Bruijn typing env extends at index 0, existing entries' indices implicitly +1).

check_deriv algorithm: for node d: (1) look up Rule named d.rule in rules; Err(UnknownRule) if absent; (2) match d.conclusion against rule.conclusion and d.subderivations[i].conclusion against rule.premises[i], solving for the rule's metavariables (the schematic Term/Type/Context holes) — first occurrence binds, later occurrences must alpha_eq; (3) arity check subderivations.len() == premises.len(); (4) recurse on each subderivation; (5) leaves = rules with empty premises (T-Var, T-True, T-False, T-Zero) must have zero subtrees. Matching, not unification: conclusion is ground (built from concrete Terms), so metavars resolve by one-directional pattern match. Rule schemas store metavariables as distinguished Atoms / sentinel Var indices; instantiate(rule, subst) and match_judgment(pattern, ground) -> Option<Subst> are internal helpers in deriv.rs.

3.3 Deliverable

tsp-rules compiles (cargo build -p tsp-rules, no_std check passes). Three runnable capabilities, exercised by tsp typecheck (Phase 0 CLI wiring) + unit tests:

  1. infer types closed + open STLC terms; returns precise TypeError on ill-typed input.
  2. check_deriv validates a hand-built Derivation tree against rules(); rejects malformed trees with precise DerivError.
  3. Property test infer ⟺ check_deriv: for generated well-typed terms (Phase 11 Generator preview / hand fixtures), the infer-produced type has a check_deriv-valid Derivation, and vice-versa.

3.4 Acceptance Criteria

ID Criterion Target
A1 infer(empty, Lam(Bool, Var 0)) == Ok(Arrow(Bool,Bool)) exact
A2 infer(empty, App(Lam(Bool,Var 0), TmTrue)) == Ok(Bool) exact
A3 Ill-typed App(TmTrue, TmFalse) → Err(NotAFunction(Bool)) exact variant
A4 Unbound Var 0 in empty ctx → Err(UnboundVar{index:0,ctx_len:0}) exact
A5 check_deriv(valid_tree, &rules())==Ok(()) for ≥6 hand-built trees (one per rule) all pass
A6 Each malformed tree (3.5) → its expected DerivError variant exact match
A7 Agreement: ∀ 10k generated well-typed terms, infer=Ok(T) ⟺ valid Derivation(T) exists 0 disagreements
A8 infer total: never panics on any closed Term (fuzzed 100k) 0 panics
A9 infer is O(size of e) — single bottom-up pass, no exponential re-inference no If/App re-walk

3.5 Conformance Fixtures

tsp-rules/tests/fixtures/ — .term input + .expected (golden):

Fixture Input Expected
id_bool.term Lam(Bool, Var 0) infer: Bool -> Bool
app_id_true.term App(Lam(Bool,Var 0), TmTrue) infer: Bool
if_nat.term If(TmTrue, Zero, Succ(Zero)) infer: Nat
nested_lam.term Lam(Bool, Lam(Nat, Var 1)) infer: Bool -> Nat -> Bool
deriv_tabs.json Derivation for id_bool via T-Abs→T-Var check_deriv: Ok
bad_app.term App(TmTrue, TmFalse) TypeError::NotAFunction(Bool)
unbound.term Var 3 (empty ctx) TypeError::UnboundVar{index:3,ctx_len:0}
if_branch_mismatch.term If(TmTrue, Zero, TmFalse) TypeError::Mismatch{expected:Nat,found:Bool}

Malformed Derivation cases (each → named DerivError): deriv_wrong_arity.json (T-App with 1 subtree → ArityMismatch{rule:"T-App",expected:2,found:1}); deriv_unknown_rule.json (rule:"T-Foo" → UnknownRule("T-Foo")); deriv_concl_mismatch.json (subtree conclusion ≠ stated premise → PremiseMismatch{rule,index}); deriv_metavar_clash.json (T-App with T11 differing between premise-2 and the function's domain → MetavarConflict); deriv_leaf_has_subtree.json (T-Var with a child → ArityMismatch{rule:"T-Var",expected:0,found:1}).

3.6 Pitfalls

# Failure mode Root cause Debugging
P1 infer says well-typed but no Derivation validates (or vice-versa) infer deviates from the declarative rules (e.g. coerces, or T-If infers else instead of checking) A7 agreement test pinpoints the term; diff infer path vs rule premises
P2 check_deriv accepts an invalid tree metavar match too lax — later occurrence not re-checked, or args vec length unchecked assert occurrence-consistency uses alpha_eq; unit-test deriv_metavar_clash
P3 Wrong type for open terms / under binders Context indexing off-by-one — Lam must push so Var 0 = its own binder dump ctx at each Var; cross-check against tsp-term de Bruijn convention
P4 Mismatch reports types swapped check compares (found, expected) in wrong order; or structural == on Box<Type> not deref'd print both; assert expected is the check argument
P5 check_deriv infinite-loops / stack-overflows cyclic Derivation (a node referencing itself) — trees are owned + acyclic by construction, but a hand-built/parsed tree could nest pathologically deep depth guard + the recursion-limit op-note; reject > MAX_DERIV_DEPTH
P6 infer total but check panics check calls infer then compares — fine; panic means a Type variant (e.g. Sum/Ref) unhandled in == despite STLC-only scope exhaustive match on Type; #![deny(unreachable_patterns)]

3.7 Performance Concerns

  • infer: single bottom-up traversal, O(n) in term size n; each node O(1) work + O(depth of Type) for the one == at App/If/Succ. No memoization needed.
  • Avoid quadratic: If must check the else-branch against the inferred then-type (one walk each), never infer both then equate twice; App checks arg once.
  • Context as Vec<Type> with push → clone-on-extend; for deeply nested Lam use Rc<[Type]> or cons-list to avoid O(depth²) copies (note as future opt — STLC fixtures shallow, not on critical path).
  • check_deriv: O(nodes × premise-arity) match cost; metavar Subst is a small Vec<(Atom,Term)> (≤3 vars/rule) — linear lookup acceptable.
  • Targets: infer < 1 µs/node; check_deriv < 2 µs/node; full fixture suite < 50 ms; 100k-term fuzz < 30 s.

3.8 Edge Cases

  • Empty context + closed term — baseline; open term (free Var i, i < ctx.len()) — must resolve via ctx.get.
  • Var i with i >= ctx.len() → UnboundVar, not panic (de Bruijn index out of env range).
  • Maximally nested Lam (depth = stack-limit boundary) — recursion-depth guard shared with tsp-term.
  • If with then/else of Arrow type (higher-order branches) — equality recurses structurally.
  • Succ(TmTrue) — check fails Mismatch{expected:Nat,found:Bool}, not silent.
  • Derivation whose conclusion.form ≠ the rule's conclusion form (e.g. tagged T-App but conclusion is a step judgment) → FormMismatch.
  • Duplicate rule names in rules() slice — check_deriv uses first match; assert uniqueness at construction.

3.9 Failure Analysis

Failure Detection Containment / blast radius
infer unsound (types ill-typed term) A7 agreement + A8 fuzz High — feeds Phase 7 substitution lemma & Phase 11 generator; a bad infer corrupts every downstream soundness claim. Must be caught here
check_deriv unsound (validates bad tree) malformed-fixture suite (3.5) High — Phase 6 encodes Derivation→kernel proof term; a fake-valid tree would mint a false Proof. The kernel (Phase 5) is the backstop, but trust starts here
infer/check_deriv disagree on a corner A7 over 10k terms Medium — flags a rule/algorithm gap; fix before Phase 8 preservation depends on the agreement
Panic on adversarial Term/Derivation A8 + Phase 13 fuzz Medium — total functions required; a panic in infer aborts tsp typecheck

Distinct from kernel risk: tsp-rules is not the TCB — its bugs are caught by the agreement theorem + (later) the kernel re-checking proofs. The gravest project failure (kernel accepting an ill-typed proof term) lives in Phase 5, not here.

3.10 Operational Notes

  • MAX_DERIV_DEPTH const (default 1024) guards check_deriv recursion; configurable via tsp typecheck --max-depth.
  • Error display: TypeError/DerivError impl Display with the offending subterm + path (e.g. App.func), surfaced by tsp typecheck.
  • rules() is the canonical STLC rule set; Phase 10/12 extend it (products, sums, refs, System F) — keep Rule data-driven so extensions add rows, not match-arms in check_deriv.
  • Logging: infer/check_deriv take an optional trace flag printing the derivation as it's built/validated (debug aid for P1/P2).
  • TCB note: this crate is outside the trusted base; its correctness is established by test (agreement + fixtures) and re-validated when Phase 6 turns derivations into kernel-checked proof terms.

16. Phase 4 — Small-step operational semantics (the stepper)

Phase 3 gave infer/check (the typing relation Gamma |- e : T, executable) and check_deriv (validate a Derivation tree against Rule data). This phase delivers the other inductive relation soundness pairs with: the small-step reduction relation e --> e', realized as tsp_rules::step. Progress (## 21) and preservation (## 20) are statements about this relation; if step and the declared step Rules disagree, every later proof is meaningless. Scope: reduction (computation) rules, the value predicate, evaluation contexts / congruence (search) rules, the executable step, stuck-term detection, and (optional) a property-tested determinism lemma. STLC + booleans only here; naturals/products/sums arrive in ## 22.

4.1 Objectives

  • Implement pub fn step(e: &Term) -> Option<Term> in tsp-rules — call-by-value (CBV) small-step; Some(e') = one step taken, None = value-or-stuck. Define the reduction relation as two disjoint rule classes: computation (beta E-AppAbs, E-IfTrue, E-IfFalse) firing on redexes, and congruence/search (E-App1, E-App2, E-If) descending into a unique evaluation-context hole.
  • Pin the value predicate is_value (from tsp-term, Phase 1) as the single arbiter of "done": step never reduces under is_value and never recurses past a value's structure. Make stuck terms detectable + distinguishable from values: step(e)==None && !is_value(e) ⇒ stuck (the configuration progress forbids for well-typed closed e).
  • Keep step and the declared step Rules/check_deriv in lockstep: a separate step_relates(e,e') driver builds the Derivation and asserts check_deriv accepts it — the executable/relational bridge tested in 4.4. (Optional, property-tested) determinism: step is a partial function, so the --> relation is deterministic — at most one e'.

4.2 Detailed Implementation Steps

Theory anchors: B1 ch.3 + ch.9 (small-step E-AppAbs + evaluation contexts E[·] — normative source for step), B4 Part I (evaluation-context / congruence model → tsp-rules::step), P4 Wright-Felleisen (small-step is the form progress+preservation are stated over), ADR-11 (small-step CBV choice), INV-13 (step deterministic + type-respecting on closed terms), S1 (normative rule set for step including E-AppAbs, E-IfTrue, E-IfFalse).

Files (per section 10 layout), all in crate tsp-rules:

File Adds
tsp-rules/src/step.rs pub fn step(e:&Term)->Option<Term>; private reduce_head, EvalCtx, decompose/plug
tsp-rules/src/values.rs re-export tsp_term::is_value; is_stuck(e), is_normal(e) helpers
tsp-rules/src/eval.rs pub fn eval(e:&Term, fuel:u64)->EvalOutcome; multi-step driver
tsp-rules/src/step_rel.rs step_relates(e,e')->Result<Derivation,StepError>; the relational bridge
tsp-rules/src/lib.rs pub use step::step; pub use eval::{eval,EvalOutcome};

Core data structures: EvalCtx is a single-hole CBV evaluation context — Hole | AppL(Box<EvalCtx>,Term) | AppR(Term,Box<EvalCtx>) | IfC(Box<EvalCtx>,Term,Term) (AppR's left Term is already a value); EvalOutcome is Value(Term) (reached is_value within fuel) | Stuck(Term) (step==None && !is_value) | Diverged(Term) (fuel exhausted, last term kept for diagnostics).

step realizes one canonical decomposition with no nondeterminism, one case per rule of 8.4: at App(f,a), if f is not yet a value it steps f (E-App1 search); else if a is not yet a value it steps a (E-App2 search, f fixed as the already-reduced value); else, with both sides values, f must be a Lam to fire the beta redex (E-AppAbs) — any other value in function position (e.g. true applied to something) is stuck, yielding None. At If(c,t,e2), a non-value guard steps under E-If; a TmTrue/TmFalse guard selects t/e2 (E-IfTrue/E-IfFalse); any other guard shape is stuck. Var, Lam, TmTrue, TmFalse are already values (or a stray free variable), so step returns None without recursing into them. Beta uses the exact tsp-term de Bruijn discipline: substitute the argument at index 0 then shift the result down by 1 to close the consumed binder — subst/shift reused verbatim (no reimplementation) so the operational beta and the substitution-lemma beta (## 19) are byte-identical. Inference-rule presentation that the Rule data encodes:

        e1 --> e1'                    e2 --> e2'   (e1 value)
E-App1 ----------------      E-App2 -------------------------
       e1 e2 --> e1' e2              e1 e2 --> e1 e2'

E-AppAbs ----------------------------------   ( (lam:T. e12) is value, v2 is value )
         (lam:T. e12) v2 --> [v2/0] e12

E-IfTrue  if true  then e2 else e3 --> e2     E-IfFalse if false then e2 else e3 --> e3

          e1 --> e1'
E-If ------------------------------------------------------
     if e1 then e2 else e3 --> if e1' then e2 else e3

decompose(e)->Option<(EvalCtx, Term)> finds the unique redex + its context; plug(ctx,e')->Term refills. step MAY be specified context-first (decompose then reduce_head then plug) or fused (above); both MUST yield identical results — a prop_decompose_eq_step test enforces equivalence. step_relates re-derives the firing Rule name from the redex shape, builds the Derivation, and runs check_deriv (4.4 cross-check). eval loops step up to fuel, returning EvalOutcome.

4.3 Deliverable

cargo run -p tsp-cli -- step <prog.tm> (subcommand step from section 10) prints the CBV reduction trace e0 --> e1 --> ... --> vn (or ... STUCK at ek) for an object program, one term per line, terminating at a value, a stuck term, or fuel. Library-level: tsp_rules::{step, eval} plus is_stuck/is_normal, with step provably (by test) agreeing with check_deriv on the declared step Rules. The Phase 0 smoke goal step((lam:Bool.0) true) == Some(true) now runs through real step.

4.4 Acceptance Criteria

  • step((lam:Bool.0) true) == Some(TmTrue); step(TmTrue)==None && is_value(TmTrue); step(Lam(Bool,Var0))==None && is_value(..).
  • step(App(TmTrue, TmFalse)) == None && !is_value(App(TmTrue,TmFalse)) ⇒ classified is_stuck (non-function applied).
  • Relational agreement: for every fixture e with step(e)==Some(e'), step_relates(e,e') returns a Derivation that check_deriv(.., rules) accepts; for step(e)==None, no firing rule's conclusion unifies with e --> _.
  • Determinism (property): step is a function ⇒ trivially ≤1 result; the --> relation modeled by step_relates admits no second derivation with a different e' (10k generated terms, 0 violations).
  • Type-directed sanity (pre-soundness): on 10k well-typed closed terms from tsp-test::Generator, every eval(e,1000) ends in Value or Diverged, never Stuck (a Stuck here would pre-empt the progress proof — treated as a bug now).
  • eval is fuel-bounded: no fixture, however large, can hang the CLI; default fuel 100_000, override --fuel.

4.5 Conformance Fixtures

Under tsp-rules/tests/fixtures/step/; each .tm has a sibling .trace (expected, one term/line, last line VALUE|STUCK|FUEL). stuck_*/open_var.tm are ill-typed (infer rejects them) and exist only to test stuck-detection: step must still terminate with None, never a panic:

Fixture Input (sugar) Expected trace tail
beta_id.tm (lam:Bool.0) true true → VALUE
if_true.tm if true then false else true false → VALUE
if_cong.tm if ((lam:Bool.0) true) then false else true 2 steps → false VALUE
app2_order.tm (lam:Bool->Bool.0) ((lam:Bool.0) true) arg steps first → then beta → VALUE
nested_beta.tm (lam:Bool->Bool. 0 true) (lam:Bool.0) (lam:Bool.0) true → true VALUE
value_noop.tm lam:Bool.0 VALUE (zero steps)
stuck_app.tm true false STUCK (value, not Lam, in fn position)
stuck_if.tm if (lam:Bool.0) then true else false STUCK (guard is a Lam, not bool)
open_var.tm 0 (free index) STUCK per CBV (no closedness assumed at step level)
diverge.tm (lam:Bool.0 0) ...-shaped looper FUEL at --fuel 50

4.6 Pitfalls

  • Beta off-by-one (gravest): forgetting shift(arg,1,0) before subst at index 0, or shift(.,-1,0) after, frees/captures indices — beta_id yields a wrong-index Var and the substitution lemma (## 19) cannot be proved because operational beta ≠ logical [v/x]. Debug: dump pre/post indices; assert step of a closed redex stays closed.
  • Reducing under lambda breaks CBV and invalidates is_value(Lam ..) (symptom: value_noop takes a step; root cause: missing Lam=>None guard); catch via prop: is_value(e) ⇒ step(e)==None.
  • App2-before-App1 (eval-order drift): stepping the argument while the function is not yet a value contradicts the declared E-App1/E-App2 ordering and determinism can falsify (symptom: app2_order reordered); fix by checking !is_value(f) strictly before !is_value(a).
  • Stuck vs value conflation: returning None for both a stuck term and a value with no is_value check makes progress look vacuously true — always pair step(e)==None with is_value(e); is_stuck exists to keep them distinct.
  • Clone-induced index errors: If branch selection is a correct bare clone (branches are not lifted under a new binder); the temptation to "optimize" via shared subterms without re-shifting reintroduces capture bugs.
  • decompose/step divergence: maintaining context-first and fused code paths that drift when only one is edited; prop_decompose_eq_step runs both on every generated term as a CI-blocking guard.

4.7 Performance Concerns

  • step is O(spine size to the hole); full eval is O(steps × depth) — acceptable at textbook scale (< 10^3 nodes). subst/shift allocate a fresh Term per beta (cost ∝ body size); no in-place mutation (keeps step total and the borrow checker honest).
  • Targets: single step < 5 µs on terms ≤ 100 nodes; eval throughput ≥ 10^5 steps/s; determinism property (10k cases × ≤ 200 steps) completes < 2 s, gate at 5 s.
  • Avoid quadratic trace materialization in the CLI: stream each term to stdout rather than accumulating a Vec<Term> of the whole history unless --collect is passed.

4.8 Edge Cases

  • Value in function position that is not a Lam (true false) → stuck, None (ill-typed; stuck_app); an already-a-value input → None, zero steps, eval returns Value immediately.
  • Free de Bruijn index (open term) at top level → step returns None, flagged Stuck (step makes no closedness assumption; that premise lives in progress, ## 21, not here).
  • LOCKED left-to-right CBV (E-App1 before E-App2); an eager-vs-lazy variant is out of scope (NON-GOAL).
  • Diverging term under eval → Diverged(last) at the fuel boundary, never an infinite loop or stack overflow (fuel + non-recursive driver loop).
  • If with a value-but-non-boolean guard → stuck (stuck_if); only TmTrue/TmFalse select a branch.

4.9 Failure Analysis

Failure Class Detection Containment
Operational beta ≠ logical [v/x] (shift bug) Critical — silently dooms ## 19/## 20 prop: step-beta of closed redex == subst(..) differential test block phase; both reuse same tsp_term::subst
step disagrees with declared step Rules High step_relates+check_deriv on all fixtures CI gate 4.4; relation is source of truth
Well-typed term reaches Stuck High (pre-falsifies progress) 10k-term sweep, expect 0 Stuck investigate as typing/step mismatch before writing ## 21
Non-determinism (two e') Medium determinism property fix eval-order guards
Panic / stack overflow on adversarial term Medium (availability) fuzz step on random Terms; expect total Option, never panic iterative driver; pre-checked recursion depth
Trace OOM on diverging input Low --fuel + streaming output bounded fuel default

Note: this phase is pre-soundness — Stuck on a well-typed term is a bug to fix HERE, not a counterexample to report (that role belongs to tsp-test, ## 23, and the broken-rule demo, ## 24).

4.10 Operational Notes

  • CLI: tsp step <prog.tm> [--fuel N] [--collect] [--quiet]; exit 0=value, 2=stuck, 3=fuel-exhausted (machine-readable for conformance scripts). tsp step --explain annotates each line with the firing rule name via step_relates; default omits for speed.
  • Determinism property (prop_step_deterministic) runs in CI on every PR touching step.rs/values.rs/eval.rs; tsp-rules stays no_std + alloc — eval's fuel loop uses core iteration, no std collections beyond alloc::vec.
  • tsp-rules/README documents that step is the executable witness of the --> relation; the relation (Rule data + check_deriv) remains ground truth that ## 20/## 21 reason over, step validated against it, never the reverse.
  • No new public deps; step's public surface freezes after this phase — ## 22 extends it (naturals/products/sums) only by adding match arms + congruence contexts, never by changing the signature.

17. Phase 5 — The proof-kernel core (lambda-Pi / LF checker)

tsp-kernel is the TRUSTED computing base (TCB). Every machine-checked theorem in tsp-meta (Phases 7–10, 12) is valid iff infer_type/check_type accepts its proof term. A single soundness bug here silently validates a false theorem — the gravest failure class. The kernel is therefore small, total, audited, and dependency-free (no_std + alloc). Scope: KTerm (lambda-Pi: Var, Sort, Pi, Lam, App), Sort (Type : Kind), Ctx, bidirectional infer_type/check_type, whnf (weak-head normalization), equal (definitional equality — beta mandatory, eta optional/flagged). No inductives yet (that is Phase 6); this phase checks the bare lambda-Pi calculus.

5.1 Objectives

  • Define KTerm, Sort, Ctx exactly per spine; de Bruijn indices (usize), so kernel terms reuse the tsp-term shift discipline but with kernel-local total functions (no panics). Implement whnf(t) -> KTerm: beta-reduce only the head redex spine until the head is Var/Sort/Pi/Lam; never normalize under Pi/Lam bodies or in arguments.
  • Implement equal(ctx, a, b) -> bool: definitional equality via whnf + structural recursion; beta-conversion mandatory, eta for Lam/Pi behind a const flag ETA: bool. Implement bidirectional infer_type (synthesize) / check_type (analyze) per the lambda-Pi rules — the only trusted entry points; Type : Kind, Kind has no type (no Kind : ? rule) → predicative, no Type-in-Type.
  • Hold the TCB to a budget: <= 600 LOC in tsp-kernel/src/ (checker + whnf + equal + ctx + shift), zero unsafe, zero external deps, no recursion that can diverge on well-typed input.

5.2 Detailed Implementation Steps

Theory anchors: P2 Harper-Honsell-Plotkin LF (K-Var/K-Sort/K-Pi/K-Lam/K-App/K-Conv rules — normative kernel spec), P3 Löh-McBride-Swierstra LambdaPi (bidirectional infer/check/whnf/equal design — blueprint for TCB), P6 Coquand 1996 (whnf-driven conversion algorithm), P7 Pierce-Turner / Dunfield-Krishnaswami (bidirectional typing discipline), P8 Harper-Pfenning (correctness target for equal/whnf), ADR-04 (proof-term checking), ADR-05 (lambda-Pi calculus), ADR-06 (TCB size isolation), ADR-07 (bidirectional algorithm), ADR-08 (definitional equality via whnf), S4 (normative K-rules), INV-1 (kernel soundness), INV-2 (kernel totality), INV-11 (whnf/equal soundness), DP-1 (tiny TCB), DP-6 (bidirectional definitional equality), DP-15 (conversion terminates or fails closed).

Crate tsp-kernel (no_std + alloc), modules under tsp-kernel/src/:

File Contents (interfaces added this phase)
lib.rs #![no_std], extern crate alloc;, re-exports; crate-level KernelError; TCB audit comment block
term.rs enum KTerm, enum Sort; fn k_shift(t,d,cutoff), fn k_subst(t,j,s) (kernel-local de Bruijn ops; total)
ctx.rs struct Ctx(Vec<KTerm>); fn push(&self, ty)->Ctx, fn lookup(&self, i:usize)->Option<KTerm> (returns type shifted by i+1)
whnf.rs fn whnf(t:&KTerm)->KTerm — head-redex loop, beta only
equal.rs fn equal(ctx,a,b)->bool, helper fn equal_whnf(ctx,a,b); eta gated by const ETA: bool
check.rs fn infer_type(ctx,t)->Result<KTerm,KernelError>, fn check_type(ctx,t,ty)->Result<(),KernelError>
error.rs enum KernelError

Core types:

pub enum Sort { Type, Kind }                 // Type : Kind ; Kind is top (untyped)
pub enum KTerm {
    Var(usize),                              // de Bruijn
    Sort(Sort),
    Pi(Box<KTerm>, Box<KTerm>),              // dependent fn type:  (x:A) -> B ; A is dom, B binds x
    Lam(Box<KTerm>, Box<KTerm>),             // typed abstraction:  \x:A. body
    App(Box<KTerm>, Box<KTerm>),
}
pub struct Ctx(alloc::vec::Vec<KTerm>);      // index 0 = innermost binder
pub enum KernelError {
    UnboundVar(usize), NotAPi(KTerm), NotASort(KTerm),
    Mismatch { expected: KTerm, got: KTerm }, KindHasNoType, CannotInfer(KTerm),
}

whnf (head-spine, beta only) collects the application spine t = App(App(.. h, a_n) .. a_1) with head h; while h is a Lam and an argument remains, it fires the head redex (k_subst the popped argument into the Lam body) and repeats; once h is not a Lam, or the spine is exhausted, it stops and rebuilds App(h, remaining args) — the remaining arguments are left un-normalized (weak-head only). The bidirectional pair realizes S4 (8.6) directly, one clause per rule: Var i synthesizes the context entry at i (shifted by i+1, K-Var); Sort Type synthesizes Sort Kind and Sort Kind is untypeable (Err(KindHasNoType), no Kind : _); Pi A B requires both A and B (under A) to check as some sort, synthesizing the codomain's sort (K-Pi, PTS-style (s1,s2,s2)); Lam A b requires A to be a sort and synthesizes b's type B under A, yielding Pi A B (K-Lam); App f a requires f to synthesize a Pi A B (via whnf, else NotAPi), checks a against A, and yields the dependent substitution k_subst(B,0,a) (K-App). check_type(ctx,t,ty) synthesizes t's type and accepts iff it is equal (definitional, K-Conv) to ty, else Err(Mismatch).

Helper sort_of(ctx,t): match whnf(infer_type(ctx,t)) { Sort s => Ok(s), other => Err(NotASort(other)) }. check_type is the public verification call from tsp-meta, infer_type is its engine; Proof valid ⟺ check_type(empty, proof.term, proof.goal) == Ok(()).

5.3 Deliverable

tsp-kernel compiles no_std; a kernel test suite type-checks hand-written lambda-Pi proof terms (e.g. the polymorphic identity \A:Type. \x:A. x : (A:Type) -> A -> A) and rejects every ill-typed term in the negative corpus. tsp check --kernel-selftest runs the suite. TCB LOC counted and reported (<= 600). No panics on any input in the fuzz corpus (Phase 13 extends this; here a 10k-iteration smoke fuzz must pass clean).

5.4 Acceptance Criteria

# Criterion Measure
A1 Positive corpus checks all >= 20 well-typed terms: check_type == Ok(())
A2 Negative corpus rejected all >= 30 ill-typed terms: Err(_), never Ok
A3 Soundness invariant no input yields Ok for an ill-typed term (cross-checked vs reference)
A4 Totality 10k random/adversarial KTerms: zero panics, zero overflow, zero hang (per-call <= 50ms)
A5 Type : Kind, Kind untyped infer_type(Sort Kind) == Err(KindHasNoType)
A6 TCB budget tsp-kernel/src <= 600 LOC, unsafe count == 0, extern deps == 0
A7 Conversion equal accepts beta-equal terms, rejects beta-distinct; eta tests pass when ETA=true

5.5 Conformance Fixtures

Fixture Input (KTerm) Expected
kernel/pos/id_poly.kt \A:Type.\x:A. x Ok, type (A:Type)->(_:A)->A
kernel/pos/app_dep.kt (\A:Type.\x:A.x) Bool (Bool:Type in ctx) Ok, type Bool->Bool
kernel/pos/pi_type.kt / kernel/pos/eta.kt (A:Type)->A; \x:A. (f x) vs f Ok : Type; equal == true iff ETA=true
kernel/neg/self_app.kt / kernel/neg/apply_sort.kt \x:A. x x; Type x Err(NotAPi) (both)
kernel/neg/kind_ty.kt Sort Kind synth Err(KindHasNoType)
kernel/neg/dom_mismatch.kt (\x:Bool.x) tt where tt:Nat Err(Mismatch{expected:Bool,got:Nat})
kernel/neg/unbound.kt Var 7 in empty ctx Err(UnboundVar 7)
kernel/mal/whnf_loop.kt (\x.x x)(\x.x x) (untyped Ω) whnf MUST diverge → but infer_type rejects at \x.x x before reduction (A4 hang-guard)

Each fixture: a .kt s-expr term + a .expected line (OK <type> or ERR <KernelError variant>). Runner = tsp check --kernel-fixtures DIR; golden-compared.

5.6 Pitfalls

Pitfall Root cause Detection / fix
lookup returns unshifted type de Bruijn var i lives under i+1 binders added since it was pushed; its stored type must be k_shift(ty, i+1, 0) id on (x:A)->(y:B x)->... mistypes; unit test lookup-shift; assert infer_type(Var 0) of ctx=[A] == A shifted by 1
App rule forgets to substitute arg into B dependent codomain: App f a : B[a/0], not B dependent app yields wrong type but still Ok → silent unsoundness; fixture app_dep catches; never return B verbatim
equal compares before whnf beta-redexes look unequal; or normalizes too eagerly and diverges structural-then-whnf: compare heads after whnf, recurse on sub-terms; gate recursion on the reduced spine
whnf normalizes arguments / under binders full NF is non-terminating on open/ill-typed terms; whnf must be lazy A4 hang on whnf_loop; whnf touches ONLY the head redex; args stay frozen
Off-by-one in k_shift cutoff at Pi/Lam binder raises cutoff by 1; mirror tsp-term::shift exactly substitution lemma (Phase 7) breaks downstream; unit-test k_shift parity vs tsp-term::shift on shared shapes
Treating Kind as having a type (Type-in-Type) adding Sort Kind : Sort Kind for convenience makes kernel inconsistent (Girard's paradox) → accepts false props; A5 forbids; Kind is strictly top
infer_type(Lam) skips checking A is a sort accepting \x:foo. .. where foo is not a type ill-formed domains slip in; always sort_of(ctx,A) first

5.7 Performance Concerns

  • whnf is the hot path (called inside equal and every App/conversion). Use an explicit spine Vec<KTerm> + iterative loop, NOT deep recursion → no stack blowup on long application chains (>10k args). k_subst allocates; for proof terms this is acceptable (terms are small, < few KB) — avoid premature de-Bruijn-graph sharing, a Vec-backed KTerm tree keeps the TCB auditable, sharing/memoization is a post-audit optimization only.
  • Target: kernel check of a single tsp-meta lemma proof term <= 50 ms; full soundness proof suite (Phases 7–10) <= 2 s; proof-term size budget: preservation proof <= ~5k KTerm nodes. equal short-circuits with a pointer/structural-equal fast path before whnf; only reduce on mismatch.

5.8 Edge Cases

  • Empty Ctx: closed proof terms (the normal tsp-meta case) — lookup always None → UnboundVar. Var index == ctx.len() (exactly out of range) and very large index (usize::MAX) → UnboundVar, no overflow.
  • Pi A B where B does not use the bound var (non-dependent → ordinary arrow): App still substitutes (harmless, k_subst is identity on unused index). equal on two Sorts: Type==Type, Kind==Kind, Type!=Kind.
  • Eta with ETA=false: \x:A.(f x) and f must be UNequal (regression guard so eta isn't accidentally on). Deeply nested Pi/Lam (depth > 1000): iterative or guarded recursion; document max safe depth.

5.9 Failure Analysis

Failure Class Consequence Mitigation
Kernel returns Ok for ill-typed term UNSOUNDNESS (gravest) every theorem suspect negative corpus A2/A3 + Phase 13 fuzz + tiny audited TCB; reference re-check
whnf/equal diverges on well-typed input liveness proof checking hangs well-typed lambda-Pi is WN; A4 timeout flags any regression; whnf head-only
Panic / arithmetic overflow on adversarial KTerm robustness crash = DoS, not unsoundness total fns, checked index ops, #![deny(clippy::arithmetic_side_effects)]; 10k fuzz
KernelError too coarse diagnosability hard to debug failed proofs in Phase 7+ structured variants carry expected/got; pretty-printer in tsp-cli
TCB exceeds 600 LOC / grows deps trust erosion larger audit surface CI gate fails build on LOC/dep/unsafe regression

5.10 Operational Notes

  • TCB audit ritual: lib.rs carries a header listing the 6 trusted functions (infer_type, check_type, whnf, equal, k_shift, k_subst) and the LOC count; any PR touching tsp-kernel/src requires explicit reviewer sign-off + re-run of full positive+negative corpus. CI gates: cargo build --no-default-features (no_std), tsp check --kernel-selftest, tsp check --kernel-fixtures, LOC counter (tokei tsp-kernel/src <= 600), grep -c unsafe == 0, dep check (cargo tree -p tsp-kernel shows only core/alloc).
  • Flags: ETA is a const bool compile-time switch (default per spine: beta mandatory, eta optional); record chosen value in the LangDef conformance report. Logging: kernel itself is silent (no I/O in TCB); tsp-cli wraps results and renders KernelError with de-Bruijn-aware term printing.
  • This phase ships NO inductives and NO object-language encoding — that boundary (Phase 6) keeps the kernel general-purpose and the encoding outside the TCB, so encoding bugs are caught by the kernel rather than trusted.

18. Phase 6 — Propositions-as-types encoding of object judgments

Encode object judgments (Has-Type, Step) as kernel inductive signatures in tsp-meta over the tsp-kernel lambda-Pi core; derive their induction/elimination principles; compile a tsp-rules::Derivation into a KTerm whose infer_type result is the encoded KType of its conclusion. Bridge: tsp-rules data world → tsp-kernel proof-term world. NON-GOAL: proving the lemmas (Phase 7) — here only statability + derivation realizability.

6.1 Objectives

  • Define LF-style inductive signatures: each object type/term constructor, each judgment form, each inference Rule becomes a typed KTerm constant in an environment Sig (list of name:KType) — no new kernel primitives, Church/LF encoding only. Encode Type and Term as kernel codes: tm : Type (a KType of object terms), ty : Type, with constructors (arrow, lam, app, tt, ff, …) as Pi-typed constants.
  • Encode judgments as type families: Has-Type : Pi ctx. Pi tm. Pi ty. Type (an indexed proposition), Step : Pi tm. Pi tm. Type. Each Rule → one constant whose KType is the Pi-telescope of premises ⇒ conclusion (propositions-as-types: premise = hypothesis, Pi/Arrow = implication).
  • Emit the eliminator/induction principle Has-Type-rec / Step-rec as a derived constant (motive + per-rule methods) — consumed by Phase 7–9 proofs. encode_deriv: &Derivation -> KTerm produces a proof term; infer_type(&sig_ctx, &encode_deriv(d)) == encode_judgment(d.conclusion) is the phase's round-trip gate.

6.2 Detailed Implementation Steps

Theory anchors: P2 Harper-Honsell-Plotkin LF (judgments-as-types, rules-as-constants, derivations-as-terms — the adequacy obligation), P3 LambdaPi (Pi-telescope encoding of premises), ADR-09 (shallow LF judgments-as-types over deep embedding), ADR-04 (proof terms, not tactics), INV-7 (substitution-lemma proof term type-checks), INV-8 (preservation/progress proof terms type-check), DP-3 (propositions-as-types / Curry-Howard), R1 Twelf (reference for signature/constant style).

Crate tsp-meta, modules under src/:

File Adds Responsibility
tsp-meta/src/sig.rs Signature, Const{name:Atom, kty:KTerm}, build_signature(&LangDef)->Signature LangDef → LF signature; ordered so each const’s KType is well-formed in the prefix
tsp-meta/src/encode_ty.rs enc_type(&Type)->KTerm, enc_term(&Term)->KTerm object Type/Term → KTerm codes (de Bruijn preserved)
tsp-meta/src/encode_jdg.rs enc_judgment(&Judgment)->KTerm, enc_ctx(&Context)->KTerm Judgment → indexed KType (the proposition)
tsp-meta/src/encode_rule.rs enc_rule(&Rule)->Const, telescope(...) premises→conclusion as Pi-telescope
tsp-meta/src/elim.rs induction_principle(form:Atom,&[Rule])->Const motive + method telescope ⇒ Pi target. App(motive,…)
tsp-meta/src/deriv_term.rs encode_deriv(&Derivation,&Signature)->KTerm, sig_ctx(&Signature)->Ctx derivation tree → applicative proof term

Signatures / encodings (lambda-Pi shown as Pi/Lam/App, no Greek):

// sig.rs — LF signature is a context of named constants
pub struct Const { pub name: Atom, pub kty: KTerm }
pub struct Signature { pub consts: Vec<Const> }       // ordered, prefix-well-formed
pub fn build_signature(lang: &LangDef) -> Signature;  // sorts -> tm/ty; rules -> rule consts; + Has-Type/Step + eliminators
pub fn sig_ctx(sig: &Signature) -> Ctx;               // Signature -> kernel Ctx (de Bruijn) for infer_type
// LF base signature (codes), shown as name : KType
tm        : Type
ty        : Type
arrow     : Pi (_:ty). Pi (_:ty). ty          -- S -> T
lam       : Pi (_:ty). Pi (_:tm). tm          -- Lam(T, body); body uses de Bruijn 0
app       : Pi (_:tm). Pi (_:tm). tm
tt, ff    : tm
ctx       : Type                              -- typing context code
Has-Type  : Pi (_:ctx). Pi (_:tm). Pi (_:ty). Type    -- the proposition family
Step      : Pi (_:tm). Pi (_:tm). Type
// each inference rule -> one constant (premises = Pi-hyps, conclusion = result type)
// T-App :  G |- f : S->T    G |- a : S
//          ---------------------------
//                 G |- (app f a) : T
t_app : Pi (G:ctx). Pi (S:ty). Pi (T:ty). Pi (f:tm). Pi (a:tm).
          Pi (_: Has-Type G f (arrow S T)).
          Pi (_: Has-Type G a S).
            Has-Type G (app f a) T

Algorithms: enc_term/enc_type structurally map object de Bruijn index n → kernel Var(n) only inside binder-introducing constants (lam body lives under one kernel Pi/Lam); otherwise indices reference signature constants via sig_ctx offset — a two-zone de Bruijn discipline (signature-constant zone at fixed offset vs object-binder zone incremented per lam/Lam). enc_rule collects metavariables (ctx/types/terms) into outer Pis, each premise Judgment becomes Pi (_: enc_judgment(p)), and the conclusion is the tail, with metavars ordered by first occurrence (left-to-right premises then conclusion) so every Pi is in scope.

induction_principle(form, rules) builds Pi P:(telescope-of-indices -> Type). (Pi method_r. …) -> Pi indices. Pi (d: form indices). App(P, indices), one method per rule = its enc_rule telescope with each conclusion replaced by App(P,…) and each recursive premise replaced by an IH App(P, premise-indices). encode_deriv(d, sig) folds the tree into an App-spine of the rule constant applied to (a) metavariable witnesses recovered from d.conclusion.args (via enc_term/enc_type) then (b) recursively encode_deriv of each d.subderivations[i] for each premise hypothesis, yielding a closed KTerm; the validation hook then runs infer_type(&sig_ctx(sig), &term) and asserts equal(ctx, inferred, enc_judgment(&d.conclusion)).

6.3 Deliverable

tsp-meta exports build_signature, enc_type, enc_term, enc_judgment, enc_ctx, enc_rule, induction_principle, sig_ctx, encode_deriv. Given any LangDef accepted by Phase 2 and any Derivation accepted by Phase 3’s check_deriv, the encoded proof term type-checks in the kernel at the encoded conclusion. CLI: tsp check <lang> --encode-only prints the signature size, the STLC Has-Type/Step KTypes, and the eliminators; exits 0 iff every signature constant’s KType passes infer_type at Sort::Type/Sort::Kind.

6.4 Acceptance Criteria

  • build_signature(stlc) produces a Signature where every Const.kty satisfies check_type(prefix_ctx, kty, Sort(Kind)) (families) or Sort(Type) (term/proof types) — 100% pass, 0 kernel errors.
  • For all 200 check_deriv-valid STLC derivations in the fixture corpus: infer_type(sig_ctx, encode_deriv(d)) ≡ enc_judgment(d.conclusion) under equal — 200/200.
  • induction_principle("Has-Type", rules) for STLC type-checks at Sort(Kind); method count == rule count (5 for core STLC: var, lam, app, true, false… per LangDef). Round-trip stability: enc_term(t) then a kernel whnf then structural compare to enc_term(t) is identity for all values (no spurious redexes).
  • Signature build + 200 encodings + checks complete < 150 ms; encoded STLC Has-Type KType ≤ 40 KTerm nodes; a typical depth-6 derivation term ≤ 600 nodes.

6.5 Conformance Fixtures

File Input Expected
tsp-meta/fixtures/sig_stlc.lang core STLC LangDef signature: tm,ty,ctx + 6 ctors + Has-Type,Step + 2 eliminators; all kinds check
tsp-meta/fixtures/jdg_idfn.txt ` - Lam(Bool,Var 0) : Bool->Bool`
tsp-meta/fixtures/deriv_app.json T-App derivation tree encode_deriv golden KTerm; infer_type ≡ conclusion
tsp-meta/fixtures/elim_hastype.golden — pretty-printed Has-Type-rec KType
tsp-meta/fixtures/bad_arity_rule.lang / bad_open_meta.lang wrong arity for Has-Type; metavar absent from telescope MetaError::ArityMismatch; MetaError::UnboundMeta (const rejected before kernel)
tsp-meta/fixtures/bad_deriv_premise.json derivation w/ subderivation count ≠ rule premises encode_deriv → MetaError::PremiseMismatch (no malformed KTerm reaches kernel)
tsp-meta/fixtures/bad_index_term.json object term w/ free var beyond ctx enc_term ok but infer_type → KernelError::UnboundVar; surfaced as encoding failure

Each .golden is byte-compared; bad_* cases assert the exact error variant and a non-zero CLI exit.

6.6 Pitfalls

  • Two-zone de Bruijn skew (mixing signature-constant indices with object-binder indices off-by-one, from forgetting that entering a lam/Pi shifts the object zone but not the signature zone): symptom is infer_type reporting a constant where a bound var was expected; debug by diffing sig_ctx length against the Var(n) indices in enc_term. Premise/metavar ordering: a Pi referencing a metavar introduced later gives KernelError::UnboundVar at signature-build time (root cause: enc_rule ordering metavars by conclusion, not first-occurrence) — print the telescope and enforce a left-to-right premise scan.
  • Recursive IH omission in eliminator: induction_principle missing the App(P, sub-indices) IH for a recursive premise stalls Phase 7 inductions (no IH available), not caught here — assert method telescope arity == premises + recursive-premise IH count. Spurious redex in enc_term: emitting App(Lam …, arg) instead of the saturated constant makes the inferred type only match the goal after whnf, breaking the strict golden compare — encode object app via the app constant, never beta; debug via structural-equal pre/post whnf.
  • equal vs structural confusion: asserting inferred == goal syntactically rather than via equal(ctx,·,·) gives false negatives on eta/beta-equal indices — route all acceptance comparisons through kernel equal. Eliminator kind mismatch: motive P given Sort(Type) instead of an indexed Pi … Type fails check_type at Kind (root cause: dropping index Pis on the motive) — debug by printing P's expected KType.

6.7 Performance Concerns

  • Encoding is O(size of term/derivation); no kernel reduction during enc_* (reduction only in the final infer_type). Keep enc_* allocation-light: build KTerm bottom-up, reuse Box via small arena if profiling shows >5% in alloc.
  • infer_type over a depth-d derivation term: O(nodes) with whnf calls bounded by conversion checks at each rule application; targets — depth-6 ≤ 1 ms, depth-12 ≤ 8 ms.
  • sig_ctx rebuilt once per check, not per derivation; memoize enc_type for repeated object types (typing contexts repeat arrow/bool heavily) → ~30% fewer nodes on app-heavy corpora.
  • Eliminator KType grows linearly in rule count; cache induction_principle output per form (computed once, reused by Phases 7–10).

6.8 Edge Cases

  • Empty context enc_ctx(∅) → the empty : ctx constant; non-empty via extend : Pi ctx. Pi ty. ctx (cons); lone-variable derivation (T-Var) → App-spine bottoming at extend/var lookup proof. Closed term with no binders (tt, Zero) → constant of type tm, judgment fully ground, derivation term is a single rule constant applied to indices only (zero premise args).
  • Maximally nested lam (depth 64) → object zone index reaches 63; assert sig_ctx offset unaffected, no overflow (indices usize). Rule with zero premises (axiom, e.g. T-True) → telescope is metavars⇒conclusion, no hypothesis Pi; encode_deriv supplies only index witnesses.
  • Step judgment encoding parallels Has-Type but binary (tm tm); a beta-step derivation's term mentions subst only at the object level (Phase 1), never inside the kernel proof — the encoded step relation is opaque to tsp-term::subst. Multi-conclusion / non-Horn shapes → MetaError::NonHorn (LF encoding assumes single conclusion); rejected, not silently flattened.

6.9 Failure Analysis

  • GRAVEST (latent unsoundness seed): encode_deriv emits a KTerm the kernel accepts at a goal that does not equal enc_judgment(d.conclusion) — a true-looking but wrong proposition. Detected by the mandatory equal(inferred, enc_judgment(conclusion)) gate; never trust infer_type succeeding alone. This is the Phase-6 analogue of the project’s load-bearing TCB invariant.
