  • Signature mis-build (a Const.kty that happens to kind-check but encodes the wrong rule) → caught by golden .lang/.golden diffs, since the pretty-printed KType is byte-compared, not just kind-checked. encode_deriv partiality: any tree check_deriv rejected must also be rejected by encode_deriv (PremiseMismatch/UnboundMeta) before a KTerm reaches the kernel — fail closed, never hand the TCB a malformed term.
  • Eliminator unsoundness is impossible to introduce here (it is derived, then kernel-checked); a wrong eliminator simply fails check_type at Kind and aborts the phase.

6.10 Operational Notes

  • tsp check <lang> --encode-only --dump-sig writes the full Signature and eliminators to stdout (golden source); --dump-deriv <file.json> prints the encoded KTerm + its inferred KType. TCB boundary unchanged: tsp-meta is untrusted — it only constructs KTerms; soundness rests entirely on tsp-kernel::infer_type/equal re-checking them, so audit effort stays in the kernel (~Phase 5 budget), not here.
  • All MetaError variants carry the offending Atom/Rule name + a one-line LF-context for diagnostics, logged at warn by the CLI. Versioning: the LF base signature (tm,ty,ctx,Has-Type,Step + ctors) is frozen at this phase; Phase 10/12 extend it (nat/prod/sum/ref/System F) additively — never mutate existing constant KTypes, so earlier golden derivations stay valid.

19. Phase 7 — Structural lemmas: canonical forms, inversion, weakening, substitution

State the four structural metatheorems as kernel Props (KType) and machine-check each via a proof term (KTerm) that tsp-kernel::check_type accepts. Order is load-bearing: canonical_forms and inversion feed weakening feeds the substitution lemma — the crux, whose proof term mirrors tsp-term::subst/shift exactly. Substitution is the gating result; Phases 8–9 (preservation, progress) consume these as opaque lemma constants.

7.1 Objectives

  • Encode 4 lemma Props over the Phase 6 LF signatures (HasType, Step, Value); each Prop : KType, each proof term : Prop checked by the trusted kernel — no axioms beyond the signature constructors/eliminators.
  • canonical_forms: closed Value at type Bool → TmTrue|TmFalse; at S->T → Lam; at Nat → Zero|Succ. Inversion of Value + HasType together.
  • inversion: from HasType(G,e,T) for compound e, recover subterm typings + shape of T (one lemma per term former; STLC core only this phase).
  • weakening: G |- e:T ⟹ G,x:S |- shift(e,1,0):T. Proves index-shift commutes with typing — prerequisite for substitution.
  • substitution_lemma: G,x:S |- e:T ∧ G |- v:S ⟹ G |- subst(e,x,v):T. Proof structure isomorphic to subst's recursion; off-by-one in shift ⟹ proof fails to check (the dual guard with Phase 11 tester).
  • Lemma constants exported from tsp-meta as Proof { goal, term }, reusable by name in later phases.

7.2 Detailed Implementation Steps

Theory anchors: P1 de Bruijn 1972 (off-by-one in shift breaks the substitution lemma — the crux invariant), P4 Wright-Felleisen (canonical-forms + inversion lemmas used in progress proof), B1 ch.9 (TAPL substitution lemma statement), B2 StlcProp (lemma ordering: canonical-forms → inversion → weakening → substitution), ADR-12 (shift/subst total; lemma mirrors code), ADR-09 (LF eliminators from Phase 6 encode induction on derivations), INV-7 (substitution-lemma proof term type-checks), DP-5 (substitution lemma mirrors subst exactly), DP-4 (de Bruijn or capture bugs), R3 Software Foundations Coq dev (lemma decomposition order).

  • tsp-meta/src/lemmas/mod.rs — pub struct Proof { pub goal: KTerm, pub term: KTerm }; pub fn check(p:&Proof, sig:&Ctx) -> Result<(), KernelError> = check_type(sig, &p.term, &p.goal). LemmaLib { canonical:Vec<Proof>, inversion:Vec<Proof>, weakening:Proof, substitution:Proof }.
  • tsp-meta/src/lemmas/canonical.rs — fn canonical_forms(lang:&LangDef) -> Vec<Proof> (one Proof per base/arrow type). Goal built as nested Pi over the LF Value+HasType codes from Phase 6; proof term = eliminator application (case split) on the Value derivation, each branch returning the recovered shape witness.
  • tsp-meta/src/lemmas/inversion.rs — fn inversion(lang:&LangDef) -> Vec<Proof>; goals quantify Pi (d: HasType G e T). <conjunction of subterm typings>. Proof = apply the HasType eliminator (induction principle from §6) and project the relevant constructor's premise codes. Encode conjunction as iterated dependent pair (Sigma simulated by Church-encoded Pi-pair, per §6 propositions-as-types).
  • tsp-meta/src/lemmas/weakening.rs — fn weakening(lang:&LangDef) -> Proof. Goal: Pi G S e T (d:HasType G e T). HasType (cons S G) (Shift e 1 0) T. Proof by induction on d; Var case invokes the kernel-level lemma shift_var_lt/shift_var_ge (an LF function symbol whose computation matches tsp-term::shift's cutoff branch).
  • tsp-meta/src/lemmas/substitution.rs — fn substitution_lemma(lang:&LangDef) -> Proof. Goal: Pi G S T e v (de:HasType (cons S G) e T)(dv:HasType G v S). HasType G (Subst e 0 v) T. Proof by induction on de. Var-hit case (Var 0) uses dv; Var-miss case uses index decrement; Lam case applies weakening to dv then recurses under the binder with shift. The Subst/Shift LF function symbols MUST reduce (under whnf) identically to tsp-term::{subst,shift}.
  • tsp-meta/src/sigfns.rs — register LF computation rules for Shift,Subst as kernel definitional rewrites so equal discharges Subst (Var 0) 0 v ≡ v etc. (these are the load-bearing conversion steps).
  • Wire-up: tsp-cli check builds LemmaLib, runs check on each Proof through one shared Ctx (the LF signature), reports per-lemma PASS/FAIL with KernelError on failure.

7.3 Deliverable

Four lemma families produced by tsp-meta::{canonical_forms, inversion, weakening, substitution_lemma} as Proof values; every proof.term type-checks against its proof.goal under the Phase 6 signature Ctx via tsp-kernel::check_type. tsp check stlc.lang --lemmas prints canonical_forms: OK (n), inversion: OK (n), weakening: OK, substitution: OK and exits 0; any kernel rejection exits non-zero naming the lemma + KernelError. Substitution PASS is the phase gate.

7.4 Acceptance Criteria

Criterion Target
Lemmas checked canonical_forms (≥3 types), inversion (≥4 formers), weakening, substitution — all PASS
Trust only check_type validates; 0 assume/axiom constants beyond §6 signature
Substitution conversion equal discharges Subst/Shift redexes w/o unfolding limit hit
Kernel check time ≤ 150 ms total for STLC lemma set (release)
Proof-term size substitution term ≤ 4 KB serialized KTerm
Mirror invariant a deliberate ±1 in §6 Shift rule ⟹ substitution FAILS to check (negative test passes)
Determinism re-running tsp check --lemmas is byte-identical, exit-stable

7.5 Conformance Fixtures

Fixture Expected
fixtures/lemmas/canonical.expected canonical_forms: OK (3) for Bool, Nat, Arrow
fixtures/lemmas/inversion.expected inversion: OK (4) (App, If, Succ, Lam)
fixtures/lemmas/weakening.expected weakening: OK
fixtures/lemmas/substitution.expected substitution: OK + term hash
fixtures/lemmas/broken_shift.lang malformed: Shift cutoff off-by-one → substitution: FAIL (KernelError::Mismatch at Lam case)
fixtures/lemmas/missing_inv_premise.proof malformed: dropped premise projection → inversion: FAIL (KernelError::Unbound)
fixtures/lemmas/canonical_open_term.proof malformed: non-closed witness → canonical_forms: FAIL (free var index)

7.6 Pitfalls

  • Off-by-one in Shift/Subst LF rules — root: cutoff mismanaged crossing the Lam binder; symptom: substitution Lam case equal returns false ⟹ KernelError::Mismatch. Debug: dump whnf(Subst e 0 v) vs tsp-term::subst on same indices; they MUST coincide.
  • Weakening used at wrong index in Lam case — root: shifting dv by 1 at cutoff 0 omitted; symptom: bound-var type clash. Debug: print the Ctx at the recursive call.
  • Eliminator motive (return type) ill-formed — root: induction principle's motive not a closed Pi over the indexed family; symptom: KernelError::NotAType before any branch. Debug: infer_type the motive alone.
  • Conjunction encoding leak — Church-pair projector applied to wrong universe; symptom: Sort mismatch (Type vs Kind). Debug: check §6 pair encoding sort annotations.
  • whnf non-termination on Subst — root: Subst rewrite rule self-applies without index decrease; symptom: kernel loops / unfold-limit trip. Debug: assert each rewrite strictly shrinks term depth or index.
  • De Bruijn drift between tsp-term and LF Subst — two independent definitions silently diverge; root cause of false "proved". Mitigation: §7.10 cross-check test.

7.7 Performance Concerns

  • equal on Subst/Shift redexes dominates; memoize whnf per subterm — cuts substitution check ~3x. Cap unfold depth (e.g. 2^16) to convert loops into KernelError, never hangs.
  • Proof terms are eliminator-heavy ⟹ deep App spines; use iterative (not recursive) spine walk in infer_type to avoid stack growth (>10k depth).
  • Build all four lemmas against one shared Ctx (signature) to avoid re-checking signature well-formedness 4×.

7.8 Edge Cases

  • Empty context G = nil: weakening/substitution must still type-check (base of induction).
  • Var 0 substitution hit vs Var (n+1) miss — both branches exercised by fixtures.
  • Substitution under nested Lam (depth ≥2): shift accumulates; verify cutoff arithmetic.
  • Type T independent of the substituted var (no occurrence): proof must not require dv in result — still well-typed.
  • Canonical forms at Arrow with the value being an already-applied Lam (no further redex): Value witness still constructible.

7.9 Failure Analysis

  • Gravest: a kernel/Subst-rule bug makes substitution spuriously PASS while tsp-term::subst is wrong → every downstream theorem (preservation) is a false proof. Caught only by §7.10 cross-check + Phase 11/13 fuzzing. Treat any substitution change as TCB-adjacent.
  • Lemma FAIL is the safe failure (kernel rejecting) → exit non-zero, name lemma + KernelError variant + offending KTerm path.
  • Partial PASS (canonical/inversion OK, substitution FAIL) ⟹ block Phase 8; preservation cannot be attempted.

7.10 Operational Notes

  • Cross-check harness tsp-meta/tests/subst_mirror.rs: for N random closed terms, assert whnf(Subst code(e) 0 code(v)) == code(tsp-term::subst(e,0,v)) — guards the LF/tsp-term definitions against drift.
  • Export LemmaLib as named constants; Phases 8–9 reference weakening/substitution by name, never re-prove.
  • tsp check --lemmas --emit-terms dumps each KTerm proof for audit; record substitution term hash in substitution.expected for regression.
  • TCB audit reminder: this phase adds 0 lines to tsp-kernel; only tsp-meta proof-construction logic grows. Keep Subst/Shift LF rules in sigfns.rs ≤ 60 lines, reviewed alongside tsp-term::{shift,subst}.

20. Phase 8 — Preservation (subject reduction)

Capstone soundness half #1. State |- e : T /\ e --> e' => |- e' : T as a kernel KType (tsp-meta::preservation_statement), and supply a tsp-meta::Proof whose term : goal passes tsp-kernel::check_type. Proof by induction on the typing derivation; the load-bearing case is the beta step App(Lam(S,e12), v) --> [v/0]e12, discharged by inversion (recovers Gamma,x:S |- e12 : T and Gamma |- v : S) feeding the substitution lemma (Phase 7). Congruence cases recurse on subderivation IHs. STLC only here; extensions deferred to Phase 10/12. No new tsp-term/tsp-rules computation — this phase is pure metatheory assembled atop Phases 6–7.

8.1 Objectives

  • State preservation_statement(lang) -> KType: closed-typing HasType(e,T) /\ Step(e,e') => HasType(e',T), encoded via Phase-6 LF signatures (Pi over e e' T : Tm/Ty, Pi over proof args).
  • Build the proof KTerm by structural induction on the HasType derivation using the HasType eliminator (Phase 6); each typing rule = one branch.
  • Discharge beta/elimination cases with inversion + substitution (Phase 7 lemmas, applied as proof-term applications) -> closes the soundness crux.
  • Discharge congruence cases (If, App-arg/fun, Succ) by recursion on the relevant subderivation's IH (eliminator's motive carries it).
  • tsp-kernel::check_type(&Ctx::base(lang), &proof.term, &proof.goal) returns Ok(()); record proof-term node count + check time.

8.2 Detailed Implementation Steps

Theory anchors: P4 Wright-Felleisen §3 (preservation / subject reduction — the normative theorem statement), B1 ch.9 (STLC preservation proof by induction on typing derivation; beta case uses substitution lemma), B2 StlcProp.v Preservation (mechanized proof structure — lemma ordering, inversion on Step), ADR-04 (proof-term checking; no tactics), INV-8 (preservation_statement proof term type-checks), DP-5 (case_app beta case invokes substitution lemma → tsp-term::subst), S5 (normative preservation statement).

  • tsp-meta/src/statement.rs: preservation_statement(lang: &LangDef) -> KTerm. Builds the goal KType: Pi e:Tm. Pi T:Ty. Pi e':Tm. Pi _:HasType(nil,e,T). Pi _:Step(e,e'). HasType(nil,e',T). Reuses judgment_sig codes from Phase 6 (sig_has_type, sig_step). nil = empty Context constant encoded in the LF signature.
  • tsp-meta/src/preservation.rs: pub fn preservation_proof(lang: &LangDef) -> Proof. Constructs term: KTerm = nested Lam binding e,T,e',d_ty,d_step, then applies the HasType eliminator elim_has_type to d_ty with motive M := \e \T \d_ty. Pi e':Tm. Step(e,e') -> HasType(nil,e',T).
  • Branch builders (one fn per STLC typing rule, returning the eliminator's method KTerm):
    • case_var: Var is a value AND closed Var has no closed typing under nil -> Step premise is absurd; discharge by step_no_var lemma (Step(Var i, _) -> Empty) producing any HasType.
    • case_lam: Lam(S,e12) is a value; Step(Lam..,e') impossible at top -> step_no_lam (values don't step) closes it.
    • case_app: split on which Step rule fired (use elim_step on the Step proof): (a) E-App1 (fun steps) -> recurse via fun-subderivation IH, reassemble T-App; (b) E-App2 (arg steps, fun is value) -> recurse via arg-subderivation IH; (c) E-AppAbs (beta) -> inversion on T-App+T-Abs recovers Gamma,x:S|-e12:T & |-v:S, then substitution gives |-[v/0]e12:T. This is THE crux.
    • case_if: elim_step: E-If (guard steps) -> guard IH; E-IfTrue/E-IfFalse -> one branch typing already in T-If premises.
    • case_zero: value, step_no_value. case_succ: E-Succ congruence -> subderivation IH.
  • Proof assembly: Proof { goal: preservation_statement(lang), term: <above> }. Beta-redexes from eliminator application are left for whnf/equal during checking — do NOT pre-normalize (keeps term small + auditable).
  • Verification call site: tsp-cli check (Phase 13 hooks here) calls check_type(&ctx, &proof.term, &proof.goal).

8.3 Deliverable

tsp-meta::preservation_proof(&stlc_langdef()).term type-checks against .goal via tsp-kernel::check_type -> Ok(()). CLI: tsp check examples/stlc.lang --theorem preservation prints preservation: CHECKED (kernel-verified) + proof-term node count + ms. Reproducible from a clean checkout via make check-preservation.

8.4 Acceptance Criteria

Metric Target
check_type(ctx, proof.term, preservation_statement) Ok(()), deterministic
Beta case uses substitution lemma yes (grep proof DAG for substitution node)
Proof-term node count (STLC, 5 typing rules) <= 4000 KTerm nodes
Kernel check time (release, warm) < 50 ms
Peak kernel Ctx depth during check <= 64
Tamper test: swap inversion for identity check_type -> Err(KernelError::TypeMismatch)
Adding a bogus 6th branch / dropping a branch eliminator arity mismatch -> Err, never Ok

8.5 Conformance Fixtures

Fixture Expected
fixtures/meta/preservation_stlc.proof check_type = Ok(()); node count printed matches golden +/-0
fixtures/meta/preservation_goal.golden serialized preservation_statement KType (de Bruijn) — byte-exact
fixtures/meta/pres_beta_subderiv.txt trace: beta case resolves to substitution(d_body, d_arg)
fixtures/meta/pres_broken_no_inversion.proof (malformed) Err(TypeMismatch) — beta case closes with wrong typing
fixtures/meta/pres_missing_app_case.proof (malformed) Err(ArityMismatch) — eliminator under-applied
fixtures/meta/pres_swapped_motive.proof (malformed) Err(TypeMismatch) — motive omits Step premise

8.6 Pitfalls

  • Motive too weak (drops Step(e,e') quantifier) -> branches can't produce e'; check_type fails with opaque TypeMismatch deep in elim_has_type. Root cause: motive M must be \e\T\_. Pi e'. Step(e,e')->HasType(nil,e',T), not HasType(nil,e,T). Debug: print inferred motive type vs eliminator's expected.
  • Wrong induction target: inducting on Step instead of HasType leaves App typing un-inverted; some texts do step-induction but then need MORE inversion. LOCKED: induct on typing. Mixing both -> non-exhaustive branches.
  • Substitution shift off-by-one surfaces HERE not Phase 7: if tsp-term::subst/shift cutoff is wrong, substitution lemma's stated type mismatches [v/0]e12's actual de Bruijn form -> beta case equal check fails. Debug: whnf both sides, diff index-by-index.
  • E-AppAbs value-premise dropped: forgetting is_value(v) premise admits non-value args; the recovered |-v:S from inversion is then unjustified -> kernel rejects (no proof of Value(v)). Root cause: T-App+E-AppAbs premises must align.
  • Eliminator method order: branch KTerms must match the Phase-6 declared constructor order of HasType; transposing case_app/case_if -> each gets wrong expected type -> TypeMismatch. Use a named-record builder, not positional.
  • Pre-normalizing the proof term to "simplify" -> term blows up (eliminator betas expand), check time > target, audit harder. Leave redexes; whnf handles conversion lazily.

8.7 Performance Concerns

  • check_type cost dominated by equal/whnf calls at beta-case conversion ([v/0]e12 vs substitution-lemma codomain). Memoize whnf per subterm pointer -> avoid re-normalizing shared d_body.
  • Proof-term size grows ~linearly in #typing-rules (5 STLC -> ~3–4k nodes). Extensions (Phase 10) push toward ~10k; keep per-branch builders flat (no nested eliminator unless required) to bound depth <= 64.
  • Eliminator application creates N method-redexes; whnf is call-by-name so only the taken branch normalizes -> cost ~one branch, not all. Verify via redex-fire counter.

8.8 Edge Cases

  • Open terms: statement quantifies closed e (HasType(nil,..)); the Var case is vacuous via step_no_var. Do not state preservation for open terms — Gamma|- form deferred (needed only inside substitution, Phase 7).
  • Stuck-but-typed: cannot occur for STLC (progress, Phase 9 guarantees), but preservation alone must NOT assume non-stuck — it only constrains the case where a step EXISTS. Step is the hypothesis, never derived here.
  • Multi-step: single-step only; -->* (reflexive-transitive) corollary is Phase 10 (type soundness), proved by induction on the step count using this lemma.
  • If with non-bool guard: excluded by T-If's HasType(g,Bool) premise; inversion + canonical-forms (Phase 7) guarantee guard reduces to TmTrue/TmFalse, so only E-IfTrue/False/If fire.

8.9 Failure Analysis

Symptom Likely cause Diagnostic
check_type Err at top Lam goal/term binder count mismatch (5 expected) count leading Lam in proof.term vs Pi in goal
Err localized to case_app beta uses wrong substitution instance / missing inversion dump infer_type on the App-branch KTerm
equal returns false in beta case tsp-term::subst/shift off-by-one (Phase 1 regression) property-recheck shift/subst round-trips
Stack overflow in whnf during check non-normalizing proof term (accidental self-app) bound whnf fuel; report the diverging subterm
Ok(()) on a KNOWN-broken variant GRAVEST: kernel unsoundness (Phase 5 bug) run Phase 13 kernel fuzzer; freeze release

8.10 Operational Notes

  • TCB unchanged: this phase adds only tsp-meta proof construction; trust still bottoms out in tsp-kernel (Phase 5). preservation_proof is UNtrusted — it's checked.
  • make check-preservation is a CI gate; failure blocks merge. Golden files (*.golden) regenerated only via cargo run -p tsp-cli -- check --emit-golden, reviewed in diff.
  • Log on success: theorem name, kernel version hash, proof node count, check ms -> append to proof-ledger.jsonl for regression tracking of check-time creep.
  • Pairs with Phase 9 (Progress) to yield Phase 10's type soundness corollary; do not claim soundness from preservation alone.

21. Phase 9 — Progress

State |- e : T (closed) implies is_value(e) \/ exists e'. e --> e'; machine-check the proof term by induction on the typing derivation, using canonical_forms. This is the second soundness half (Phase 8 = preservation); together they yield the stuck-freedom corollary (Phase 10). Closedness Gamma = [] is load-bearing — open terms have stuck variable heads.

9.1 Objectives

  • Define progress_statement(lang) -> KTerm: Pi e:Tm. Pi T:Ty. Pi _:HasType(nil,e,T). Or(IsValue(e), Ex(Tm, \e'. Step(e,e'))) — closed only (ctx fixed to nil).
  • Encode IsValue : Tm -> Type, Or : Type -> Type -> Type (sum/inl,inr,case), Ex : Pi A:Type. (A->Type) -> Type (sigma/pack,unpack) as kernel inductive signatures (extend Phase 6 tsp-meta::encode).
  • Build the proof term progress_proof(lang) -> Proof by recursion on the typing-derivation structure; Proof.term : Proof.goal == progress_statement(lang) accepted by infer_type.
  • One case per STLC typing rule: T-Var (vacuous, ctx=nil → no var typeable), T-Abs/T-True/T-False/T-Zero (values → inl), T-App/T-If/T-Succ (step or recurse → inr), discharging E-cases via canonical_forms.
  • Reuse canonical_forms (Phase 7) at every elimination: App head of S->T is Lam (E-AppAbs fires); If guard of Bool is TmTrue/TmFalse (E-IfTrue/E-IfFalse).
  • Cross-check vs tsp-rules::step and tsp-test::check_progress on a shared closed-term corpus — proof, stepper, tester must agree (no disagreement allowed).

9.2 Detailed Implementation Steps

Theory anchors: P4 Wright-Felleisen §3 (progress theorem — "well-typed closed terms are either values or can step"), B1 ch.9 Theorem 9.3.5 (progress for STLC; canonical-forms lemma used at each elimination), B2 StlcProp.v Progress (mechanized case structure), INV-8 (progress_statement proof term type-checks), ADR-09 (induction via LF eliminator from Phase 6), DP-13 (exhaustive match = closed-world inversion; compiler enforces progress case completeness), S5 (normative progress statement).

  • tsp-meta/src/disjunction.rs (new): pub fn or_sig() -> Vec<(Atom,KTerm)> — Or:Type->Type->Type, inl:Pi A B. A->Or A B, inr:Pi A B. B->Or A B, eliminator or_elim:Pi A B C. (A->C)->(B->C)->Or A B->C. Encoded as Phase-6 LF constants.
  • tsp-meta/src/existential.rs (new): pub fn ex_sig() -> Vec<(Atom,KTerm)> — Ex:Pi A:Type.(A->Type)->Type, pack:Pi A P. Pi w:A. P w -> Ex A P, ex_elim (dependent). Used for exists e'. Step(e,e').
  • tsp-meta/src/progress.rs (new):
    • pub fn progress_statement(lang: &LangDef) -> KTerm — assembles the Pi-chain above; nil is the empty Context reflected as kernel Ctx.
    • fn prove_progress(d: &Derivation, lang: &LangDef) -> KTerm — case-split on d.rule, emits the case's proof KTerm.
    • pub fn progress_proof(lang: &LangDef) -> Proof — generic proof = nested or_elim/ex_elim driven by the typing derivation of the bound e; returns Proof{goal,term}.
  • Per-rule case construction (Atom dispatch on d.rule):
Typing rule Term shape Progress witness Lemma used
T-Var Var n, ctx=nil absurd (no nil-var) inversion (empty ctx)
T-Abs Lam(_,_) inl (v_lam) — (is_value axiom)
T-True/T-False TmTrue/TmFalse inl (v_bool) —
T-Zero Zero inl (v_zero) —
T-App App(e1,e2) IH e1: val→canonical_forms→Lam→inr(pack β); step→inr(pack E-App1) canonical_forms (Arrow), substitution-via-subst
T-If If(g,_,_) IH g: val→canonical_forms→true/false→inr(E-If*); step→inr(E-If) canonical_forms (Bool)
T-Succ Succ(e1) IH e1: val→inl(v_succ); step→inr(E-Succ) —
  • tsp-kernel: NO changes — infer_type/check_type/whnf/equal already total; Phase 9 only feeds it a larger KTerm. Confirm whnf reduces the or_elim/ex_elim redexes during conversion.
  • tsp-cli: tsp check <lang> --theorem progress routes to progress_proof, then infer_type(&Ctx::with_sigs(...), &proof.term); print proved: progress + proof-term node count on success.
  • tsp-meta/src/lib.rs: re-export progress_statement, progress_proof; register or_sig+ex_sig in the signature builder shared with Phase 8.

9.3 Deliverable

progress_proof(stlc) returns Proof{goal,term} where infer_type(ctx, &term) == Ok(goal) and equal(ctx, goal, progress_statement(stlc)); tsp check stlc.lang --theorem progress exits 0 printing proved: progress (kernel-checked, N nodes). Both soundness theorems (Phase 8 preservation + Phase 9 progress) now check against the SAME kernel.

9.4 Acceptance Criteria

  • infer_type accepts progress_proof(stlc).term at progress_statement(stlc); flipping inl↔inr in any case → KernelError (negative test).
  • All 8 STLC typing rules covered; an uncovered d.rule Atom → explicit MetaError::UnhandledRule, never silent inr.
  • T-Var case is provably vacuous (derivation in empty ctx impossible) — asserted, not skipped.
  • Proof-term node count <= 4000 for STLC; kernel check <= 25 ms (cold), <= 8 ms (warm); peak RSS < 64 MB.
  • Agreement gate: for 5000 generated closed well-typed terms (tsp-test::Generator), check_progress(e) == true AND step(e).is_some() == !is_value(e) for every e — zero mismatch.

9.5 Conformance Fixtures

Fixture Input Expected
progress_stlc.lang full STLC LangDef tsp check --theorem progress → exit 0, proved: progress
progress_term.golden progress_proof(stlc).term pretty-printed byte-stable golden (node count in header)
progress_value_inl.t Lam(Bool,Var 0) progress = inl (value), step = None
progress_step_inr.t App(Lam(Bool,Var 0),TmTrue) progress = inr (pack TmTrue E-AppAbs), step = Some(TmTrue)
progress_if_inr.t If(TmTrue, Zero, Succ Zero) inr (E-IfTrue), step = Some(Zero)
BROKEN progress_open.lang proof asserts progress for OPEN e (ctx not nil) kernel REJECTS — Var 0 neither value nor steps
BROKEN progress_flip.t inr returned for Lam (value) case kernel REJECTS — Step(Lam,_) underivable
MALFORMED progress_missing_app.rs omit T-App case MetaError::UnhandledRule(T-App) before kernel

9.6 Pitfalls

  • Open-term progress is FALSE: Var 0 is neither value nor reducible. Root cause: forgetting ctx=nil in the statement. Debug: instantiate goal at Var 0 — kernel can't build the Or; fix the Pi-chain to fix ctx to nil.
  • canonical_forms applied off the value path: invoking it before establishing is_value(head) gives a non-canonical head → wrong constructor assumed → equal fails at the Step premise. Debug: trace which IH branch (inl vs inr) feeds the lemma.
  • Ex witness/proof swap: pack expects (w:A, P w); supplying Step(e,e') where e':Tm is wanted (or vice-versa) → KernelError in pack arg 2. Root cause: Ex(Tm, \e'. Step(e,e')) arg order; confirm whnf of (\e'.Step(e,e')) e' = Step(e,e').
  • Stepper/proof divergence: proof says inr (steps) but step returns None (or reverse). Root cause: an E-rule encoded in tsp-meta that tsp-rules::step doesn't implement (or different reduction strategy). Debug: the 5000-term agreement gate isolates the offending term shape.
  • Succ non-value confusion: Succ(App(...)) is a value only if argument is a value (v_succ premise). Encoding Succ as unconditional value → false inl for Succ(non-value); the term actually steps via E-Succ. Debug: check is_value(Succ e) == is_value(e).
  • Shared-signature collision: Or/Ex Atoms clashing with Phase-8 Eq/connective names in the same Ctx → equal conflates props. Root cause: unscoped Atom interning; namespace meta-constants.

9.7 Performance Concerns

  • Proof term grows linearly in typing-rule count (8 cases) but each App/If case nests or_elim+ex_elim → constant blow-up; keep STLC proof <= 4000 nodes (preservation Phase 8 ≈ comparable).
  • Kernel cost dominated by whnf of or_elim/ex_elim redexes during conversion at each leaf; memoize whnf on KTerm ptr-identity to cut repeat normalization on shared subgoals (target warm check <= 8 ms).
  • Avoid eta-expanding \e'. Step(e,e') repeatedly — build the Ex predicate once per case, share by Box/Rc reference.
  • Agreement gate (5000 terms × check_progress + step) is the slow CI step (~ test throughput, Phase 11 target ≥ 10k terms/s); run in parallel, seed-pinned.

9.8 Edge Cases

  • Empty/nil ctx: T-Var case must be unreachable; assert no derivation, do not emit a bogus branch.
  • Nested redex App(App(Lam,_),_): progress picks OUTER inr via E-App1 congruence (leftmost-outermost); confirm choice matches step's strategy.
  • If with non-canonical guard that still steps (If(App(Lam.., true), a, b)): inr via E-If congruence, NOT E-IfTrue — guard not yet a value.
  • Succ Zero is a value (inl); Succ(App(...)) steps (inr) — boundary tested by progress_step_inr-style fixtures.
  • Maximal closed value Lam(All Bool, ...) (System F stretch): progress still inl; type-abstraction is a value.
  • Already-normal stuck-looking but typed term: by soundness none exists; a fixture asserting a stuck typed closed term must FAIL to typecheck (guards the theorem's contrapositive).

9.9 Failure Analysis

  • GRAVEST: kernel ACCEPTS progress_proof for a language where progress is actually false (e.g. open-term variant) → false theorem certified. Mitigation: the BROKEN progress_open.lang/progress_flip.t fixtures MUST be rejected; TCB audit (Phase 13 fuzz) is the backstop.
  • Proof checks but disagrees with tsp-test::check_progress → either proof or tester wrong; the dual-guard (kernel + Redex tester) must converge — a disagreement blocks the "done" gate.
  • MetaError::UnhandledRule at runtime for an extension rule (Phase 10 naturals/products) → progress not re-established for the extended calculus; expected until Phase 10 adds those cases.
  • KernelError::Conversion at an Ex/Or leaf → mis-encoded eliminator or wrong witness; bisect by checking each case proof in isolation (infer_type per-case lemma).

9.10 Operational Notes

  • tsp check <lang> --theorem progress and --theorem preservation share the kernel Ctx (Phase 8 sigs + or_sig+ex_sig); --theorem soundness (Phase 10) requires BOTH to pass.
  • Emit proof-term node count + kernel ms to stderr (TSP_LOG=info); golden progress_term.golden regen via tsp emit-proof progress --golden.
  • TCB unchanged this phase — tsp-kernel LOC delta = 0; record in the TCB-size ledger (kernel stays small + audited).
  • CI: progress proof check + the 5000-term agreement gate are blocking; cache the seeded corpus to keep the gate deterministic across runs.

22. Phase 10 — Type soundness corollary and language extension

Combine Phase 8 (Preservation) + Phase 9 (Progress) into the type soundness corollary — well-typed terms never get stuck — then extend the STLC object language (naturals, booleans, products, sums) and re-establish progress + preservation for the extended calculus. The corollary is the headline theorem; the extension proves the framework is not over-fit to bare STLC. All theorems are kernel-checked Proof terms in tsp-meta, accepted by tsp-kernel::check_type.

10.1 Objectives

  • Soundness corollary soundness_statement(lang) -> KTerm: |- e:T => !stuck(multistep e) — closed well-typed terms never reach a stuck (non-value, non-steppable) state. Compose preservation_statement + progress_statement over step* (reflexive-transitive closure).
  • Stuck-freedom as the operational reading: stuck e ⇔ !is_value(e) && step(e)==None; corollary ⇒ no reachable e_n is stuck.
  • Extend object language: Type::{Base(Nat),Base(Bool),Prod,Sum} + Term::{Zero,Succ,TmTrue,TmFalse,If,Pair,Fst,Snd,Inl,Inr,Case} already in spine; wire their typing Rules, step rules, is_value cases.
  • Re-prove canonical_forms, inversion, substitution, preservation, progress for the extended LangDef — additive new cases only, no rewrite of STLC cases.
  • Invariant: corollary proof term is derived (combinator over P0/P1 lemmas), not re-inducted — proof size grows O(constructors), not O(constructors²).

10.2 Detailed Implementation Steps

Theory anchors: P4 Wright-Felleisen (soundness corollary = progress + preservation + induction on multistep), B1 ch.11 (TAPL simple extensions — naturals, booleans, products, sums), ADR-11 (CBV step drives the corollary's multistep induction), INV-8 (soundness corollary proof term type-checks), DP-11 (bounded object-language scope; every extension re-proves both theorems before merging), DP-13 (exhaustive match on new constructors enforced at compile time).

  • tsp-meta/src/soundness.rs: pub fn soundness_statement(lang:&LangDef)->KTerm encodes forall e T. HasType([],e,T) -> forall e'. MultiStep(e,e') -> (Value(e') \/ exists e''. Step(e',e'')). MultiStep = LF-encoded reflexive-transitive closure of the Step signature from Phase 6 (multistep_refl, multistep_step constructors).
  • tsp-meta/src/soundness.rs: pub fn soundness_proof(lang:&LangDef)->Proof — proof term = induction on the MultiStep derivation: refl case ⇒ apply progress_proof; step case ⇒ apply preservation_proof then IH. Body is a KTerm::Lam/App tree composing the two named proofs; no new structural induction.
  • tsp-rules/src/typing.rs: add Rules T-Zero, T-Succ, T-True, T-False, T-If, T-Pair, T-Fst, T-Snd, T-Inl, T-Inr, T-Case. Each is Rule{ name, premises:Vec<Judgment>, conclusion:Judgment } over Has-Type form.
  • tsp-rules/src/step.rs: add congruence + reduction rules: E-Succ, E-IfTrue/E-IfFalse/E-If, E-PairBeta (Fst(Pair(v1,v2))-->v1), E-CaseInl/E-CaseInr (with subst), E-Fst/E-Snd/E-Pair1/E-Pair2/E-Inl/E-Inr/E-Case congruences. Extend step match arms; None only for true values/stuck.
  • tsp-term/src/lib.rs: extend is_value (Zero, Succ(v) where v numeric value, TmTrue, TmFalse, Pair(v,v), Inl(v), Inr(v)); extend shift/subst recursion over new constructors (mechanical — every binder-free node recurses, Case binds in its two branches so subst shifts there).
  • tsp-meta/src/lemmas.rs: add canonical_forms cases — Nat ⇒ Zero|Succ, Bool ⇒ TmTrue|TmFalse, Prod(S,T) ⇒ Pair, Sum(S,T) ⇒ Inl|Inr. Add matching inversion cases. substitution gains arms for new constructors (Case needs the shift-under-binder argument exactly mirroring tsp-term::subst).
  • tsp-cli: tsp check extended.lang runs soundness_proof(lang) through check_type; exit 0 ⇔ kernel accepts.

10.3 Deliverable

  • tsp-meta::soundness_proof(extended_lang) is a KTerm that tsp-kernel::check_type(&ctx, term, &soundness_statement) accepts; tsp check fixtures/extended.lang exits 0.
  • Extended LangDef (fixtures/extended.lang: STLC + Nat + Bool + Prod + Sum) with full typing + step rules; infer, step, is_value total over all constructors.
  • Kernel-checked progress + preservation for the extended calculus (new canonical-forms + inversion + substitution cases land).
  • Corollary proof term derived from P0/P1, not re-inducted; cargo test -p tsp-meta soundness green.

10.4 Acceptance Criteria

Criterion Target
check_type(soundness_statement(extended)) accepts in < 250 ms
Soundness proof-term size < 4× (progress + preservation) terms (derived, not re-inducted)
infer/step/is_value over extended Term total — no unreachable!/panic on any constructor
New canonical_forms cases one per added value type (Nat, Bool, Prod, Sum)
STLC proofs unchanged zero edits to existing Arrow cases — additive only
tsp check fixtures/extended.lang exit 0; --proof-size printed

10.5 Conformance Fixtures

File Expects
fixtures/extended.lang parses; soundness_proof checks; exit 0
fixtures/soundness/stuck_free.expect multistep((λx:Nat.Succ x) Zero) --> Succ Zero; final is_value=true
fixtures/soundness/case_subst.expect Case(Inl v, x.e1, y.e2) --> [v/x]e1; type preserved
fixtures/soundness/proof_size.txt derived term node-count vs P0+P1 sum (ratio < 4)
fixtures/broken/case_swap.lang E-CaseInl returns [v/y]e2 (wrong branch) ⇒ preservation proof rejected by kernel; exit ≠ 0
fixtures/broken/succ_value.lang Succ e deemed value w/o e numeric ⇒ progress/canonical-forms rejected
fixtures/malformed/sum_no_annot.lang Inl e without target Sum type ⇒ ParseErrorKind::MalformedRule

10.6 Pitfalls

  • Re-inducting soundness instead of composing P0/P1 ⇒ O(n²) proof blow-up + duplicated maintenance. Root cause: forgetting MultiStep is the only new induction target. Debug: assert proof-size ratio < 4 in CI.
  • Case substitution off-by-one: branch bodies bind a fresh variable, so subst/shift must increment cutoff under each branch. Root cause: copy-pasting the non-binding App arm. Symptom: substitution lemma fails to check, or E-CaseInl ill-typed. Debug: alpha_eq round-trip on subst against hand-computed term.
  • Numeric-value canonical form: Succ(t) is a value only if t is a numeric value; treating any Succ(_) as a value breaks progress (a stuck Succ((λ..) ) slips through). Debug: is_value(Succ(Lam..)) must be false.
  • Forgetting a congruence rule (e.g. E-Fst on a non-value pair) ⇒ progress false: a Fst(e) with reducible e is non-value and non-steppable = stuck. Root cause: only adding the beta rule, not the congruence. Debug: tester (Phase 11) shrinks to Fst(Fst(Pair(..))).
  • Inversion missing a new form ⇒ preservation case unprovable for that constructor; kernel error points at the missing eliminator, not the rule — read it as "no inversion lemma for T-Pair".
  • MultiStep encoding non-strictly-positive in LF ⇒ kernel rejects the inductive signature itself; ensure the recursive MultiStep arg sits in a strictly-positive position of multistep_step.

10.7 Performance Concerns

  • Corollary check cost = P0+P1 check + small composition layer ⇒ < 250 ms total; dominated by whnf on the larger extended HasType signature.
  • Each added type constructor adds O(1) canonical_forms/inversion/substitution cases ⇒ proof-term node count grows linearly in constructors; keep Box<KTerm> sharing to bound memory < 30 MB for the full extended proof.
  • equal/whnf calls scale with proof-term depth; Case/Sum rules deepen subst recursion — memoize whnf on repeated subterms if check time regresses > 2×.
  • infer over extended Term: still O(size × ctx-depth); products/sums add no super-linear cost.

10.8 Edge Cases

  • Empty Sum/Prod mismatch: Case on an Inl of wrong summand type ⇒ infer TypeError, never a runtime panic.
  • If with non-Bool guard: rejected by T-If premise; progress unaffected (term not well-typed, outside theorem hypothesis).
  • Nested values Pair(Pair(v,v), Inl v) ⇒ is_value recursion must bottom out; verify no infinite recursion on cyclic-looking (but de Bruijn-acyclic) terms.
  • Succ Zero vs Succ(λ..): former value, latter stuck-candidate caught by canonical-forms.
  • Zero-step soundness: e already a value ⇒ refl case ⇒ progress yields Value immediately; corollary holds trivially.
  • Case(Inl v, x.x, y.Zero): branch binds and uses Var 0 ⇒ exercises the binder-shift path of subst.

10.9 Failure Analysis

  • Kernel accepts a false extended soundness (gravest, TCB unsoundness) ⇒ a stuck term is silently "sound". Mitigation: fixtures/broken/* MUST be rejected; cross-check with Phase 11 tester (dual-guard). Detection: a broken-rule fixture that checks = P0/P5 regression.
  • Soundness proof checks but tester finds a counterexample ⇒ encoding mismatch between LF Step/HasType signature and executable step/infer. Resolve by re-deriving the signature from the same Rule set (single source of truth in tsp-rules).
  • Proof checks for STLC, fails for extension ⇒ a missing additive case (canonical/inversion/step); kernel error names the unfilled goal.
  • subst lemma fails only on Case ⇒ binder-shift bug; isolate via a unit proof of substitution restricted to Case before re-running full preservation.

10.10 Operational Notes

  • tsp check fixtures/extended.lang --proof-size prints node count of the soundness term + P0/P1 ratio (CI gate < 4).
  • tsp check --explain on rejection prints the kernel goal that failed + nearest Rule/lemma name — primary debugging surface for missing cases.
  • Lock fixtures/broken/case_swap.lang + succ_value.lang into CI as must-reject; a green check on them is a hard build failure (catches TCB regressions).
  • Audit boundary unchanged: extension touches tsp-rules/tsp-meta/tsp-term only; tsp-kernel (the TCB) is not modified — preserves the small-audited-base invariant.
  • Phase exit gate: extended soundness checks, STLC proofs untouched, all broken/ fixtures rejected, proof-size ratio < 4.

23. Phase 11 — Redex-style randomized testing harness

11.1 Objectives

  • Build tsp-test: a PLT-Redex-style falsifier that empirically corroborates the kernel-checked proofs by fast counterexample search. Target: 10^5+ well-typed Terms/sec, zero Pass failures on the sound STLC+extensions.
  • Ship a size-bounded Generator of well-typed Terms (typed-generation, not generate-then-filter — filtering wastes >99% at size>6).
  • Ship check_progress(e) -> bool and check_preservation(e) -> bool as oracles wrapping tsp-rules::infer / step, plus a Shrinker minimizing any CounterExample.
  • Dual-guard invariant: on the sound language tester yields only TestResult::Pass(n), agreeing with tsp-meta proofs; a broken rule (Phase 12) must be caught by BOTH layers. This phase establishes the agreement half (only-Pass); the catch half lands in Phase 12. Non-goal: tester does NOT replace proofs — fast empirical falsification only, refutes but never proves a universal.

11.2 Detailed Implementation Steps

Theory anchors: B4 Felleisen-Findler-Flatt PLT Redex (redex-check methodology — type-directed generation + counterexample shrinking), R4 PLT Redex (reference for Generator/Shrinker design), R5 proptest/quickcheck (shrinking strategy patterns, seed/replay ergonomics), ADR-13 (dual-guard assurance model), ADR-15 (type-directed size-bounded generator), ADR-16 (exactly the two soundness properties: check_progress/check_preservation), INV-9 (Generator emits only well-typed closed terms), INV-14 (Shrinker preserves the falsified property), DP-7 (prove AND test — dual guard), DP-9 (determinism: same seed → same verdict).

Crate tsp-test (depends on tsp-rules, transitively tsp-term); no_std + alloc core, std harness via feature std. Modules + files:

File Contents
tsp-test/src/gen.rs Generator, type-directed gen_well_typed, Rng
tsp-test/src/props.rs check_progress, check_preservation, TestResult, Witness
tsp-test/src/shrink.rs Shrinker, shrink_candidates, fixpoint loop
tsp-test/src/runner.rs run(seed, n, max_size) -> TestResult, seed logging
tsp-test/src/lib.rs re-exports; pub fn run(..)

Signatures (locked names; bodies omitted per RULE 10):

pub struct Generator { max_size: usize, rng: Rng, env: Vec<Type> } // env = de Bruijn var types in scope
pub enum TestResult { Pass(u64), CounterExample(Term, Witness) }    // u64 = # cases that passed
pub enum Witness { ProgressStuck(Term), PreservationLostType{ before: Type, after: Type, stepped: Term } }
pub fn check_progress(e: &Term) -> bool;       // is_value(e) || step(e).is_some(), for typeable closed e
pub fn check_preservation(e: &Term) -> bool;   // step(e)=Some(e') => infer(e')==infer(e)
fn gen_well_typed(g: &mut Generator, goal: &Type, fuel: usize) -> Term; // type-directed
pub struct Shrinker;  pub fn shrink(s: &Shrinker, e: &Term, fails: impl Fn(&Term)->bool) -> Term;
  • Rng: deterministic, reproducible — splitmix64/xorshift seeded by u64; NO std::rand/getrandom in core (must replay from a logged seed). Rng::next_u64, Rng::below(n).
  • Type-directed generation (gen_well_typed(goal, fuel)): recurse on goal + a coin between introduction and elimination:
    • goal=Arrow(S,T): intro → Lam(S, gen(T, env.push(S))).
    • goal=Base/Bool: intro → TmTrue|TmFalse (Bool), Zero|Succ(gen(Nat)) (Nat).
    • any goal: elim → pick arg type S (bounded), App(gen(Arrow(S,goal)), gen(S)); or If(gen(Bool), gen(goal), gen(goal)).
    • Var rule: scan env for de Bruijn i with env[i]==goal, emit Var(i) — sole leaf using context, closes terms by construction.
    • fuel=0 → force a leaf (intro/Var); guarantees termination.
  • Type sampling: random Type via gen_type(depth) bounded by max_ty_depth (default 4) to avoid Arrow blowup.
  • run: loop n cases; each gen_well_typed(gen_type(), max_size); assert with debug_assert!(infer(&e).is_ok()) (generator self-check); evaluate check_progress && check_preservation; first false → shrink → CounterExample; else Pass(n).
  • Shrinker (shrink.rs): greedy fixpoint. shrink_candidates(e) enumerates strictly-smaller, still-well-typed edits: (1) replace subterm by a canonical value of its type (Lam(_,b)→smallest closed value of T); (2) hoist a subterm of the goal type (App(f,a)→a if types match); (3) If(_,t,_)→t, If(_,_,e2)→e2; (4) Succ(n)→n. Keep first candidate where fails(&c) still holds; repeat until no candidate shrinks. Each candidate re-typechecked — never emit ill-typed shrinks.

11.3 Deliverable

cargo run -p tsp-cli -- test --seed S --cases 100000 --max-size 12 stlc.lang prints Pass(100000) on the sound language. tsp-test::run returns TestResult::Pass(n); a (future, Phase-12) broken rule returns a shrunk CounterExample(Term, Witness). Library API tsp_test::run/check_progress/check_preservation/shrink callable from conformance suite. Reproducible: same seed → identical case stream and (if any) counterexample.

11.4 Acceptance Criteria

Metric Target
Sound-language run, 10^6 cases, all seeds in CI matrix Pass(10^6), no CounterExample
Generator validity (infer(gen(T))==Ok(T)) 100% over 10^6 (debug_assert never trips)
Throughput (gen + both checks), max_size=12, release ≥ 1.0×10^5 cases/sec, single core
Determinism seed S replays byte-identical case sequence
Shrinker (on injected bug) terminates ≤ 200 iters, output size ≤ 8 nodes, still fails property
Memory, steady state < 16 MB (bounded by max_size, no leak across cases)

11.5 Conformance Fixtures

Fixture Input Expected
fx/test/sound_stlc.json seed=7, 50_000 cases, STLC Pass(50000)
fx/test/sound_ext.json seed=7, 50_000, +Nat/Bool/Prod/Sum Pass(50000)
fx/test/replay_seed.json seed=42 twice identical case-0..N hashes
fx/test/shrink_known.json hand-seeded stuck Term App(TmTrue, Zero) CounterExample, Witness::ProgressStuck, shrunk to App(TmTrue, Zero)
fx/test/gen_welltyped.json gen 10_000, assert each infer Ok all Ok, type matches goal
malformed fx/test/illtyped_inject.json feed ill-typed App(Zero, Zero) to check_progress oracle returns false; runner skips (guards infer().is_ok() precondition), logs skipped_illtyped
malformed fx/test/zero_fuel.json max_size=0 only leaf values generated; Pass, no panic

11.6 Pitfalls

Pitfall Root cause Debug
Generator emits ill-typed terms Var(i) chosen with env[i]≠goal, or de Bruijn index off-by-one after env.push debug_assert!(infer); diff env snapshot vs index at each Var leaf
Filter-then-keep is too slow (<10^3/s at size 10) generate-untyped + reject; rejection rate →100% with size switch to type-directed gen_well_typed; never use rejection sampling
Shrinker loops forever a shrink candidate is equal size or re-grows (If→branch reintroduces same node) enforce strict size(c) < size(e); fixpoint guard on size monotone-decreasing
Shrinker yields ill-typed minimal term candidate edit ignored typing (e.g. hoist a when a:S ≠ goal) re-run infer on every candidate; drop non-typeable
"Pass" but proofs disagree (false confidence) generator never reaches the offending rule's term shape (coverage gap) coverage histogram over rule names hit; require every rule ≥1 hit before trusting Pass
Counterexample non-reproducible Rng reseeded mid-run or used std entropy single seeded Rng, log seed first line; forbid getrandom in tsp-test

11.7 Performance Concerns

  • Cost per case ≈ gen + infer(e) + step + infer(e'). infer is O(size); keep max_size≤12 so ~10^5/s holds.
  • Allocation churn: Term is Box-heavy → each case allocs O(size) nodes. Reuse an arena/bump per case; drop between cases to bound RSS.
  • Type sampling depth dominates blowup: Arrow nesting multiplies subgoals; cap max_ty_depth=4.
  • Shrinker: each iter is O(candidates × infer); bound candidates per node-class; expected ≤200 iters → sub-second even on large counterexamples.
  • Parallelism (std harness): shard seeds across N threads, disjoint seed ranges, linear scaling; results merged (any CounterExample wins, lowest seed reported for determinism).

11.8 Edge Cases

  • max_size=0 / fuel=0 → emit only leaf values; must still satisfy progress trivially (Pass).
  • Goal Arrow(S,T) with no inhabitant reachable in fuel → generator forced to Lam intro (always inhabited); never deadlock.
  • Empty env, goal needs a Var → fall back to intro; closed-term invariant preserved (all generated terms closed).
  • Sum/Prod with one uninhabited side (e.g. Sum(empty, T)) — generator picks the inhabited injection; never the empty side.
  • Counterexample is already minimal (size 1) → shrinker returns input unchanged, no infinite loop.
  • Generated term that is already a value → check_progress true by is_value; check_preservation vacuous (step=None) → counts as pass.

11.9 Failure Analysis

Failure Severity Detection
Tester reports CounterExample on sound language critical — implies a bug in generator (ill-typed term) OR in infer/step (a real soundness bug the proofs should have caught too) cross-check: re-run kernel proof on the same Term's judgment; if proof checks, bug is in tester; else bug is genuine — fix tsp-rules
Tester Pass but kernel proof FAILS high — coverage gap; tester missed the shape rule-hit histogram; add directed generators for unhit rules
debug_assert!(infer) trips in generator high generator unsound; localize via env+index trace at the failing Var/App
Shrinker non-termination medium size-monotone guard + iter cap → emit "shrink incomplete" with last failing term, never hang
Non-deterministic CI flake medium seed logging; replay the exact seed offline

11.10 Operational Notes

  • CLI: tsp test --seed <u64> --cases <n> --max-size <k> [--max-ty-depth d] [--shrink/--no-shrink] [--threads t] <lang>. Exit 0 on Pass, exit 3 on CounterExample (printed Term + Witness + minimal seed).
  • Always log the seed as line 1 of output → any flake is replayable: tsp test --seed <logged>.
  • CI: nightly long run (10^7 cases, fixed seed-matrix); PR gate runs fx/test/* (~5×10^4 cases, <2s).
  • Coverage gate: fail the run if any inference rule has 0 hits (prevents silent "Pass" from a generator blind spot). TCB note: tsp-test is outside the trusted base — a tester bug can only yield false counterexamples (investigated) or missed ones (covered by the proofs), never a falsely-validated theorem; soundness rests on tsp-kernel, this layer is empirical insurance.

24. Phase 12 — Stress extension and the broken-rule demonstration

Stress the framework with a heavyweight extension (references + store + store typing), re-prove soundness, then plant a deliberately broken typing rule and show the dual guard fires: the kernel rejects the tsp-meta proof term AND tsp-test finds + shrinks a stuck counterexample. This is the project's thesis in one phase — proofs and property tests catch the same bug from two sides.

12.1 Objectives

  • Extend object language with references: Type::Ref(Box<Type>), terms Ref/Deref/Assign/Loc(usize)/TmUnit, a runtime store Vec<Term>, and store typing Sigma : Loc -> Type threaded through typing/step.
  • Re-establish preservation + progress for the references calculus in tsp-meta (store-typing weakening + the new alloc/deref/assign cases); kernel accepts every proof term.
  • Cross-validate: tsp-test Generator extended to references yields only TestResult::Pass on the sound calculus. Plant T-Deref-Broken (drops the Ref — types Deref e : Ref T instead of Deref e : T); prove (a) the preservation_statement proof term no longer check_types in the kernel, and (b) check_preservation returns CounterExample(Term, Witness) that the Shrinker minimizes.
  • Deliver the dual-guard artifact: one CLI run shows sound-extension green; one shows broken-rule red on both guards.

12.2 Detailed Implementation Steps

Theory anchors: B1 ch.13 (TAPL references + store typing — normative source for T-Ref/T-Deref/T-Assign/T-Loc and store-weakening lemma), ADR-13 (dual-guard assurance model), ADR-14 (broken-rule demonstration as a first-class CI artifact), ADR-16 (check_progress/check_preservation are exactly the two soundness properties), INV-10 (broken rule rejected by kernel AND falsified by tester), DP-7 (prove AND test — dual guard), DP-8 (broken rule must fail BOTH guards).

  • tsp-term::term — add Type::Ref(Box<Type>) (already in spine), Term::{Ref(Box<Term>), Deref(Box<Term>), Assign(Box<Term>,Box<Term>), Loc(usize), TmUnit}; extend shift/subst to recurse (locations are closed — Loc is shift/subst-invariant, the key invariant); is_value true for Loc,TmUnit,Lam,TmTrue,TmFalse,Zero,Succ(v).
  • tsp-rules::ty — Context gains store_ty: Vec<Type> (the Sigma); infer rules T-Ref/T-Deref/T-Assign/T-Loc/T-Unit; T-Loc reads store_ty[i].
  • tsp-rules::step — step becomes store-aware via new step_st(e: &Term, mu: &Store) -> Option<(Term, Store)> (Store = Vec<Term>); rules E-RefV (allocates → Loc(len)), E-DerefLoc, E-AssignLoc, plus congruences E-Ref/E-Deref/E-Assign1/E-Assign2; step (no-store) kept for the pure fragment.
  • tsp-rules::deriv — judgment forms Has-Type(Sigma, Gamma, e, T) and Step(mu, e, mu', e'); check_deriv validates store-typing premises.
  • tsp-kernel — unchanged (the TCB does not grow; only object-level signatures change). This is load-bearing: extension must not touch the trusted base.
  • tsp-meta::encode — kernel inductive sigs HasType : Store->Ctx->Tm->Ty->Type, Step : Store->Tm->Store->Tm->Type, StoreOk : Store->StoreTy->Type (every cell well-typed); eliminators auto-derived per Phase 6.
  • tsp-meta::lemmas — store_weakening (extending Sigma preserves typing — needed because E-RefV grows the store); update preservation_statement/progress_statement to quantify over mu, Sigma with StoreOk(mu, Sigma).
  • tsp-meta::broken — pub fn broken_deref_rule() -> Rule; (T-Deref-Broken), plus fn preservation_statement_broken(lang) -> KTerm whose proof term is the unmodified preservation proof — it must fail check_type at the broken HasType.
  • tsp-test::gen — Generator emits references terms (size-bounded, well-typed under a freshly grown Sigma); Shrinker deletes/replaces subterms toward minimal stuck witnesses.
  • tsp-cli — tsp check --rule-broken and tsp test --rule-broken flags select the broken LangDef; default selects sound.

Has-Type for references (inference-rule notation, no Greek):

Sigma; Gamma |- e : T              Sigma; Gamma |- e : Ref T
-------------------------- T-Ref   ------------------------- T-Deref (SOUND)
Sigma; Gamma |- Ref e : Ref T      Sigma; Gamma |- Deref e : T

Sigma(i) = T                       Sigma; Gamma |- e1:Ref T   Sigma; Gamma |- e2:T
------------------ T-Loc           --------------------------------------------- T-Assign
Sigma; G |- Loc i : Ref T          Sigma; Gamma |- Assign e1 e2 : Unit

         Sigma; Gamma |- e : Ref T
--------------------------------------- T-Deref-Broken  (PLANTED BUG: result Ref T, not T)
Sigma; Gamma |- Deref e : Ref T

Store-aware reduction (the alloc + deref steps):

mu' = mu, v        (i = |mu|)         mu(i) = v
----------------------------- E-RefV   ------------------------- E-DerefLoc
(mu, Ref v) --> (mu', Loc i)           (mu, Deref(Loc i)) --> (mu, v)

12.3 Deliverable

References calculus fully wired (tsp-term/tsp-rules), tsp-meta preservation+progress proofs check through tsp-kernel, tsp-test reports Pass on it. The dual-guard demo: tsp check --rule-broken exits non-zero with KernelError::TypeMismatch at the Deref case; tsp test --rule-broken prints a shrunk CounterExample (a stuck term). A make demo-dual-guard target runs both and asserts both fail in the expected way.

12.4 Acceptance Criteria

# Criterion Measure
1 Sound references soundness proven tsp check (refs lang) exit 0; kernel accepts preservation+progress proof terms
2 Tester agrees on sound lang tsp test --seed=0..99 → Pass(n) only, n ≥ 10000 terms
3 Kernel guard fires tsp check --rule-broken exit ≠ 0; KernelError::TypeMismatch cites HasType … Ref T vs T
4 Tester guard fires tsp test --rule-broken → CounterExample, found in < 2000 generated terms (median over 100 seeds)
5 Shrink minimal Shrunk witness ≤ 5 Term nodes (e.g. Deref(Ref TmUnit) class), deterministic per seed
6 TCB unchanged tsp-kernel LOC delta = 0 vs Phase 11; infer_type byte-identical
7 Both guards same bug Kernel failure case (Deref typing) and tester witness localize to T-Deref-Broken

12.5 Conformance Fixtures

File Input Expected
refs_sound.lang references LangDef, T-Deref sound tsp check → OK: preservation, progress (kernel-checked)
refs_proof.proof preservation+progress proof terms kernel exit 0
refs_test.json 10000 terms, seeds 0–99 Pass(10000) per seed
refs_deref_double.tm Deref(Ref TmUnit) sound: infer → Unit; step → TmUnit
refs_broken.lang T-Deref-Broken swapped in tsp check --rule-broken → KernelError::TypeMismatch
refs_broken.expected golden CLI stderr matches kernel msg exactly
refs_broken_cex.golden shrunk counterexample CounterExample(Deref(Ref TmUnit), Witness::PreservationFailed)
refs_malformed_locty.lang T-Loc with Sigma(i)=T but result T (not Ref T) check_deriv → DerivError; not a kernel false-accept
refs_dangling_loc.tm Deref(Loc 7) under empty store runtime stuck; tester Witness::Stuck; not typeable (no Sigma(7))
refs_assign_unit.tm Assign (Ref Zero) (Succ Zero) infer → Unit; step grows store

12.6 Pitfalls

  • Loc treated as a variable in shift/subst → indices drift across binders, breaking subst lemma silently. Root cause: copy-pasting the Var arm. Debug: assert shift(Loc i, d, c) == Loc i and subst(Loc i, j, s) == Loc i as unit invariants.
  • Forgetting store_weakening in the E-RefV preservation case → proof term fails to type at the grown Sigma; looks like a soundness bug but is a missing lemma. Debug: kernel error points at StoreOk(mu', Sigma') premise; supply weakening.
  • Broken rule "accidentally sound" via eta/whnf → equal(Ref T, T) must be false; if whnf over-reduces Ref, the kernel wrongly accepts. Root cause: treating Ref as a transparent definition. Debug: assert!(!equal(ctx, RefT, T)) before running the demo — if it passes the planted bug, the demo is vacuous.
  • Tester never reaches the broken Deref → Generator underweights Deref so the bug hides; criterion 4 fails. Debug: log production frequencies; bias Deref/Ref to ≥ 15% each.
  • Counterexample found but Shrinker diverges (shrink steps re-introduce the same large term, no measure decrease): enforce strictly-decreasing term_size per shrink step, cap at 64 iterations. Store typing checked structurally but store grows during step (Sigma/mu lengths diverge, T-Loc indexes OOB): assert mu.len() == Sigma.len() invariant in both step_st and infer.

12.7 Performance Concerns

  • Kernel check of references preservation proof: target < 80 ms (proof term ~2–4× STLC; store cases add ~600 KTerm nodes). Budget vs Phase 8 STLC ≤ 25 ms.
  • Tester throughput with references: ≥ 8000 well-typed terms/s single-thread (store alloc on each Ref adds copies; reuse a scratch Vec).
  • Broken-rule counterexample search: median < 30 ms to first CounterExample over 100 seeds; shrink < 5 ms.
  • step_st clones the store per step — for stress runs cap store size at 256 cells and term depth at 12 to bound O(store·steps) copying.

12.8 Edge Cases

  • Empty store + Deref(Loc 0): ill-typed (no Sigma(0)) — must be rejected by infer, never generated as "well-typed".
  • Aliasing: two Loc i to same cell; Assign then Deref observes update — preservation must hold across the mutation.
  • Ref (Ref TmUnit) → Ref(Ref Unit); nested store types; double Deref round-trips to Unit.
  • Cyclic store (a Loc cell holding its own Loc) — allowed by store typing, must not loop whnf (locations are values, not reduced).
  • Broken rule on a term with no Deref → tester returns Pass for that subspace; the demo must still surface the bug elsewhere (Generator must emit Deref). System-F alternative path: if All/TyVar chosen instead, parametric Lam over types — subst at the type level reuses the same shift discipline, and the planted bug becomes a bad T-TApp substitution.

12.9 Failure Analysis

  • Gravest: kernel accepts the broken proof (false-accept) → the whole dual-guard thesis collapses; treated as a P0 TCB regression. Mitigation: criterion 3 + the !equal(RefT,T) precheck; fuzz in Phase 13.
  • Kernel rejects the sound proof (false-reject) → blocks legitimate extension; usually a missing store_weakening or mis-encoded StoreOk. Localize via the cited premise in KernelError.
  • Tester Pass on broken lang (false-negative) → generation gap, not unsoundness; widen Generator distribution + raise term budget.
  • Tester CounterExample on sound lang (false-positive) → bug in check_preservation/step_st (store mishandling), NOT in the language; reproduce with the printed seed before touching rules. Kernel and tester disagreeing on which rule is broken is diagnostic ambiguity; require the Witness to carry the failing judgment so both guards name T-Deref-Broken.

12.10 Operational Notes

  • CLI surface: tsp check [--rule-broken], tsp test [--rule-broken] [--seed N] [--max-terms K]; make demo-dual-guard asserts both guards fire and is wired into CI as a required job.
  • Reproducibility: every CounterExample prints its RNG seed; tsp test --seed N replays bit-for-bit (deterministic Generator/Shrinker).
  • Keep refs_sound.lang and refs_broken.lang as a diff of one rule so reviewers see exactly the planted change; store both + the goldens under fixtures/refs/.
  • Audit gate: PR touching tsp-kernel in this phase is rejected by policy (TCB-LOC-delta check) — the extension is object-level only. Logging: RUST_LOG=tsp_meta=debug dumps the failing kernel subderivation, tsp_test=debug dumps the shrink trace; the demo script captures both into target/dual-guard/{kernel.log,tester.log}.

25. Phase 13 — Validation: conformance, kernel fuzzing, benchmarks

The "done" gate. Three independent guards close over the whole stack: (1) a conformance suite — every STLC + extension soundness proof checks, every known-broken language variant is rejected by both tsp-kernel and tsp-test, and the two agree on the sound language; (2) fuzzing — tsp-kernel::infer_type/check_type never accepts an ill-typed proof term and never panics on adversarial KTerms, plus tsp-syntax parser and tsp-term::subst/shift survive their input grammars; (3) benchmarks vs targets — kernel check time, proof-term size, test throughput, TCB line count. No new object-language or kernel semantics — this phase only exercises and measures Phases 1–12.

13.1 Objectives

  • Conformance: a deterministic, hermetic corpus driving tsp check / tsp test / tsp typecheck / tsp step; every soundness proof for STLC + the Phase 10 extension (nat, bool, prod, sum) checks; every broken variant is dual-rejected (kernel proof fails to check and tester yields a shrunk counterexample).
  • Kernel fuzzing: the load-bearing invariant — tsp-kernel never accepts an ill-typed proof term (false-theorem soundness bug) and never panics/OOMs/diverges on adversarial KTerms, inside a fixed CI budget.
  • Differential agreement: tsp-test::check_progress/check_preservation agree with the kernel-checked Preservation/Progress on the sound language (no Pass where a proof would fail; no CounterExample where the proof holds).
  • Benchmarks + TCB report: publish kernel infer_type ns/proof-node, proof-term node counts, tsp test terms/s, and a machine-counted TCB line total with a regression gate.
  • Coverage: every Rule in the sound LangDef, every KTerm constructor in infer_type, every whnf/equal branch hit by the corpus.

13.2 Detailed Implementation Steps

Theory anchors: ADR-19 (cargo-fuzz targets on kernel — no panic, no false accept), ADR-20 (criterion micro-benchmarks with regression gates), ADR-23 (CI pipeline: conformance + fuzz-smoke + bench regression), P5 POPLmark (conformance yardstick for STLC/System F subset), INV-1 (kernel soundness — fuzz gate), INV-2 (kernel totality — fuzz gate), INV-10 (broken-rule dual-guard — conformance gate), DP-1 (TCB ≤800 LoC — CI gate), DP-7 (dual guard verified end-to-end).

Files live in existing crates (no new workspace member) plus a top-level conformance/ corpus and benches/.

Artifact Path Role
Conformance driver tsp-cli/tests/conformance.rs walks conformance/, runs check/test/typecheck/step, diffs against .expected
Corpus conformance/{sound,broken,parse,step}/*.lang,*.proof,*.tm,*.expected locked fixtures (§13.5)
Kernel fuzz target tsp-kernel/fuzz/fuzz_targets/kernel_check.rs infer_type/check_type on arbitrary KTerm (libFuzzer/cargo-fuzz)
Roundtrip fuzz target tsp-kernel/fuzz/fuzz_targets/kernel_roundtrip.rs well-formed KTerm generator → infer_type must Ok; whnf idempotence
Parser fuzz target tsp-syntax/fuzz/fuzz_targets/parse.rs Parser on arbitrary bytes → never panic; parse∘print roundtrip
Subst/shift fuzz target tsp-term/fuzz/fuzz_targets/subst.rs shift/subst/alpha_eq algebraic laws (§13.8)
Proptest suite tsp-test/tests/agreement.rs differential: tester vs kernel-checked theorems
Benches benches/{kernel,testgen,parse}.rs Criterion harness
Reports target/tcb-report.json, target/bench-report.json committed artifacts + CI diff gate

Driver conformance.rs: enumerate corpus, dispatch by subdir; sound/ → tsp check must exit 0 + tsp test --iters N must Pass(N); broken/ → tsp check must exit nonzero with KernelError::TypeMismatch/NotAFunction and tsp test must report CounterExample(e, w) with the expected shrunk term in .expected; parse/ → exact ParseErrorKind; step/ → exact reduction trace. All comparisons byte-exact on canonical pretty-print (de Bruijn form, no name choice → deterministic).

Kernel fuzz harness (kernel_check.rs): the fuzz_target feeds an arbitrary KTerm to infer_type(&Ctx::empty(), &t); whenever inference synthesizes a type ty, the harness re-runs check_type(&Ctx::empty(), &t, &ty) and asserts it also accepts — a libFuzzer/cargo-fuzz target, not a reference implementation, so no independent oracle is required.

Properties enforced by fuzz targets (oracle-style, no reference checker needed): re-check law infer_type(ctx,t)=Ok(ty) ⇒ check_type(ctx,t,ty)=Ok(()) (internal consistency of the bidirectional pair); whnf idempotence + preservation equal(ctx, t, whnf(t)) and whnf(whnf(t))==whnf(t) syntactically; equal reflexive/symmetric on inferred terms (transitivity spot-checked on triples); no-panic / bounded via a depth-capped Arbitrary impl for KTerm (max depth 24, max index 64), with a recursion-depth guard converting deep terms to KernelError::DepthLimit rather than stack overflow.

Differential agreement.rs: Generator (Phase 11) produces N=10⁵ size-≤k well-typed Term; for each, assert check_preservation(e)==true and check_progress(e)==true; cross-check: build the per-term Preservation Proof via tsp-meta::preservation_statement instantiated at e and assert kernel check_type Ok — i.e. the tester's Pass is witnessed by a kernel proof on the sound language.

Bench harness (Criterion): kernel.rs measures infer_type on the Progress + Preservation proof terms (largest = extended-language Preservation) reporting ns/proof-node; testgen.rs measures Generator + check_* throughput (terms/s); parse.rs measures LangDef + object-term parse. xtask tcb-report runs tokei/scc over tsp-kernel/src/** (Var/Sort/Pi/Lam/App, infer_type,check_type,whnf,equal,Ctx) emitting tcb-report.json.

13.3 Deliverable

cargo test -p tsp-cli --test conformance green; cargo fuzz run kernel_check (+ roundtrip, parse, subst) clean for the CI budget (≥ 5 min each, zero crashes/timeouts/OOM, zero false-acceptances); cargo bench produces bench-report.json; xtask tcb-report produces tcb-report.json. CI job validate aggregates all four and fails on any regression past threshold. This is the project's terminal acceptance gate.

Metric Target How measured

Kernel false-acceptances 0 (an ill-typed KTerm accepted = build-blocking) fuzz re-check law + curated ill-typed corpus
Kernel panics/OOM/timeout under fuzz 0 over ≥ 5 min / target / arch cargo fuzz, ASan + libFuzzer -max_total_time
Tester↔kernel agreement 0 disagreements over 10⁵ terms agreement.rs

Proof-term size Preservation ≤ 40k KTerm nodes; Progress ≤ 15k; substitution lemma ≤ 20k node counter

TCB line count tsp-kernel/src ≤ 1200 SLOC; regression gate ±5% tcb-report.json
Branch coverage of TCB ≥ 95% of infer_type/check_type/whnf/equal branches cargo llvm-cov over corpus

sound/stlc.test.expected tsp test --iters 100000 sound/stlc.lang Pass(100000)
broken/preservation_bad_app.lang T-App drops domain check (subst lemma breaks) tsp check → KernelError::TypeMismatch exit 2; tsp test → CounterExample((λx:Bool.x) 0, PreservationFailed) after shrink
broken/progress_missing_succ_val.lang is_value omits Succ, removes E-Succ congruence tsp check → proof fails (NotAFunction/stuck); tsp test → CounterExample(Succ 0, ProgressFailed:stuck)

Every .expected is byte-exact against canonical pretty-print; fixtures are seed-pinned (--seed 0) so tsp test counterexamples are reproducible.

13.6 Pitfalls

  • Fuzz oracle gap — without a reference checker, a too-weak oracle (only "no panic") misses false-acceptances. Root cause: silent unsoundness has no crash. Mitigation: the re-check law + a curated ill-typed corpus that fuzzing mutates; treat any Ok on a known-ill-typed mutant as a hard failure. Debug: minimize with cargo fuzz tmin, print the KTerm + the accepting infer_type path.
  • Non-deterministic counterexamples — unseeded Generator/Shrinker makes broken/*.expected flaky. Root cause: RNG + nondeterministic shrink order. Fix: pin --seed, make Shrinker order total/deterministic; assert the minimal term, not the first found.
  • Coverage illusion from trivial proofs (corpus checks proofs but never exercises whnf/equal conversion branches because proofs are built already-reduced): include fixtures requiring beta/eta conversion at check_type; gate on llvm-cov branch %, not line %.
  • Pretty-print drift (byte-exact diffs break when the de Bruijn printer changes spacing): freeze + unit-test the canonical printer; conformance diffs the structural AST when the printer version bumps. Stack overflow ≠ caught error (adversarial deep KTerm overflows the native stack before DepthLimit fires): thread a depth counter through infer_type/whnf, fuzz with small stack (RUST_MIN_STACK), CI under a 64 MB stack.
  • Bench measuring the wrong thing (Criterion times include LangDef parse or proof construction, not kernel infer_type): pre-build the KTerm in iter_batched setup so only infer_type is in the timed routine. equal non-termination on ill-formed input (definitional equality loops on a cyclic/ill-typed KTerm reached via mutation, since whnf/equal assume well-formedness): a fuel/step budget in equal turns exceeding it into KernelError::ConvBudget, never a hang.

13.7 Performance Concerns

  • Kernel check time dominated by whnf + equal during conversion; memoize whnf per subterm within one infer_type call (do not cache across calls — TCB simplicity > speed). Target ≤ 400 ns/proof-node holds with de Bruijn (no name lookups).
  • Proof-term size is the real cost driver (check time ∝ nodes); extended-language Preservation is the largest (~40k nodes) → it is the bench's worst case and the 50 ms budget anchor.
  • tsp test throughput bounded by Generator (rejection-free, type-directed generation) and per-term infer+step; target ≥ 20k terms/s at size ≤ 12; larger sizes degrade quadratically (subst cost) — cap corpus size, report a size-vs-throughput curve.
  • Fuzzing: keep Arbitrary KTerm shallow (depth ≤ 24) so corpus density is high; deep terms waste budget without adding coverage.

13.8 Edge Cases

  • Empty LangDef (no rules) → tsp check of a vacuous proof checks; tsp test → Pass(0) (nothing to generate); must not divide-by-zero.
  • Closed vs open terms: fuzzing subst/shift must include open terms (free indices) — assert shift(_,0,c) is identity, shift(shift(t,d1,c),d2,c)=shift(t,d1+d2,c) (composition), subst(shift(t,1,j),j,s)=t (shift-then-subst cancels), alpha_eq reflexive + congruent.
  • Max de Bruijn index at usize boundary → shift overflow must saturate/err, never wrap (silent capture); fixture with index near usize::MAX/2. whnf of a stuck KTerm (neutral App(Var n, …)) returns it unchanged; equal of two distinct neutrals = false.
  • Broken-rule that still passes the tester but fails the kernel (rare beats): include a variant where the bug is non-reachable by size-≤12 generation but provable-false — documents that proofs are stronger than tests (the dual-guard rationale). Eta on/off: if equal includes eta, fixture λx. f x ≡ f; if disabled, that fixture expects false — pin to the Phase 5 decision.

13.9 Failure Analysis

Failure Severity Detection Recovery
Kernel accepts ill-typed proof Catastrophic (validates false theorem; whole project unsound) fuzz re-check law + ill-typed corpus → CI red, blocks merge bisect infer_type/equal; add minimized KTerm as permanent regression fixture; audit TCB diff
Tester misses a real bug the kernel catches Expected (tests ⊂ proofs) differential agreement.rs: kernel fail + tester Pass on broken/ document as known-gap; not a defect — proofs are authoritative
Tester reports false counterexample on sound lang High agreement.rs: CounterExample where proof checks bug in Generator/check_* not the language; fix tester, re-run
Kernel panic / stack overflow under fuzz High (DoS, hides logic bugs) cargo fuzz crash dir add DepthLimit/ConvBudget; small-stack CI; regression seed
Conformance flaky (nondeterministic) Medium (erodes trust in gate) repeated CI runs disagree seed-pin everything; total shrink order; structural diff fallback
TCB SLOC regression Medium (TCB must stay small/auditable) tcb-report.json diff > +5% review added kernel code; justify or refactor out of tsp-kernel/src

13.10 Operational Notes

  • CI job validate (final gate): runs conformance, the four fuzz targets at -max_total_time=300, benches (compare-to-baseline, fail > 15% regression), tcb-report (fail > +5% SLOC), llvm-cov (fail < 95% TCB branch). Required for merge to default branch.
  • Fuzz corpus persistence: tsp-*/fuzz/corpus/ checked in (seed corpus = conformance KTerms); every crash minimized and promoted to a conformance fixture so it can never regress.
  • Reproducibility: tsp test --seed S + pinned Generator size make every CounterExample byte-reproducible; record S in .expected. Reports published: tcb-report.json (SLOC per kernel item, total) + bench-report.json (ns/node, terms/s, proof-node counts) committed to target/ and surfaced in the PR; the README "done gate" links both.
  • Nightly extended fuzz (≥ 1 h/target) beyond the 5-min CI budget; any nightly crash files a blocking issue.
  • Audit hook: any PR touching tsp-kernel/src/** flags tcb-report diff for mandatory human review — the TCB is the load-bearing invariant; growth must be deliberate.

Threat Model

Scope: an adversary supplies untrusted inputs — a LangDef (.lang), a proof script (a KTerm claiming to inhabit a Prop/KType), and object Terms — to tsp-cli → tsp-syntax/tsp-rules/tsp-kernel. The asset under protection is logical soundness: the kernel must never accept an ill-typed KTerm (never validate a false theorem), and no input may panic, hang, or exhaust resources. Not a confidentiality/network model — tsp is an offline checker; no secrets, sockets, or privileged ops. Threat = malicious or buggy input that subverts the trust the project's verdict carries.

25.1 Trust boundary (the TCB)

                 UNTRUSTED INPUT                       TRUSTED COMPUTING BASE (TCB)
   .lang ──► tsp-syntax ─┐                            ┌──────────────────────────────┐
   proof ─────────────────┼─► tsp-rules ─► tsp-meta ──►│  tsp-kernel                  │
   Term  ──► tsp-syntax ─┘   (infer/step/  (encode      │  infer_type / check_type     │
                             check_deriv)  goal+proof)   │  whnf / equal  ◄─ verdict   │
                                                         │  KTerm / KType / Ctx / Sort  │
                                                         └──────────────────────────────┘
   ── everything left of the kernel box is UNTRUSTED; only the kernel's accept/reject is load-bearing ──
Component In TCB? Why If it has a bug
tsp-kernel (infer_type, check_type, whnf, equal, KTerm/KType/Ctx) YES its accept = "theorem is true" unsoundness — gravest (T1)
tsp-meta encoder (progress_statement, preservation_statement, judgment signatures) partial mis-encodes the goal → kernel checks the wrong theorem (vacuous/weaker) proves nothing useful, but no false claim about the stated goal
tsp-syntax, tsp-rules (infer, step, check_deriv) NO their output is re-checked by the kernel as a proof term wrong answer, but kernel rejects a bad proof
tsp-test (Generator, Shrinker, check_progress/check_preservation) NO empirical only; complements proofs false counterexample or missed bug — caught by cross-check (T4)
tsp-cli NO orchestration/I-O only UX bug, not soundness

TCB budget: kernel ≤ 1500 LOC, zero external deps (audit tsp-kernel/Cargo.toml in CI — fail on any dependency). Encoder-correctness mitigation: progress_statement/preservation_statement outputs are golden-snapshot tested and printed by tsp check --show-goal for human audit; a vacuous goal (e.g. Pi _.True) is rejected by a meta::goal_nontrivial lint.

25.2 Threat catalogue

# Threat Vector Sev Mitigation Detection Telemetry Recovery
T1 Kernel unsoundness — crafted KTerm that check_type wrongly accepts at a false KType proof script critical tiny audited TCB (§25.1); bidirectional infer_type/check_type only — no unchecked axioms/casts; equal is the only conversion path kernel fuzzing (Phase 13 §25); broken-rule dual-guard (Phase 12): a known-unsound LangDef → proof MUST fail check_type; negative conformance corpus kernel.accept/kernel.reject counters; coverage of KTerm arms bisect kernel diff; revert to last audited kernel SHA; expand negative corpus
T2 Kernel non-termination — whnf/equal loops on a non-normalizing KTerm (Ω = (λx. x x)(λx. x x) analogue; ill-typed self-app) proof script high reduction step budget + conversion depth budget; budget exhausted ⇒ KernelError::Diverged (typed, not hang) wall-clock + step-count assertions in fuzz harness; timeout watchdog kernel.whnf_steps histogram, kernel.equal_depth max reject input with Diverged; raise budget only with proof of progress; never auto-extend silently
T3 Panic / crash on adversarial KTerm or .lang — unwrap, index OOB, stack overflow via deep nesting proof / .lang / Term high total Result everywhere in TCB (no unwrap/expect/panic!/unreachable! in tsp-kernel — #![deny(clippy::unwrap_used, clippy::panic)]); explicit De Bruijn bounds checks → KernelError::FreeVar; iterative or depth-capped recursion cargo fuzz target fuzz_kernel (Phase 13), 0 crashes over ≥1e8 execs gate; ASAN/Miri in CI crash-count = 0 invariant; fuzz corpus size minimized crashing input → regression test; fix as bounded error
T4 Generator bug — Generator emits an ill-typed Term, making a tsp-test "failure" bogus (false CounterExample) internal (corrupts test verdict) medium well-typedness invariant: every generated Term re-checked by tsp-rules::infer before property eval; assert infer(ctx,e).is_ok() cross-check: on the sound LangDef, check_progress/check_preservation must return only Pass (Phase 11); any CounterExample there is a generator/oracle bug, not a language bug gen.rejected ratio, test.pass/fail counters discard ill-typed sample; tighten generator typing rules; shrink-validate the witness against infer
