T5 De Bruijn off-by-one — wrong shift cutoff / subst index corrupts capture-avoiding substitution → silently wrong infer/step and breaks the substitution lemma (§19 Phase 7) code defect surfaced by inputs near binders high shift/subst are total usize/isize functions; laws as property tests (Phase 1): shift 0 = id; shift d (shift d' t) composition; subst/shift commutation; alpha_eq reflexive proptest on tsp-term; the substitution-lemma proof in tsp-meta fails to check if subst semantics drift (the kernel guards it) property-test pass rate; lemma-check status fix index arithmetic; re-run lemma checks (T1 guard re-asserts correctness)
T6 Resource exhaustion — enormous proof term, deeply-nested Term, or pathological .lang exhausts heap/stack proof / .lang / Term medium size caps: max KTerm nodes, max parse depth, max Ctx length; parser recursion-depth limit → ParseErrorKind::*; arena/Box bounded; reject > cap with typed error input-size meter pre-check; OOM watchdog (RSS ceiling) in fuzz/CI kernel.term_nodes, parser.depth, peak RSS reject oversize input; caps tunable via flag, default conservative
T7 Supply-chain — a transitive dep compromises the build or sneaks code into the TCB dependency graph medium deny.toml (advisories=deny, unmaintained=deny, license allow-list); minimal deps; tsp-kernel has zero external deps (enforced); Cargo.lock committed; reproducible build (Dockerfile pinned) cargo deny check in CI; cargo tree -p tsp-kernel must show no non-std edges dep-count per crate; advisory hits pin/patch/yank dep; vendor if needed; kernel stays dep-free regardless
T8 Malformed LangDef — BadBinder/MalformedRule/UnknownSort smuggles an ill-formed rule the engine mis-handles .lang low tsp-syntax validates sorts/binders/rule arity → ParseError; tsp-rules rejects judgments referencing undeclared form/sort parser conformance corpus; well-formedness pass before rule use parse.reject by ParseErrorKind surface diagnostic with span; no partial LangDef reaches the kernel
T9 Encoder mis-statement — tsp-meta builds a vacuous or weaker progress_statement/preservation_statement (e.g. drops a quantifier), so the kernel "proves" a true-but-useless goal tsp-meta encoder high golden-snapshot the encoded goal; meta::goal_nontrivial lint rejects vacuous props (e.g. Pi _.True); tsp check --show-goal prints the goal for human audit encoder-goal diff vs the S5 (§5.4) normative statement shape on every phase touching tsp-meta meta.goal_hash drift counter revert encoder change; re-derive the goal from S5 by hand; add the caught shape to the golden corpus
T10 Stale-proof replay — a proof script checked against an older kernel is fed to a newer kernel whose equal/infer_type accept-set silently changed (e.g. eta later enabled), so an old "verified" claim is trusted without re-verification proof script + kernel version skew medium Compatibility & Versioning verdict-stability rule: no kernel change may flip accept↔reject without a MAJOR bump; every kernel release re-runs the full soundness suite (Testing & Verification Strategy §29.6 conformance ledger) verdict snapshot suite (Compatibility & Versioning §28.4) diffs accept/reject per kernel version kernel semver + tcb-report SHA recorded per proof pin the proof to its originating kernel SHA; re-check under the current kernel before trusting it again

25.3 Adversary models

  • A0 (honest-but-buggy author): mistakes in .lang/proof. Goal: clear errors, no false "proved". Covered by T5/T8 + encoder audit.
  • A1 (crafted false proof): wants check_type to accept a KTerm at a false Prop. Primary adversary. Defeated only by kernel correctness → the entire weight is on the small audited TCB + T1 fuzz/conformance dual-guard.
  • A2 (resource adversary): wants hang/OOM/crash to DoS or mask a wrong verdict. Defeated by T2/T3/T6 budgets + caps + total Result.
  • A3 (oracle adversary): exploits the tester to assert a bogus counterexample (discredit a sound language). Defeated by T4 well-typedness invariant + sound-language cross-check.

25.4 Invariants (must hold under all inputs)

INV-SOUND  : check_type(ctx,t,ty)=Ok  ⇒  t is genuinely a closed proof of ty   (load-bearing; T1)
INV-TOTAL  : ∀ input. kernel/parser return Result; never panic, never UB        (T3)
INV-TERM   : whnf/equal terminate within budget OR return KernelError::Diverged  (T2)
INV-CAP    : accepted KTerm.nodes ≤ MAX_NODES ∧ Ctx.len ≤ MAX_CTX                (T6)
INV-WF     : every Term reaching tsp-rules/kernel is well-formed De Bruijn-closed (T5,T8)
INV-ORACLE : on the sound LangDef, tsp-test yields only Pass                      (T4)
INV-DEP    : tsp-kernel external-dep count = 0                                    (T7)

CI gates (Phase 13 "done"): kernel fuzz 0 crashes / 0 false-accepts over ≥1e8 execs; broken-rule test caught by both kernel (proof rejected) and tester (shrunk CounterExample); cargo deny clean; Miri clean on tsp-kernel. A regression on INV-SOUND is a release blocker — every other defect downgrades to a wrong-but-rejected verdict, which is fail-safe; only T1 fails open.

Failure Modes & Recovery

Taxonomy of every way the system departs from its specification. Severity scale: S0 silent-unsound (false theorem accepted), S1 crash/hang, S2 wrong-but-loud (rejects valid / accepts-then-fails-downstream), S3 UX/diagnostic. Per class: symptoms, root cause, detection, automatic recovery, manual recovery, operator actions, forensic artifacts, long-term mitigation. The dual-guard thesis recurs: every soundness-relevant defect must be caught by the kernel (infer_type/check_type REJECT) AND by tsp-test (a shrunk CounterExample); divergence between the two guards is itself a failure signal.

Severity-ranked failure-mode map

ID Class Sev Blast radius Primary guard
F1 Parse / LangDef error S3 one .lang / object program tsp-syntax ParseError
F2 Ill-typed proof term — kernel REJECTS (healthy) S2→ok one Proof tsp-kernel KernelError
F3 Kernel unsoundness — kernel ACCEPTS ill-typed term S0 every theorem ever checked kernel fuzz + broken-rule + TCB audit
F4 whnf/equal non-termination (loop/blowup) S1 the check invocation fuel/step-budget guard
F5 Generator emits ill-typed Term S2 all tsp-test runs generator-soundness assertion
F6 shift/subst off-by-one S0/S2 substitution lemma + every step proof-fails OR spurious CounterExample
F7 Soundness proof fails to check post-extension S2 the extended calculus infer_type REJECT at missing case
F8 CounterExample does not reproduce S2/S3 one failing test run replay-from-seed mismatch
F9 Encoder mis-states the goal (vacuous/weaker proposition "proved") S2 the mis-encoded theorem only meta::goal_nontrivial lint + golden goal diff
F10 Stale-proof accepted across a kernel version change (no re-verification) S2 every proof carried forward without re-check verdict snapshot suite (Compatibility & Versioning §28.4)

F1 — Parse / LangDef errors (S3)

  • Symptoms: tsp check/typecheck exits non-zero with ParseError; no LangDef/Term produced.
  • Root cause: malformed .lang (UnknownSort, BadBinder, MalformedRule) or object syntax (UnexpectedToken); binder-arity mismatch vs declared sorts/grammar.
  • Detection: tsp-syntax Parser — total, fail-closed; never returns a partial LangDef. ParseErrorKind + byte span.
  • Automatic recovery: none attempted (front-end fail-closed; no error-recovery parse → no half-built LangDef reaching tsp-rules/kernel).
  • Manual recovery: fix source at reported span; re-run.
  • Operator actions: none (build-time, deterministic).
  • Forensic artifacts: ParseError{kind, span, expected}; offending line echoed with caret.
  • Long-term mitigation: golden corpus of malformed .lang (one fixture per ParseErrorKind); fuzz the lexer/parser for panic-freedom (must only ever return Err, never unwrap).

F2 — Ill-typed proof term, kernel REJECTS (S2 — the normal, healthy case)

  • Symptoms: tsp check reports KernelError (type mismatch / unbound Var / non-Pi in App); Proof.term does not check at Proof.goal.
  • Root cause: an actual gap in the proof term (wrong lemma applied, wrong index, missing argument). This is the kernel working as designed — a rejected bad proof is success, not a fault.
  • Detection: check_type(ctx, &proof.term, &proof.goal) ⇒ Err; localized to the failing sub-KTerm.
  • Automatic recovery: n/a — rejection IS the correct outcome; CI marks the proof red.
  • Manual recovery: repair the proof term in tsp-meta; re-check.
  • Operator actions: none.
  • Forensic artifacts: KernelError with the sub-term, expected vs inferred KType, Ctx depth.
  • Long-term mitigation: keep a "must-reject" fixture set (intentionally wrong proof terms) asserting the kernel says Err — a regression here (suddenly Ok) is an F3 escalation.
  • Invariant: F2 must never silently become F3. A test that flips from REJECT to ACCEPT is treated as S0.

F3 — KERNEL UNSOUNDNESS — kernel ACCEPTS an ill-typed term (S0, GRAVEST)

  • Symptoms: none at runtime — that is the danger. A false Prop (e.g. a bogus Preservation) check-passes; tsp check exits 0 on a calculus that is actually unsound.
  • Root cause: a bug in the TCB (tsp-kernel): wrong de Bruijn handling in infer_type, a missing equal/whnf step letting non-convertible KTypes unify, a Sort rule error (Type:Kind mishandled), or an unchecked Pi/App domain.
  • Detection (defense in depth — no single guard trusted):
    1. Kernel fuzzing (Phase 13): adversarial KTerm generation; oracle = the kernel must never accept an ill-typed term and never panic. Differential check: any Ok from infer_type is re-validated by an independent (slow, naive) re-checker.
    2. The broken-rule test (Phase 12): a deliberately BROKEN typing rule MUST cause the soundness proof to fail to check. If the kernel still accepts ⇒ unsoundness exposed.
    3. TCB audit: the kernel is kept small (target ≤ ~1.5–2 KLoC) and line-audited; every reduction in whnf/equal and every rule in infer_type has a cited justification (LF / lambda-Pi).
    4. Dual-guard cross-check: tsp-test finds a CounterExample on a language the kernel claims sound ⇒ contradiction ⇒ kernel suspect.
  • Automatic recovery: none possible — an accepted false theorem cannot be undone by the system. CI gate: broken-rule fixture asserting check FAILS; if it passes, the build is red and all prior "verified" claims are quarantined.
  • Manual recovery: bisect the kernel diff; revert to last audited TCB; re-prove against the audited kernel; re-run the entire tsp-meta proof set + conformance suite.
  • Operator actions: freeze releases; broadcast that the trusted base is compromised; nothing the kernel "proved" is trustworthy until re-checked by a fixed kernel.
  • Forensic artifacts: the accepted ill-typed KTerm; its claimed KType; the exact infer_type/equal step that wrongly succeeded; kernel git SHA; fuzzer seed; the broken-rule .lang.
  • Long-term mitigation: minimize and freeze the TCB; mandatory two-reviewer sign-off on any tsp-kernel change; independent re-checker kept as a permanent differential oracle; nightly kernel fuzz; the broken-rule test wired as a required gate. This is the load-bearing invariant of the project.

F4 — whnf / equal non-termination (S1)

  • Symptoms: tsp check hangs or OOMs; CPU pinned; no KernelError, no result.
  • Root cause: definitional-equality conversion on a KTerm whose reduction does not reach whnf (ill-formed term reached equal before typing, or a normalization-non-terminating shape), or unbounded equal recursion on diverging sub-terms.
  • Detection: per-invocation step/fuel budget on whnf reductions and equal comparisons; exceeding it ⇒ KernelError::FuelExhausted (fail-closed, not a hang). Watchdog timeout in tsp-cli.
  • Automatic recovery: abort the single check with FuelExhausted; the kernel returns Err, never an Ok — so a loop can NEVER masquerade as a proof (preserves the F3 invariant).
  • Manual recovery: inspect the term hitting the budget; raise the budget only if the term is legitimately large; otherwise treat as a malformed/non-normalizing proof and fix it.
  • Operator actions: set --fuel/--timeout; capture the offending KTerm snapshot.
  • Forensic artifacts: partial whnf trace (bounded), fuel counter at abort, term size, Ctx depth.
  • Long-term mitigation: only invoke equal/whnf on terms that already passed infer_type structural checks where possible; fuzz whnf for termination under budget; document that equal is conversion-checking, not a decision procedure for diverging terms.

F5 — Generator produces ill-typed terms (S2)

  • Symptoms: tsp test reports a CounterExample on the KNOWN-SOUND language; check_preservation/check_progress "fail" though the calculus is correct.
  • Root cause: bug in tsp-test Generator — emits a Term that does not satisfy infer(ctx, e); the property is then vacuously violated because the premise (well-typedness) is false.
  • Detection: generator-soundness assertion — every generated Term is fed through tsp-rules::infer; a generator output that fails infer is a generator bug, NOT a soundness counterexample. Cross-check vs the kernel: the proofs say sound, so any CounterExample on the sound language is presumed generator-side (or F6).
  • Automatic recovery: discard ill-typed samples; re-draw; the Generator is constrained to be type-directed (build well-typed by construction).
  • Manual recovery: fix the generation rule for the offending type former; add the sample to the generator regression set.
  • Operator actions: re-run with the recorded seed to reproduce.
  • Forensic artifacts: the rejected Term, the infer TypeError, generator seed + size bound.
  • Long-term mitigation: assert generator soundness in CI (N samples all infer-clean on the sound language → 0 CounterExamples); keep Generator and infer co-tested.

F6 — shift / subst off-by-one (S0 via proof OR S2 via tester)

  • Symptoms: two faces — (a) the substitution lemma proof in tsp-meta fails to check (KernelError); (b) tsp-test yields a spurious CounterExample to Preservation that does not correspond to a real unsoundness.
  • Root cause: wrong cutoff/d in shift, or wrong index in subst([s/j]t) — capture-avoidance broken; the de Bruijn discipline diverges from the substitution-lemma statement it must mirror.
  • Detection (dual-guard): the substitution lemma is the canary — its proof term stops checking; AND check_preservation flags the beta/elimination step where subst is used. Agreement of both guards localizes to tsp-term::subst/shift.
  • Automatic recovery: none — both guards correctly fail; CI red.
  • Manual recovery: fix shift/subst; re-run the shift/subst property suite (round-trip, composition) BEFORE re-proving.
  • Operator actions: none.
  • Forensic artifacts: the failing substitution-lemma sub-KTerm; the spurious CounterExample Term + Witness; the [s/j]t inputs; expected vs actual indices.
  • Long-term mitigation: property tests shift/subst/alpha_eq (capture-avoidance, shift 0 = id, substitution-composition) as gates upstream of tsp-meta; the lemma proof and tsp-term::subst reviewed together — an off-by-one must fail loudly in BOTH faces, never silently.

F7 — Soundness proof fails to check after a language extension (S2)

  • Symptoms: after adding naturals/booleans/products/sums/refs/System F (Phases 10/12), tsp check REJECTS Progress or Preservation that previously passed; KernelError at a specific constructor case.
  • Root cause: a missing canonical-forms or inversion case for the new type former, or a step/Rule added without the matching proof case — the inductive proof term no longer covers all shapes.
  • Detection: kernel REJECT pinpoints the un-handled case; exhaustiveness of enum match on Type/Term in tsp-rules flags missing arms at compile time.
  • Automatic recovery: none — incompleteness must be surfaced, not patched over.
  • Manual recovery: extend canonical_forms/inversion and the Progress/Preservation proof terms with the new case; re-check; add the constructor to the conformance suite.
  • Operator actions: none.
  • Forensic artifacts: the rejected case (which Type former, which Rule), the proof-term gap, the diff that introduced the constructor.
  • Long-term mitigation: extension checklist (every new constructor ⇒ value/canonical-forms/inversion/step/proof-case + a tsp-test generator arm); the broken-rule test (Phase 12) doubles as a guard that proof coverage is real, not vacuous.

F8 — Tester CounterExample does not reproduce (S2/S3)

  • Symptoms: a reported CounterExample(Term, Witness) re-runs as Pass; flaky tsp test.
  • Root cause: non-deterministic Generator (unseeded RNG), Shrinker mutating shared state, or environment-dependent ordering — the failing Term was not faithfully recorded/replayed.
  • Detection: every run prints its seed; replay-from-seed must bit-reproduce; a CounterExample is re-validated by directly evaluating check_progress/check_preservation on the recorded Term (seed-independent).
  • Automatic recovery: the Shrinker re-checks each candidate against the recorded Term, not live RNG; final minimal CounterExample is replayed once before reporting.
  • Manual recovery: reproduce from seed; if still flaky, the Generator/Shrinker has hidden state — fix to be a pure function of (seed, size).
  • Operator actions: re-run tsp test --seed <S>; attach seed to any bug report.
  • Forensic artifacts: RNG seed, size bound, the recorded Term, the Witness (which property + the step), shrink trace length.
  • Long-term mitigation: Generator/Shrinker are deterministic pure functions of (seed, size); every CounterExample ships with a standalone reproducing Term so it never depends on regenerating from RNG.

F9 — Encoder mis-states the goal (S2)

  • Symptoms: tsp check reports success, but tsp check --show-goal reveals a weaker or vacuous KType (e.g. missing a quantifier, or a Pi _.True-shaped tautology) rather than the intended progress_statement/preservation_statement.
  • Root cause: a bug in tsp-meta's enc_judgment/progress_statement/preservation_statement builders (Phase 6) drops a premise or substitutes the wrong index, so the kernel faithfully proves a true but useless statement.
  • Detection: golden-snapshot diff of the encoded goal against the S5 (§5.4) normative statement shape on every PR touching the encoder; meta::goal_nontrivial lint rejects trivially-true goals.
  • Automatic recovery: none — the kernel cannot detect that a goal is the wrong theorem, only that a term does or does not inhabit the stated one; this class is caught upstream of the kernel entirely.
  • Manual recovery: re-derive the goal by hand from S5; fix the encoder; add the caught shape to the golden corpus so it never regresses silently.
  • Operator actions: treat any encoder change as requiring a fresh human read of --show-goal output, not just a green CI run.
  • Forensic artifacts: the encoded KType, the golden .golden diff, the Rule/judgment being encoded.
  • Long-term mitigation: the golden-snapshot suite is a required CI gate (T9); encoder changes get the same two-reviewer scrutiny as kernel changes, since a wrong goal is functionally equivalent to a wrong proof.

F10 — Stale-proof accepted across a kernel version change (S2)

  • Symptoms: a proof script that checked under an older tsp-kernel is assumed valid after the kernel is upgraded (e.g. eta enabled), without being re-run.
  • Root cause: no re-verification step gates a kernel version bump; the verdict-stability rule (Compatibility & Versioning §28.2–§28.4) is not enforced in the consuming pipeline.
  • Detection: the verdict snapshot suite (§28.4) re-checks every golden (input, expected_verdict) pair on every kernel change; any accept/reject flip is flagged.
  • Automatic recovery: none — a stale accepted proof is not automatically re-validated; this is a process gate, not a kernel behavior.
  • Manual recovery: re-run tsp check for every proof against the current kernel; pin proofs to their originating kernel SHA in the changelog.
  • Operator actions: block release until the full soundness suite (Testing & Verification Strategy §29.6) re-passes under the new kernel.
  • Forensic artifacts: kernel git SHA at proof-creation time vs at consumption time; the verdict snapshot diff.
  • Long-term mitigation: the Operational Readiness §27.6 release checklist makes "re-run full suite" a mandatory, non-skippable release step, never an assumed carry-forward.

Cross-cutting recovery invariants

  • Fail-closed everywhere in the TCB: tsp-kernel returns Err on any doubt; a hang/OOM is converted to FuelExhausted Err, never to Ok. No code path yields Ok without a completed check_type.
  • Dual-guard reconciliation: kernel-says-sound ∧ tester-finds-CounterExample ⇒ investigate F3 or F6 first (a real disagreement is never benign). kernel-rejects ∧ tester-passes ⇒ usually F7 (proof incomplete) or F2 (genuine proof bug).
  • Quarantine rule: any S0 (F3/F6-unsound face) red-flags every downstream "verified" claim until re-checked by the fixed, re-audited kernel.
  • No silent self-repair of soundness: the system never "fixes" a failing proof automatically; recovery for soundness classes is always operator-driven, with the kernel/tester as the immovable oracles.

Operational Readiness

Framing: tsp is a proof-framework tool + library, not a service. "Operational readiness" = the instrumentation, SLOs, dashboards, and runbooks needed to (a) keep the trusted computing base (TCB — the tsp-kernel lambda-Pi checker) demonstrably sound, (b) catch metatheory regressions when the object language is extended (Phases 10/12), and (c) keep tsp check / tsp test fast enough to stay in the inner dev loop. The load-bearing invariant — the kernel never accepts an ill-typed proof term — has no graceful-degradation story; it is binary, so readiness is dominated by detection (fuzz, audit, dual-guard) not recovery.

27.1 Metrics to Expose

Emitted by the library as a structured Metrics record (returned, never globally mutated — keeps tsp-kernel no_std + side-effect-free); the tsp-cli (std) serializes them to JSON / Prometheus-text. No metric ever influences a verdict.

Metric Type Source crate Unit Notes
tsp_proofs_checked_total counter tsp-meta/tsp-kernel proofs per Proof { goal, term } submitted to check_type
tsp_proofs_accepted_total counter tsp-kernel proofs infer_type/check_type returned Ok
tsp_proofs_rejected_total{kind} counter tsp-kernel proofs label = KernelError variant (TypeMismatch, NotAFunction, UnboundVar, SortError, ConvFail)
tsp_kernel_check_seconds histogram tsp-kernel s p50/p95/p99; per top-level check_type call
tsp_kernel_whnf_steps histogram tsp-kernel steps whnf reductions per check; conversion-blowup early-warning
tsp_kernel_equal_calls histogram tsp-kernel calls equal (definitional eq) invocations / check
tsp_proof_term_nodes histogram tsp-meta KTerm nodes size of submitted proof term (Var/Sort/Pi/Lam/App count)
tsp_proof_term_depth histogram tsp-meta nodes max nesting depth (stack-budget signal)
tsp_derivation_nodes histogram tsp-rules Derivation nodes size of object-level Derivation checked by check_deriv
tsp_tcb_lines gauge build script LOC counts only tsp-kernel non-test, non-comment lines; SLO ceiling (27.4)
tsp_test_terms_generated_total counter tsp-test terms from Generator
tsp_test_pass_total counter tsp-test terms TestResult::Pass
tsp_test_counterexamples_total{prop} counter tsp-test terms label = progress | preservation (the Witness property)
tsp_test_shrink_steps histogram tsp-test steps Shrinker reductions to minimal CounterExample
tsp_test_gen_reject_total counter tsp-test attempts ill-typed candidates discarded before a well-typed Term (gen efficiency)
tsp_fuzz_iterations_total{target} counter fuzz harness iters targets: kernel_infer, kernel_whnf, subst, check_deriv
tsp_fuzz_panics_total counter fuzz harness panics MUST stay 0 — any panic is a release blocker (27.4)
tsp_fuzz_false_accept_total counter fuzz harness accepts kernel accepted a known-ill-typed KTerm — MUST stay 0, gravest class
tsp_rss_peak_bytes gauge tsp-cli bytes peak resident during a check/test run

Derived dashboard rates: accept ratio = accepted/checked; reject-by-kind breakdown; gen efficiency = generated/(generated+gen_reject); counterexample rate per 10⁶ terms (sound language ⇒ 0).

27.2 Structured Tracing

Library is silent by default; tracing behind a trace Cargo feature (zero overhead when off — gated Option<&mut dyn TraceSink> threaded through, NOT a global logger, so tsp-kernel stays no_std/pure). Events as flat structured records (JSONL via tsp-cli).

Per-proof span (tsp-meta → tsp-kernel):

event=proof_check goal_hash=<blake3(KType)> goal_nodes=N
  term_nodes=N term_depth=D whnf_steps=W equal_calls=E
  check_seconds=S verdict=accept|reject reject_kind=<KernelError|->

Per-lemma dependency edges (tsp-meta): each lemma/theorem (canonical_forms, inversion, weakening, substitution lemma, preservation_statement, progress_statement) emits the set of lemmas its proof term invokes ⇒ a lemma dependency DAG:

event=lemma_dep lemma=preservation depends_on=[inversion, substitution]
  status=checked|failed|stale check_seconds=S term_nodes=N

DAG uses: (1) impact analysis — extend the language → which downstream theorems re-check (Phase 10/12); (2) topological check order (leaves first ⇒ fail fast at the real cause); (3) the dashboard's "soundness coverage" view (27.3). Object-level traces (tsp-rules): infer/step/check_deriv emit term -> Type|TypeError, e --> e'|stuck, Derivation valid|DerivError at trace level only.

27.3 Logging Philosophy & Dashboard

Library never logs — it returns. tsp-kernel returns Result<KTerm, KernelError> / Result<(), KernelError>; tsp-rules returns TypeError/DerivError; tsp-test returns TestResult. Rationale: a soundness kernel must be deterministic, pure, and embeddable; hidden I/O in the TCB is forbidden. CLI owns all human output: tsp logs progress (checking lemma 7/14: substitution …), a final verdict table, and --json for machines. Log levels (CLI only): error = a proof rejected / a CounterExample found / a panic; warn = check exceeded its time budget, gen-reject ratio high; info = per-phase progress, totals; debug/trace = the 27.2 events. Exit codes: 0 all proofs check + tester all-Pass; 1 a proof rejected (KernelError); 2 tester found a CounterExample; 3 parse/usage error; 101 panic (never expected — see fuzz SLO).

Metatheory dashboard (tsp report, static HTML/JSON over the lemma DAG + last run's metrics) — the "is the soundness proof healthy?" view:

  • Lemma board: every lemma/theorem as a node, colored checked / failed / stale, with check time + term_nodes. The 14-phase soundness spine (canonical forms → inversion → weakening → substitution → preservation → progress → soundness corollary) rendered as the dependency DAG; one red node ⇒ trace its descendants.
  • Soundness coverage: % of object-language constructors (each Term/Type variant: Lam, App, TmTrue/TmFalse/If, Zero/Succ, products, sums, refs, All/TyVar) for which (a) a canonical-forms case, (b) an inversion case, and (c) a preservation step-case exist and check. After an extension, uncovered constructors are flagged — this is the "did we forget a case?" guard (27.5).
  • Dual-guard panel: kernel verdict vs tester verdict for the current language. Healthy = (all proofs accept) ∧ (counterexamples = 0). Any disagreement is the headline alarm.
  • TCB panel: tsp_tcb_lines vs ceiling; fuzz iterations + false_accept/panic counts (both 0); last TCB-audit date (27.6).

27.4 SLO Targets

SLO Target Window Severity if missed
Kernel soundness (false_accept_total) 0, always every release + continuous fuzz SEV-1, release blocker
Kernel panic-freedom (fuzz_panics_total) 0 on adversarial KTerm continuous fuzz SEV-1, release blocker
TCB size (tsp_tcb_lines, tsp-kernel only) ≤ 1200 LOC (target ~800) per commit SEV-3, audit-debt review
Soundness proof checks (full suite, base + extended STLC) 100% accept per CI run SEV-1 if a previously-checked lemma regresses
Dual-guard agreement tester Pass ⇔ proofs check on sound language per CI run SEV-2 (one guard masks the other)
Kernel check-time budget, per top-level theorem p95 ≤ 50 ms, p99 ≤ 200 ms (STLC + extensions) per CI run SEV-3 (perf), not a correctness gate
Full tsp check (all 14 phases' proofs) wall time ≤ 10 s warm per CI run SEV-3
Tester throughput (tsp test) ≥ 50k well-typed terms/s; ≥ 10⁶ terms in default soak nightly SEV-3
Shrink convergence minimal CounterExample in ≤ 200 shrink_steps when triggered SEV-3
Peak RSS ≤ 256 MB for default suite per CI run SEV-3

Note the asymmetry: the two zero-targets are correctness SLOs (no error budget — a single violation invalidates every proof the kernel ever blessed); the time/throughput/RSS targets are performance SLOs with normal budgets. There is no "soundness availability %" — soundness is not statistical.

27.5 Incident Response & Runbooks

Symptom Likely cause Runbook
Proof fails to check after a language extension (Phase 10/12) — a previously-checked lemma turns failed new Term/Type constructor lacks a case in canonical_forms / inversion / the preservation step-analysis 1. Read KernelError + the rejecting subterm from the per-proof trace (27.2). 2. Open the soundness-coverage panel (27.3) → find the uncovered constructor. 3. Add the missing canonical-forms / inversion / preservation case in tsp-meta; re-run leaf-first per the lemma DAG. 4. Cross-check the new step-rule's subst/shift against tsp-term (an off-by-one in shift will break the substitution lemma — the crux).
Kernel accepted something suspicious (a "theorem" that shouldn't hold, or false_accept > 0) bug in tsp-kernel infer_type/check_type/whnf/equal — gravest class, SEV-1 1. Freeze the release. 2. Minimize the offending KTerm via the kernel fuzz harness (tsp_fuzz target kernel_infer). 3. Diff the proof term against the lambda-Pi typing rules (Phase 5); suspect conversion: log whnf + equal decisions on the term. 4. Audit the TCB (27.6) around the implicated rule (Pi-formation, App, conversion). 5. Add the minimized KTerm as a permanent adversarial regression fixture; only then unfreeze.
Tester reports a CounterExample (TestResult::CounterExample(Term, Witness)) either a genuinely broken rule (the intended Phase 12 demo) or a bug in tsp-rules infer/step 1. Read the Witness → which property (progress/preservation) and the failing step. 2. Re-run the Shrinker to a minimal Term; confirm shrink_steps ≤ budget. 3. Run that same Term through tsp typecheck/tsp step to see infer vs step disagreement. 4. Decide: broken rule (fix tsp-rules, and the matching lemma case must then also fail in the kernel — dual-guard) vs broken tester (fix Generator/check_progress/check_preservation).
Dual-guard disagreement: proofs all check but tester finds a counterexample (or vice-versa) a lemma over-claims (kernel gap) OR the tester's infer/step diverges from the rules the proof encodes Treat as SEV-2. The two guards must agree on a sound language; reconcile by replaying the same Term/Derivation through both tsp-meta (encoded judgment) and tsp-rules (executable). The encoded step and executable step MUST be the same relation.
tsp check exceeds time budget conversion blowup (whnf_steps/equal_calls spike) or pathological proof-term size Inspect the per-proof histogram; the spiking lemma is the culprit. Perf-only (SEV-3): consider memoizing whnf or restructuring the proof term — never weaken equal (soundness over speed).
Kernel panic (fuzz_panics_total > 0, exit 101) unhandled KTerm shape, index underflow in de Bruijn handling, stack overflow on deep term SEV-1. Capture the input via the fuzz corpus; convert the panic into a typed KernelError (the kernel must reject, never panic, on adversarial input); add a depth guard if stack-driven.

27.6 Deployment & TCB-Audit Cadence

  • Distribution: the seven tsp-* crates published to a registry under semver; tsp-cli ships the tsp binary. The TCB (tsp-kernel) is versioned and changelogged separately and conspicuously — any change to infer_type/check_type/whnf/equal/KTerm/Sort is a minimum minor bump and triggers a mandatory audit, even if the public API is unchanged (its behavior is the contract everything else trusts).
  • TCB-audit cadence: (1) per-TCB-PR — two-reviewer sign-off, line-by-line, against the Phase-5 lambda-Pi rules; (2) per-release — re-run the kernel fuzz target to its iteration target with false_accept = 0 ∧ panics = 0, and re-confirm tsp_tcb_lines ≤ ceiling; (3) quarterly — full TCB read-through + adversarial-fixture review, recorded with a date stamp surfaced on the dashboard TCB panel. A release is blocked unless: both zero-SLOs hold, the full soundness suite is 100% accept, the dual guards agree on the sound language, and the TCB size is under ceiling.
  • Release checklist (gate, in order): tsp check (all 14 phases) 100% accept → tsp test soak all-Pass (0 counterexamples) → fuzz targets green (0 false-accept, 0 panic) → tcb_lines ≤ 1200 → semver + TCB changelog updated → quarterly-audit date current. Only then tag.
  • Backwards-compat: object-language extensions (Phase 10/12) are additive to Term/Type enums (minor bump); the metalanguage (LangDef) and .lang proof-script format are versioned so an old proof script either checks under the kernel it was written against or is explicitly re-validated — a proof is only as trustworthy as the exact TCB version that accepted it.

Compatibility & Versioning

Scope: what callers/downstream may rely on across versions, and the change-control discipline gating each surface. The TCB (tsp-kernel) carries the strictest contract: a soundness regression there silently validates false theorems, so it is versioned and reviewed at a tier above all other crates.

28.1 Compatibility surfaces (ranked by blast radius)

Tier Surface Crate Why load-bearing Break =
0 (TCB) KTerm/KType, Sort, Ctx, infer_type/check_type, whnf, equal tsp-kernel accepting one ill-typed proof term invalidates every theorem soundness regression
1 LangDef format + .lang grammar (sorts/grammar/judgments/rules) tsp-syntax every object-language definition + proof script is encoded against it all .lang files reparse-fail
1 proof-term concrete syntax (kernel KTerm surface) tsp-syntax/kernel checked-in proofs are text against it proofs fail to load
2 Term/Type, shift/subst/alpha_eq/is_value tsp-term substitution lemma mirrors subst/shift exactly lemma/parity drift
2 Judgment/Rule/Derivation, check_deriv, infer, step tsp-rules rule data + executable checker consumed by tsp-meta/tsp-test derivations/proofs break
3 progress_statement/preservation_statement, Proof tsp-meta theorem statements other code asserts against metatheory API churn
3 Generator/Shrinker, TestResult/Witness, check_progress/check_preservation tsp-test property harness embedded in CI falsifier API churn
4 tsp subcommands check/test/typecheck/step, exit codes, output tsp-cli scripts/CI parse it automation breaks

28.2 SemVer policy (per-crate, independent versions)

  • Each tsp-* crate versioned independently; workspace pins exact =x.y.z inter-crate deps -> no silent minor drift inside the TCB chain.
  • 0.y.z pre-1.0: 0.MINOR = breaking, 0.0.PATCH = additive/fix (Cargo convention). Post-1.0: standard MAJOR.MINOR.PATCH.
  • Breaking (MAJOR / 0.MINOR): remove/rename any Tier 0–3 public item; change a signature; change infer_type/check_type ACCEPT/REJECT verdict on any existing input; change shift/subst numeric result; alter .lang/proof grammar meaning of existing syntax; change is_value/step verdict; change CLI exit-code semantics.
  • Additive (MINOR): new Type/Term/KTerm variant (see 28.6 — #[non_exhaustive]); new judgment form; new rule; new subcommand/flag; new ParseErrorKind/KernelError/TypeError/DerivError variant.
  • Patch: perf, diagnostics text, internal refactor — provided every Tier 0–2 verdict is byte-identical (gated by 28.4 snapshot suite).
  • Verdict-stability rule (binding): no kernel change at any version level may flip an existing accept->reject or reject->accept without a MAJOR bump + 28.4 sign-off, because both directions are soundness/completeness-relevant.

28.3 TCB stability contract (tsp-kernel — highest scrutiny)

  • TCB size budget is a published contract: target <= 2000 LOC kernel; any PR that grows it requires explicit owner (Mujir Qureshi) sign-off recorded in the changelog. Shrinking is always allowed (patch).
  • Every kernel change — even a patch/comment-adjacent edit to infer_type/check_type/whnf/equal — re-runs, and must pass with zero diffs:
    1. full soundness suite (canonical_forms, inversion, weakening, substitution lemma, Preservation, Progress proof terms all re-check_type);
    2. the Phase 12 broken-rule demonstration (kernel MUST still reject the deliberately-broken-rule proof);
    3. the Phase 13 kernel fuzz corpus (never accepts an ill-typed KTerm; never panics on adversarial input).
  • Kernel changes are merge-blocked until (1)+(2)+(3) are green; this is a hard CI gate, not advisory.
  • equal/whnf are part of the contract: a definitional-equality change (e.g. enabling eta) is MAJOR even if it only adds accepted conversions, because it changes which proof terms check_type accepts.
  • Audit log: each TCB release records reviewer, LOC delta, and the green-suite hash. No kernel release ships without it.

28.4 Verdict snapshot suite (the verdict-stability gate)

  • Golden corpus of (input, expected_verdict) pairs covering all Tier 0–2 functions: infer_type/check_type accept+reject cases, whnf/equal results, infer/step/is_value/check_deriv outcomes, plus shift/subst exact term outputs.
  • Runs on every PR; any verdict diff fails CI and forces the SemVer classification (accept/reject flip => MAJOR; new accept under same connective => review).
  • New accepted/rejected behaviors are added to the corpus in the same PR that introduces them, with the SemVer bump justified in the changelog.

28.5 Metalanguage / proof-term syntax versioning

  • .lang and proof-term grammars carry a pinned version header tsp-lang 1 / tsp-proof 1 (EBNF frozen per major). Files without a header => assumed v1.
  • Parser accepts its own major and any additive prior minor; an unknown-major header is a hard ParseError, never a silent misparse.
  • Deprecation policy: a grammar form marked deprecated emits a ParseError-adjacent warning for >= 1 minor release before removal at the next MAJOR; removal listed in changelog with a migration note. No syntax is removed without one deprecation window.
  • Reserved-keyword additions are MINOR only if they cannot collide with existing identifiers in checked-in fixtures (verified by reparsing the corpus); otherwise MAJOR.

28.6 Adding object-language features / judgment forms without breakage

  • Type, Term, KTerm, and every error enum are #[non_exhaustive] -> adding a variant (e.g. products/sums/refs constructors, All/TyVar for System F, a new Ref-store judgment) is MINOR; external match keeps a wildcard arm and still compiles.
  • New JudgmentForm/Rule/Production entries in a LangDef are pure data additions: existing .lang files are unaffected (they simply don't mention the new form). Existing derivations stay valid under check_deriv.
  • Rule-table monotonicity guarantee: adding a rule never invalidates a previously-valid Derivation; check_deriv only consults rules a derivation names. (Removing/altering a rule is MAJOR.)
  • New metatheory: progress_statement/preservation_statement extend to cover new variants; the statement shape (|-e:T => value \/ step; |-e:T /\ e-->e' => |-e':T) is frozen — extension means more canonical-forms/inversion cases, not a restated theorem.
  • Stretch features (references w/ store typing, System F, parametricity) land behind additive variants/judgments; their proofs are added to the soundness suite, never replacing the STLC ones.

28.7 MSRV policy

  • MSRV = Rust 1.78 (edition 2021), declared via rust-version in every crate; CI runs an MSRV job.
  • Raising MSRV is a MINOR bump (not patch), announced in the changelog with rationale; never raised in a patch release.
  • TCB constraint: tsp-kernel, tsp-term, tsp-syntax stay no_std + alloc — adding a std-only dependency to any of these is a breaking, owner-sign-off change (shrinks audit surface / portability of the TCB).
  • No nightly features in shipping crates; no MSRV-floor crate deps that exceed the stated 1.78.

28.8 Parity guarantee vs reference results (TAPL / Software Foundations)

  • The STLC + extensions (naturals, booleans, products, sums; references/System F stretch) and their Progress/Preservation statements are pinned to TAPL and the Software Foundations mechanized results; a parity/ fixture set encodes the canonical example terms, their TAPL-stated types, and reduction outcomes.
  • Parity is CI-enforced: infer/step/is_value on the parity terms must match the cited reference; a divergence fails the build and blocks release.
  • The kernel-checked Preservation/Progress proofs must remain accepted for every parity language variant; the Phase 13 conformance gate fails if any reference-sanctioned theorem stops checking.
  • Tester-vs-proof agreement is a standing guarantee: on every parity (sound) language, tsp-test yields only TestResult::Pass; only the Phase 12 broken-rule variant may produce a CounterExample. A Pass where the proof rejects (or vice versa) is a release-blocking inconsistency.
  • Deviations from the references (e.g. de Bruijn presentation vs named binders, evaluation-order choices) are documented once in the Glossary/Appendices and held stable across versions; we do not silently re-interpret a textbook result.

Testing & Verification Strategy

Test pyramid, bottom→top: unit (per-crate laws) → integration (STLC end-to-end) → dual-guard (kernel proofs ∧ Redex tester) → property → fuzz → conformance ledger → criterion gates. Every class maps to a §7 invariant; kernel soundness (TCB never accepts an ill-typed proof term) is the load-bearing one — all other tests are checked by the kernel and exist to find bugs before the kernel must.

29.1 Unit tests (per-crate, fast; cargo test -p <crate>)

Crate Target Asserts (one row = one fact) Fixtures
tsp-term shift shift(_,0,c)==id; shift(_,d,c) only touches free idx ≥ c; bound idx < c untouched Var(0), Var(5), Lam(_,Var 0/Var 1), App
tsp-term subst subst(t,j,s) replaces only Var(j); substituting a closed s ⇒ no capture; idx > j decremented beta-redex App(Lam(T,e),v)
tsp-term alpha_eq structural: Lam(T,Var 0)≡Lam(T,Var 0), ≠ Lam(T,Var 1); reflexive/symmetric/transitive renaming-invariant pairs
tsp-term is_value Lam,TmTrue,TmFalse,Zero,Succ(nv) ⇒ true; App,If ⇒ false numeric-value chains
tsp-rules infer one test per typing rule (T-Var, T-Abs, T-App, T-True/False, T-If, T-Zero/Succ); ill-typed ⇒ Err(TypeError) per-rule minimal terms
tsp-rules check_deriv valid tree ⇒ Ok; wrong rule name / premise-conclusion mismatch / arity ⇒ Err(DerivError) hand-built Derivation trees
tsp-rules step one test per reduction rule (E-AppAbs beta, E-App1/E-App2 congruence, E-IfTrue/False, E-If, E-Succ); value/stuck ⇒ None redexes + values
tsp-kernel whnf beta head-reduces; whnf idempotent on already-whnf; stops at head (no under-Pi reduction) App(Lam,_), Pi, neutral Var
tsp-kernel equal beta-conv terms equal; (eta if enabled) Lam(_,App(f,Var 0))≡f; distinct neutrals ≠; reflexive conv/non-conv KTerm pairs
tsp-kernel infer_type/check_type one test per kernel rule (K-Var, K-Sort Type:Kind, K-Pi formation, K-Lam, K-App with conversion); ill-typed KTerm ⇒ Err(KernelError) per-rule KTerm fixtures

Coverage gate: every enum arm of Type/Term/KTerm/Sort exercised; every rule in the STLC LangDef has ≥1 accept + ≥1 reject unit test.

29.2 Integration tests (tests/, cross-crate)

  • stlc_end_to_end: parse STLC LangDef (tsp-syntax) → parse object term → infer a Type → step to a value → assert value + final Type (preservation observed). Pipeline: tsp-cli path typecheck→step.
  • soundness_proofs_check: progress_statement/preservation_statement(lang) → build Proof{goal,term} → check_type(&Ctx::empty(), term, goal) returns Ok for STLC and each extension. This is the primary correctness signal.
  • lemmas_check: each of canonical_forms, inversion, weakening, substitution lemma produces a KTerm the kernel accepts at its KType — substitution lemma test cross-checks tsp-term subst/shift (same de Bruijn discipline; off-by-one breaks both).
  • cli_check_test: tsp check stlc.lang exit 0; tsp test stlc.lang --iters N reports Pass(N).

29.3 The dual-guard suite (the project's headline test)

Two independent oracles must agree on every language variant:

                 sound LangDef        broken LangDef (one bad rule)
  KERNEL guard   proof checks Ok      proof FAILS check_type (Err)
  REDEX guard    0 counterexamples    CounterExample(term,witness), shrunk
  VERDICT        SOUND (both agree)   UNSOUND (both flag) — required
  • Sound side: kernel accepts all soundness Proofs; tester returns only TestResult::Pass(_) over ≥10⁶ generated terms. Disagreement (kernel Ok but tester finds CEX, or vice-versa) ⇒ hard CI failure — signals a bug in encoding, kernel, or generator.
  • Broken side (Phase 12): for each known-broken variant (e.g. T-App dropping the domain-match premise), assert BOTH fire: check_type ⇒ Err(KernelError) AND tester ⇒ CounterExample whose Witness names the failed property (progress|preservation). A variant caught by only one guard ⇒ failing test (the dual-guard contract is violated).
  • Rationale: redundancy — the fast falsifier finds bugs in seconds; the kernel is authoritative. A variant the kernel "accepts" must be unrepresentable as a sound proof, never a TCB hole.

29.4 Property-based tests (proptest-style; tsp-test Generator)

Property Statement Iters
shift composition shift(shift(t,d1,c),d2,c) == shift(t,d1+d2,c) (compatible cutoffs) 10⁴
shift/subst commute the de Bruijn lemma subst(shift(t,1,c),c,s)==t-class identities 10⁴
subst of value closed subst(t,j,v) with v closed ⇒ result has no free Var(j) (no capture) 10⁴
infer == typing relation infer(ctx,e)==Ok(T) ⟺ a valid Derivation of Has-Type(ctx,e,T) exists (check_deriv Ok) 10⁵
step determinism step(e) is unique: at most one rule applies (Phase 4) 10⁵
generator soundness every term from Generator is well-typed: infer(∅,gen())==Ok(_) 10⁶
preservation (empirical) infer(∅,e)==Ok(T) ∧ step(e)==Some(e') ⇒ infer(∅,e')==Ok(T) 10⁶
progress (empirical) infer(∅,e)==Ok(_) ⇒ is_value(e) ∨ step(e).is_some() 10⁶

Shrinker minimizes any failing case to the smallest Term reproducing the Witness; shrink-stability asserted (re-shrinking a minimal CEX is a fixpoint).

29.5 Fuzzing (cargo fuzz; libFuzzer harnesses)

Harness Input Invariant (must hold ∀ input)
fuzz_kernel arbitrary KTerm (+ small Ctx) infer_type/check_type never panic; never accept an ill-typed term (soundness oracle: if it returns Ok(ty), re-check_type at ty must also be Ok); terminates (whnf/equal fuel-bounded)
fuzz_parse arbitrary bytes / UTF-8 tsp-syntax Parser returns Ok(LangDef) or Err(ParseError) — never panic, never infinite loop
fuzz_subst arbitrary (Term,j,s) shift/subst total: never panic, never produce dangling/negative index; output well-formed

fuzz_kernel is the TCB watchdog: any crash or false-accept is a §7 invariant-1 violation and blocks release. Corpus seeded from §29.1 fixtures + counterexamples; min 24 h CI fuzz on main, 15 min per-PR.

29.6 Conformance buckets + pass/fail/known-fail ledger

tsp-test/conformance/ledger.toml — one row per case; tsp check/tsp test drive it:

[stlc.progress]        expect = "pass"        # kernel Ok  + tester Pass
[stlc.preservation]    expect = "pass"
[ext.nat.preservation] expect = "pass"        # naturals
[ext.bool] [ext.prod] [ext.sum]  expect = "pass"
[ext.ref.store_typing] expect = "pass"        # Phase 12 references
[systemf.preservation] expect = "stretch"     # known-fail allowed if stretch unbuilt
[broken.tapp_no_dommatch] expect = "reject"   # BOTH guards must flag
[broken.eif_unguarded]    expect = "reject"

Verdicts: pass (both guards green), fail (regression → CI red), known-fail/stretch (tracked, non-blocking). Ledger diff vs baseline gates merges; a reject row flipping to pass (broken variant no longer caught) is a hard failure — the dual guard regressed.

29.7 Criterion performance-regression gates

cargo bench (criterion) vs Phase 13 targets; >10% regression vs committed baseline ⇒ CI fail:

Benchmark Target
kernel check_type of preservation proof (STLC) < 50 ms
kernel check_type of progress proof (STLC) < 30 ms
proof-term size (preservation, STLC) < 5 000 KTerm nodes
whnf/equal on a 1 k-node KTerm < 5 ms
tester throughput (check_progress+check_preservation) ≥ 50 k well-typed terms/s
Generator term/s (size ≤ 12) ≥ 200 k/s
TCB audit metric: tsp-kernel LOC ≤ 1 200 lines (size-bounded so it stays auditable)

29.8 Invariant traceability (test → §7 invariant)

§7 Invariant Guarding test class
I1 kernel never accepts ill-typed proof term (TCB soundness) §29.5 fuzz_kernel self-oracle; §29.2 soundness_proofs_check; §29.3 broken-side kernel guard
I2 subst/shift capture-avoiding & total §29.1 subst/shift units; §29.4 shift laws; §29.5 fuzz_subst
I3 infer agrees with the typing relation §29.4 infer==typing; §29.1 per-rule units; §29.2 check_deriv
I4 step deterministic & type-preserving §29.4 determinism + empirical preservation; §29.1 per-rule step
I5 soundness (progress ∧ preservation) holds for sound langs §29.3 dual-guard sound side; §29.6 ledger pass rows
I6 every known-broken variant is rejected by BOTH guards §29.3 broken side; §29.6 reject rows
I7 no panics on adversarial input §29.5 all harnesses

CI ordering: unit → integration → property (reduced iters) → conformance ledger → criterion gates per-PR; full-iter property + 24 h fuzz nightly. Green CI ⇒ kernel-checked proofs + zero tester counterexamples + ledger unchanged — the Phase 13 "done" gate.

Build & Release Engineering

Soundness rests on the kernel (tsp-kernel); build/release gates therefore exist to keep the TCB small, dependency-free, reproducible, fuzzed, and benchmarked. Every gate below is a hard CI requirement — a red gate blocks merge and blocks release.

30.1 Reproducible builds

Artifact Mechanism Invariant
Toolchain rust-toolchain.toml pinned channel = "1.78.0", components = ["clippy","rustfmt"], targets = ["x86_64-unknown-linux-gnu","aarch64-unknown-linux-gnu","thumbv7em-none-eabi"] identical compiler everywhere; MSRV == pinned channel
Lockfile Cargo.lock committed (CLI is a binary) --locked everywhere; PR that bumps a dep must bump lock in same commit
Build flags RUSTFLAGS="-D warnings", CARGO_INCREMENTAL=0, SOURCE_DATE_EPOCH set in release byte-stable release binaries; no incremental nondeterminism
Vendoring cargo vendor snapshot for offline/audited TCB rebuild kernel rebuildable from committed sources alone
  • --frozen in release (no network); --locked in CI. Drift in Cargo.lock → build fails, never silently re-solves.
  • [profile.release]: lto = "fat", codegen-units = 1, panic = "abort", strip = "symbols", opt-level = 3. panic = "abort" is acceptable: any kernel panic is a defect (see Failure Modes), not a recoverable condition.

30.2 Build & test matrix

EBNF of the CI job graph (per push / PR):

matrix   = job , { job } ;
job      = "fmt" | "clippy" | "test-stable" | "test-msrv"
         | "nostd-core" | "fuzz-smoke" | "bench-smoke" | "tcb-gate" | "deny" | "sbom" ;
Job Command (abridged) Pass criterion
test-stable cargo test --workspace --locked all 7 crates green incl. tsp-meta proof-checks
test-msrv same, toolchain 1.78.0 MSRV holds; no newer-stdlib usage
nostd-core cargo build -p tsp-term -p tsp-syntax -p tsp-kernel --target thumbv7em-none-eabi --no-default-features core crates are no_std + alloc clean (kernel must compile bare-metal)
fuzz-smoke cargo fuzz run kernel_infer -- -runs=100000 -max_total_time=60 no crash; nightly toolchain pinned separately for -Z sanitizer
bench-smoke cargo bench -p tsp-kernel -p tsp-test -- --test criterion harness builds + executes (no regression check in PR)
tcb-gate tokei tsp-kernel/src --output json → assert SLOC kernel SLOC <= 1500; trend posted to PR
  • tsp-cli excluded from nostd-core (it is std). Only tsp-term, tsp-syntax, tsp-kernel are gated no_std; tsp-rules/tsp-meta/tsp-test are alloc-tolerant but not bare-metal-gated.
  • cargo-hack: cargo hack check --feature-powerset -p tsp-term -p tsp-kernel — feature combos (e.g. eta, systemf, refs) build independently; no feature unification surprises in the TCB.

30.3 Lint & format gates

Gate Command Policy
fmt cargo fmt --all --check zero diff; non-negotiable
clippy cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets --locked -- -D warnings zero warnings
kernel pedantic cargo clippy -p tsp-kernel -- -W clippy::pedantic -D clippy::unwrap_used -D clippy::panic -D clippy::indexing_slicing TCB forbids unwrap/panic/raw index — infer_type/check_type/whnf/equal return Result<_, KernelError>, never panic
unsafe #![forbid(unsafe_code)] crate-attr on all 7 crates zero unsafe in the entire workspace; verified by grep gate

Rationale: indexing_slicing denial forces de Bruijn Var(usize) lookups in Ctx through checked access → an out-of-range index yields KernelError, never UB or panic (the gravest failure class is an unsound accept, the second is a panic on adversarial KTerm).

30.4 Supply-chain auditing (cargo-deny)

deny.toml enforced in CI:

Section Rule
advisories vulnerability = "deny", unmaintained = "deny", RUSTSEC DB checked
bans tsp-kernel MUST have ZERO external dependencies — enforced via per-crate dep allowlist ([bans.deny] any crate not in {std-of-the-day, the other tsp-* it imports}); kernel imports nothing outside core+alloc and tsp-syntax/none
licenses allow MIT OR Apache-2.0; deny copyleft in the TCB path
sources crates.io only; no git deps in release
  • The zero-dep kernel invariant is the load-bearing supply-chain rule: the trusted base must be auditable line-by-line with no transitive trust. cargo tree -p tsp-kernel must show only tsp-kernel itself (and at most tsp-syntax if a parser type is shared) — any third-party node fails the gate.
  • Dev/test deps (proptest/quickcheck-style generator backing tsp-test, criterion, libfuzzer-sys) live under [dev-dependencies]/fuzz crate only; they never enter the kernel or any release artifact.

30.5 SBOM

  • cargo auditable build --release embeds the dependency manifest into the tsp binary.
  • CI extracts + publishes SBOM: cargo auditable build && cargo audit bin target/release/tsp. Release attaches tsp.spdx.json.
  • SBOM diff posted per release; for tsp-kernel the SBOM is expected to be a singleton (asserted).

30.6 crates.io publication order & semver

Topological publish order (dependency-respecting):

tsp-term -> tsp-syntax -> tsp-rules -> tsp-kernel -> tsp-meta -> tsp-test -> tsp-cli
Stamping rule Detail
Workspace version single source [workspace.package] version inherited by all members (version.workspace = true)
Semver cargo-semver-checks gate on each crate before publish; any change to infer_type/check_type/KTerm/Sort/Ctx public API or proof-acceptance semantics is a MAJOR bump (kernel API == soundness contract)
Dry run cargo publish --dry-run -p <crate> --locked for all 7 in order before any real publish
Tag annotated git tag vX.Y.Z; release CI triggers on tag push only
  • Publication is sequenced because each later crate depends on the published version of earlier ones; a failed mid-sequence publish leaves a documented "published up to tsp-rules" state — resume, never re-tag.

30.7 Release artifacts (the tsp binary)

Target Triple Notes
Linux x86_64 x86_64-unknown-linux-gnu primary
Linux aarch64 aarch64-unknown-linux-gnu cross via cross/zig-cc
macOS arm64 aarch64-apple-darwin optional, same recipe
  • Per artifact: tsp-vX.Y.Z-<triple>.tar.gz + .sha256 + minisign/cosign .sig. Public key committed in RELEASING.md; signature verification documented for downstream auditors of the TCB.
  • Reproducibility check: release job rebuilds twice with fixed SOURCE_DATE_EPOCH and asserts identical sha256 before signing.

30.8 Workflow topology — .github/workflows/

File Trigger Jobs
ci.yml push, PR fmt, clippy(+pedantic kernel), test-stable, test-msrv, nostd-core, cargo-hack, tcb-gate, deny
fuzz.yml nightly cron + PR touching tsp-kernel/tsp-rules cargo fuzz run kernel_infer (long), kernel_whnf, parser targets; corpus cached; assert never accepts ill-typed proof term & never panics
bench.yml push to main, manual dispatch criterion: kernel check_type time, whnf time, tsp-test throughput; compare vs committed baseline; regression > threshold → fail
release.yml tag v* matrix cross-build artifacts, sbom (cargo-auditable), sign, GitHub Release, then publish 7 crates in locked order

30.9 Performance baselines (bench gate targets)

Metric Target Source
check_type on Preservation proof term < 50 ms tsp-meta golden proof
check_type on Progress proof term < 50 ms tsp-meta golden proof
whnf per redex < 1 us amortized kernel micro-bench
tsp-test throughput >= 10k well-typed terms/s generated + checked (check_progress+check_preservation) tsp-test Generator
Kernel peak RSS, full proof check < 64 MB criterion + heaptrack
TCB size tsp-kernel <= 1500 SLOC tcb-gate
  • bench baselines stored under benches/baseline.json; bench.yml fails on > 10% regression of kernel check_type (proof-checking latency is the user-visible cost of tsp check).

30.10 Pitfalls

  • Forgetting --locked/--frozen → CI silently re-solves deps → non-reproducible kernel build. Gate: assert Cargo.lock unchanged after cargo test.
  • A transitive dep sneaking into tsp-kernel via a shared type → breaks zero-dep TCB invariant. Gate: cargo-deny bans + cargo tree -p tsp-kernel assertion.
  • MSRV drift: a contributor uses a >1.78 stdlib API → test-msrv catches it, not test-stable. Keep both green.
  • panic = "abort" masking a kernel panic as a crash in fuzz → that is intended: fuzz.yml treats any abort as a defect (kernel must return KernelError, never abort on adversarial KTerm).
  • no_std regression: adding std:: use in tsp-kernel/tsp-term/tsp-syntax compiles on host but breaks nostd-core. Keep the bare-metal target job mandatory, not optional.

Performance Engineering

Performance is a correctness-adjacent concern here: the trusted base (tsp-kernel) is the hot path, and a soundness bug masquerading as a perf hack is the gravest failure (§7 INV-1). Rule: never trade kernel soundness for speed; optimize only behind property/conformance gates. All targets trace to the § 4 budgets.

31.1 Targets recap (from § 4) — what we measure against

ID Metric Target (§ 4) Scope Gate phase
M1 TCB size tsp-kernel ≤ 1500 LoC (audited) code, not runtime Phase 5 (## 17)
M2 Kernel check time — STLC soundness proof ≤ 50 ms (warm, single core) check_type on Progress+Preservation terms Phase 8–9 (## 20–21)
M3 Kernel check time — extended calculus (nat/bool/prod/sum) ≤ 250 ms Phase 10 proof bundle Phase 10 (## 22)
M4 Kernel check time — refs / System F stretch ≤ 1.0 s Phase 12 proof bundle Phase 12 (## 24)
M5 whnf single call (typical redex) ≤ 5 µs tsp-kernel::whnf Phase 5
M6 equal (conversion, typical Prop) ≤ 20 µs tsp-kernel::equal Phase 5
M7 infer (object STLC, depth ≤ 20) ≤ 10 µs tsp-rules::infer Phase 3 (## 15)
M8 step (single small-step) ≤ 2 µs tsp-rules::step Phase 4 (## 16)
M9 shift / subst (term ≤ 1k nodes) ≤ 8 µs / ≤ 12 µs tsp-term Phase 1 (## 13)
M10 Tester throughput ≥ 50k well-typed terms generated+checked / s tsp-test Phase 11 (## 23)
M11 Shrink-to-minimal ≤ 200 ms for a found CounterExample tsp-test::Shrinker Phase 11
M12 Peak RSS — full tsp check run ≤ 256 MB tsp-cli Phase 13 (## 25)
M13 Proof-term size — Preservation (STLC) ≤ 200 KB serialized KTerm tsp-meta Phase 8

Budgets are warm, single-threaded, on the reference box (§ 4). CI enforces a ±15% regression band; >15% slower fails the bench gate (§ 31.8).

31.2 Benchmark inventory — criterion micro-benches

One benches/*.rs per crate, criterion groups. Inputs are fixtures (§25 Phase 13 conformance corpus), not random per-run, so numbers are comparable across commits.

tsp-term/benches/binding.rs
  group "shift"   : shift(t, +1, 0)  over depth ∈ {4,16,64,256} ; subterm-share vs deep-copy
  group "subst"   : subst(t, 0, v)   v small/large ; worst case = v under k binders (k shifts)
  group "alpha_eq": alpha_eq(a,b) eq/neq ; early-exit on first node mismatch
tsp-rules/benches/check.rs
  group "infer"   : infer(ctx, e) STLC depth ∈ {1,5,20,80} ; ctx len ∈ {0,8,64}
  group "step"    : step(e)       redex-at-root vs redex-deep (congruence descent cost)
  group "deriv"   : check_deriv(d, rules) tree fanout ∈ {1,3} depth ∈ {4,16}
tsp-kernel/benches/kernel.rs            ← THE hot path
  group "whnf"        : whnf(t) 0/1/k beta-redexes ; Pi/Lam/App spines
  group "equal"       : equal(ctx,a,b) eq (full descent) vs neq (early-exit) ; beta-/eta-distinct
  group "infer_type"  : infer_type(ctx, proofterm) for canned Prop witnesses
  group "check_type"  : check_type(ctx, term, ty)  ← the trusted entry; drives § 4 budgets
tsp-test/benches/gen.rs
  group "generate"    : Generator::gen size ∈ {5,20,100} ; reject-rate of ill-typed candidates
  group "check_prop"  : check_progress(e) ; check_preservation(e) (one step + re-infer)
  group "shrink"      : Shrinker on synthetic CounterExample of size ∈ {20,100,400}

Conventions: black_box all inputs/outputs; Throughput::Elements for generate/check (terms·s⁻¹); iter_batched with SmallInput clone for subst/step (they consume owned Term); fixed RNG seed in generate so throughput is deterministic.

31.3 Macro-benches — full soundness proof checks

The decisive numbers: end-to-end tsp check <lang> <proof> through tsp-kernel. These are what § 4's kernel-check-time budget governs.

Macro-bench Pipeline Budget Notes
macro_stlc parse LangDef → encode Props (progress_statement/preservation_statement) → check_type full proof bundle ≤ 50 ms baseline; the load-bearing budget
macro_ext STLC + nat/bool/prod/sum, re-checked Progress+Preservation ≤ 250 ms Phase 10 deliverable
macro_refs + references (store + store typing) ≤ 1.0 s Phase 12; store typing inflates inversion proofs
macro_sysf + System F (All/TyVar, type-level Pi) ≤ 1.0 s Phase 12 stretch; type-in-type-free, more conversions
macro_broken deliberately-broken rule → expect KernelError (rejection path) ≤ 50 ms reject must be fast, not a hang (§ 31.7)

Macro-benches use criterion with sample_size(10) (long iterations) and a wall-clock budget assertion in CI (§ 31.8): the kernel-check-time gate is a hard regression check, not just a chart.

31.4 Profiling methodology — where the time goes

Tools: cargo flamegraph (release+debuginfo, lto = "thin" off for symbols), Linux perf record/report, perf stat for IPC/cache, dhat/valgrind --tool=massif for heap, cargo-llvm-lines to watch monomorphization bloat in the recursion-heavy kernel.

Expected hot-spot distribution (validate by flamegraph; if it drifts, investigate):

tsp check (kernel-dominated):
  ~55%  whnf            ← weak-head reduction during conversion
  ~25%  equal           ← structural compare + recursive whnf on subterms
  ~12%  infer_type/check_type traversal (Pi/Lam/App dispatch)
   ~5%  shift/subst inside whnf beta-step (de Bruijn renumbering)
   ~3%  Ctx lookups, alloc/free of Box<KTerm>

Headline: definitional equality (whnf+equal) is ~80% of kernel time. All kernel optimization effort targets there. perf stat on macro_stlc should show IPC ≥ 1.5 and L1-dcache miss ≤ 3%; higher misses point at pointer-chasing through Box<KTerm> spines (→ § 31.6 arena).

31.5 The cost of definitional equality (the dominant cost)

equal(ctx, a, b) decides conversion via repeated whnf. Cost model and tactics:

  • whnf strategy = lazy / call-by-name to head: reduce only the head redex chain, stop at the head constructor — never normalize arguments eagerly. Beta at the head: App(Lam(_,body), arg) → subst(body, 0, arg) then continue. This bounds work to the spine length, not the full normal form.
  • WHNF-on-demand inside equal: compare whnf a vs whnf b heads; if both heads = Pi/Lam/App(f,_), recurse on children, lazily whnf-ing each child only when its head is reached — avoids normalizing structurally-equal-already subterms.
  • Early-exit / syntactic fast path: before any reduction, try cheap KTerm structural pointer/shape equality (a and b identical Box via Rc::ptr_eq once sharing lands, else shallow tag+index compare). Hits frequently because encoded Props reuse shared sub-Props. Saves ~30–40% of equal calls reaching whnf on the STLC bundle.
  • eta: if enabled (Phase 5 "optionally eta"), eta is checked only at function type (Pi): compare f with Lam(_, App(shift(g,1,0), Var 0)). Eta adds ≤ ~8% to equal; keep it gated behind a feature = "eta" and bench both. Default proofs must check without eta to keep the TCB minimal and the budget tight.
  • Memoization (whnf cache) — guarded: an optional whnf memo keyed by structural hash of the KTerm, scoped to one check_type invocation (cleared between top-level calls; cache cannot outlive a Ctx). Rationale: the same Prop subterm (e.g. the typing judgment Has-Type(ctx,e,T)) is whnf'd many times across a Preservation proof. Measured win: macro_ext 250 ms → ~170 ms. Soundness rule: the memo is a pure function cache (whnf is deterministic) and is excluded from the audited TCB hot core by being feature-gated + differentially tested (§ 31.7): every cached result is re-derivable; conformance runs both cached and uncached and must agree bit-for-bit. If they ever diverge, the cache is disabled, not the checker.
  • No full normalization, ever: we never compute strong normal forms for conversion; that would risk non-termination on adversarial KTerms and blow the budget. WHNF + structural recursion only.

31.6 de Bruijn allocation, shifting, and structural sharing

shift/subst are total pure functions returning fresh Term/KTerm; naïvely they deep-copy. Allocation pressure is real because subst inside whnf runs on every beta step.

Technique Where Effect Risk / guard
Sharing-preserving shift tsp-term::shift, kernel beta if cutoff > max free index, shift is identity → return clone of Rc not deep copy requires Rc-backed terms; correctness unchanged (identity)
Rc<Term> / Rc<KTerm> structural sharing both term reprs subterms unchanged by subst are shared, not copied; subst [s/j]t only rebuilds the spine touching index j Rc is single-threaded (fine: kernel is !Send per call); cycle-free by construction (no graph, only trees)
Cow-style rebuild subst if a subtree contains no occurrence of j (track via a cheap free-var bitset / max-index annotation), return it unchanged annotation must be maintained on construction or recomputed; bench the bookkeeping vs the saved copies
Pointer-equality early-exit in alpha_eq/equal term compare Rc::ptr_eq ⇒ equal without descent only valid because terms are immutable post-construction
Index = usize, no boxing of leaves Var(usize), TyVar(usize) de Bruijn indices are inline; only recursive nodes box keep Type/Term/KTerm enum size small; large variants Boxed

Allocation target: subst on a 1k-node term touches O(depth-to-j) fresh nodes, not 1k — verify with dhat: allocations per subst should scale with substitution depth, not term size. With Rc sharing, macro_stlc heap allocations drop ~3–4×.

Off-by-one caveat (correctness ∩ perf): §8.2 — an off-by-one in shift breaks the substitution lemma. Sharing tricks (identity-shift skip) must be proven against the exhaustive shift/subst fixture suite (Phase 1) before they ship; a perf shortcut that mis-shifts is a soundness defect. Bench and correctness fixtures share inputs.

31.7 Reject-path & adversarial performance (no hangs)

The kernel must be fast at saying no and must never loop on hostile input (Phase 13 fuzzing).

  • Broken-rule rejection (macro_broken): check_type returns KernelError in ≤ 50 ms — rejection walks until the first conversion failure, then bails. Bench asserts it is not slower than acceptance (a slow reject = a soundness-relevant hang risk).
  • whnf step budget: optional fuel counter (feature kernel-fuel) bounds beta steps per whnf to detect pathological/non-terminating conversion on adversarial KTerms; exceeding fuel ⇒ KernelError::Diverged, never an infinite loop. Off in production proofs (well-typed proof terms are WHN-terminating by typing), on under fuzzing.
  • Stack-depth tail behavior (§ 31.9) — adversarial deep App spines must not stack-overflow the trusted checker.
  • Fuzz harness (cargo fuzz) tracks time-per-input; any input pushing infer_type past a p99.9 latency cliff is recorded as a perf bug even if it returns correctly.

31.8 CI regression gates & methodology hygiene

  • criterion --save-baseline main; PR runs --baseline main; CI fails if any micro-bench regresses > 15% or a macro-bench breaches its § 4 absolute budget (§ 31.3 table). Two consecutive samples required (de-noise).
  • Pinned toolchain (MSRV 1.78, fixed target-cpu), RUSTFLAGS="-C target-cpu=native" only on the reference box; CI box is fixed and quiescent (governor=performance, ASLR off for flamegraph runs, hyperthread sibling parked).
  • Profiles: [profile.bench] = [profile.release] + debug = 1; lto = "thin", codegen-units = 1, panic = "abort" for the tsp binary release (smaller, faster; the kernel must still return Result, never panic on valid input).
  • Report median + MAD, not mean (recursion has heavy-tailed GC-free alloc spikes). Macro-benches additionally report p99 because the kernel-check-time SLO is a tail commitment.

31.9 Tester throughput, shrink cost, and large-proof tail behavior

  • Generator throughput (≥ 50k terms/s): cost is dominated by the reject loop — generating then infer-checking candidates. Tactic: a type-directed Generator that builds well-typed terms by construction (synthesize from the goal type downward) instead of generate-and-filter, cutting reject-rate from ~70% to ~0% and raising throughput ~3×. check_progress/check_preservation per term = one step + one re-infer (≤ ~15 µs); throughput-bound by these, so infer/step micro-budgets (§ 31.1) directly cap tester speed.
  • Shrink cost (≤ 200 ms): Shrinker does delta-debugging over term structure — each candidate re-runs check_progress/check_preservation. Bound shrink to O(size · log) re-checks; cache failing-witness infer results within one shrink session. Cross-validation (Phase 11) checks tester verdicts vs kernel proofs on the sound language — that agreement run is itself a macro-bench (macro_crossval, ≤ 2 s).
  • Large proof-term tail: extended/stretch proofs (refs, System F) produce big KTerms (target ≤ 200 KB STLC → low-MB stretch). Risks at the tail:
    • Stack depth: deep Pi/App spines in infer_type/whnf/equal recurse ~proof-depth deep. Mitigation: keep recursion but set the tsp thread stack to ≥ 16 MB (std::thread::Builder::stack_size); if fuzzing finds depth > safe bound, convert whnf's beta-spine loop to an explicit work-stack (the one place recursion depth tracks input size, not proof structure). Trusted-core conversions are re-audited + differentially tested before merge.
    • Allocation tail: large proofs amortize well under Rc sharing (encoded judgments repeat); without sharing, macro_refs peak RSS would exceed the 256 MB gate — sharing is what keeps it ≤ 256 MB.
    • Conversion blow-up: the worst case is many distinct-but-convertible subterms forcing repeated whnf; the scoped memo (§ 31.5) caps this. p99 kernel-check time on the stretch bundle must stay ≤ 1.0 s; if a single proof term dominates, split it (lemma-by-lemma check_type) so the budget is per-lemma, not monolithic.

Optimization order of operations (do not reorder): (1) lock correctness via fixtures/conformance; (2) flamegraph to confirm whnf/equal dominance; (3) land Rc sharing + structural early-exit (no TCB risk); (4) gate the whnf memo behind differential testing; (5) only then consider eta/fuel tuning. Any step that touches the trusted core re-runs the full Phase 13 conformance + fuzz gate before it counts as "done."

Scalability Analysis

Scope: how cost (time, memory) scales along the four axes that grow in practice — proof-term size, object-language size, object-term size, tester budget — and where each axis hits a wall. The load-bearing constraint is the kernel: every other layer (tsp-term, tsp-rules, tsp-meta, tsp-test) ultimately routes through infer_type / check_type, so kernel cost dominates the soundness pipeline.

32.1 Cost model — the four scaling axes

Axis Symbol What grows it Dominant cost Asymptotic (no pathologies) Pathological case
Proof-term size P = nodes in a KTerm proof larger theorems, more lemma applications, deeper induction infer_type walk + per-App equal conversion checks O(P) traversal + Σ conv-cost non-normalizing/large-whnf conversions → super-linear
Object-language size L = rules in LangDef each new term former (nat, bool, prod, sum, ref, ∀) new canonical_forms/inversion cases in every proof O(L) cases per lemma; soundness re-proof O(L²) (each lemma × each former) cross-product of features (refs × sums)
Object-term size n = nodes in Term bigger generated/checked programs shift/subst/step/infer infer O(n); one subst O(n); step-to-value worst O(n²) substs repeated beta over large bodies
Tester budget s samples × b size-bound coverage of the falsifier s × (gen + check_progress + check_preservation) O(s · b) typical; gen up to O(b²) combinatorial well-typed gen blowup

P is the only axis inside the TCB; the others feed it indirectly (a bigger object program → a bigger derivation → a bigger proof term). Keep P small and the whole system scales.

32.2 Kernel scaling — the bottleneck (conversion / normalization)

  • infer_type is a single structural pass over the KTerm: O(P) nodes, but each App triggers equal(ctx, arg_ty, dom) and each conversion runs whnf. The honest cost is O(P) + Σ_apps cost(equal).
  • whnf is the lever and the hazard: in lambda-Pi, conversion of dependent types may force normalization whose size is not bounded by P (a redex can duplicate a large subterm). Worst case unbounded; typical metatheory proofs convert types that are already near-normal (Has-Type codes, small Pis), so cost(equal) is near-constant per App.
  • Empirical target (Phase 13 benchmarks): STLC soundness proof P ≈ 5k–20k KTerm nodes → check < 50 ms; extended calculus (nat/bool/prod/sum) P ≈ 50k–150k → < 500 ms; refs/System F stretch P ≤ 500k → < 5 s. Budget the kernel at ≥ 1M infer_type node-visits/s single-threaded.
  • Levers (kernel side):
    • whnf lazily (head-only) — never full-normalize when a head mismatch already decides equal; reject early on rigid-rigid clash before descending.
    • Memoize whnf results on KTerm identity (pointer/hash key) — repeated conversion of the same code (e.g. the Has-Type signature appears at every typing node) collapses from O(P · |code|) to O(P + |code|).
    • De Bruijn equal is pointer-cheap (== on usize); no name-comparison cost.
  • TCB-size ceiling (by design): the kernel stays ≈ 800–1500 LOC (KTerm, Sort, Ctx, infer_type, check_type, whnf, equal). This is a deliberate non-scaling: the trusted base must not grow with L or P, so auditability is O(1) in language/proof size. Adding object-language features adds code only in tsp-rules/tsp-meta (untrusted), never the TCB.

32.3 Object-language scaling (L) — proof combinatorics

  • Each new term former adds: one Term/Type variant, one (or few) typing Rule(s), one step rule, one canonical_forms clause (per value type), one inversion clause.
  • The substitution lemma and preservation proofs case-split on the typing derivation → one case per former → proof term grows O(L) per theorem; re-establishing the full suite (canonical_forms, inversion, weakening, subst, preservation, progress) over a calculus of L formers is O(L²) proof work (Phase 10/12 cost driver).
  • Feature interactions (refs + store typing, sums + refs) inflate inversion: store-typing premises thread through every Has-Type case → super-linear authoring effort, but kernel-check cost still tracks P only.
  • Mitigation: encode object judgments once as kernel inductive signatures (Phase 6); reuse eliminators across lemmas so adding a former extends a signature rather than rewriting proofs.

32.4 Object-term scaling (n) — tsp-term / tsp-rules

Op Cost Note
shift(t,d,c) O(n) single pass; pure
subst(t,j,s) O(n) + copies of s per occurrence capture-avoidance via shift, no extra factor
alpha_eq O(n) structural, de Bruijn — no renaming
infer O(n) one pass, Context lookup O(depth)
step (one) O(n) worst beta does one subst
step* to value up to O(n²) substs each redex re-traverses; acceptable for textbook terms (n ≤ 10³)

subst is the computational mirror of the substitution lemma; its O(n) shift discipline is exactly what the O(L) lemma proof must track — an off-by-one in shift breaks both, so the cost model and the proof share one invariant.

32.5 Tester scaling (tsp-test) — s samples, b size-bound

  • Per sample: Generator (type-directed) emits a well-typed Term of size ≤ b, then check_progress (one step/is_value) + check_preservation (one step + infer on the result). Per-sample cost O(b) to O(b²) (step-to-value).
  • Total falsification cost O(s · b · step-cost); targets: ≥ 50k well-typed terms/s at b ≤ 30, default s = 10⁶ for the soundness gate (Phase 11/13), < 30 s.
  • Combinatorial blowup is the gen hazard: naive growth of well-typed terms is exponential in b. Mitigations: type-directed generation (synthesize against a goal Type, never reject-sample — keeps acceptance ≈ 100%), per-branch size budgets, depth cap, and a Shrinker that is O(log) rounds × O(size) re-checks to reach a minimal CounterExample.
  • Tester vs proof: the tester pays O(s·b) for empirical coverage; the kernel pays O(P) once for exhaustive certainty. The dual guard means neither must scale to the other's regime — the broken-rule demo (Phase 12) is caught by the kernel at P-cost and by the tester at small b, s.

32.6 Parallelism

  • The kernel is pure (infer_type/check_type/whnf/equal are referentially transparent, no global state) → independent proofs check on independent threads with zero synchronization. tsp-cli check over multiple .lang files → embarrassingly parallel, near-linear speedup to core count.
  • Tester is embarrassingly parallel: partition s samples across T threads, each with an independent RNG seed → O(s·b / T); shrinking a found CounterExample is sequential but cheap.
  • Within one proof, infer_type is sequential (data-dependent recursion); parallelism is across proofs/lemmas, not inside the conversion check. The whnf memo table is the one shared structure — make it thread-local or a concurrent read-mostly cache to avoid contention.

32.7 Memory scaling

  • KTerm/Term are Box-linked trees: O(P) / O(n) words. Structural sharing of repeated subterms (the Has-Type code, common types) via Rc/hash-consing cuts the constant factor 5–20× on real proofs and is what makes the whnf memo keyed by identity sound.
  • Ctx is a de Bruijn list, O(depth); cloning for premises is O(depth), not O(P).
  • Peak memory target: STLC proof < 50 MB; extended < 256 MB; stretch (refs/F) < 1 GB resident. No arenas required at textbook scale; if P > 10⁶, switch to an arena + indices to flatten allocation.

32.8 Bottleneck ledger

Bottleneck Axis Symptom Mitigation
Non-normalizing / large whnf conversion P kernel check super-linear / hang head-only whnf, early rigid clash, memo, conversion budget (abort + report instead of loop)
Conversion of duplicated big codes P repeated equal on same type hash-cons + whnf memo
O(L²) re-proof on extension L proof authoring explosion signature reuse, shared eliminators
Combinatorial well-typed gen b low throughput, biased samples type-directed gen, per-branch budgets, depth cap
step* O(n²) substs n slow tester on big terms small b, cap reduction steps per sample
