Memo table contention parallel sublinear thread scaling thread-local memo / read-mostly concurrent cache

The single non-negotiable scaling invariant: the TCB does not grow with L or P. Everything that must scale lives outside the kernel; the kernel stays small, pure, parallel, and audited — its constant size is the foundation the soundness guarantee rests on.

Mental Models & First Principles

The core intuitions that prevent architectural mistakes. Each model is numbered MM1…MM8 and maps directly to a load-bearing design decision.

MM1 — The TCB Is a Trust Multiplier, Not a Feature Set. Every line inside tsp-kernel multiplies the blast radius of an undetected bug: one unsound line in infer_type silently validates every false theorem the rest of the workspace produces. The kernel is not a place to add features; it is the place where the cost of code is highest. The correct mental model: adding 10 lines to the kernel is architecturally more expensive than adding 1000 lines to tsp-meta. Without this model, a developer adds a convenience rule (e.g. K-Let) straight into the kernel and silently doubles the audit surface. Applied in §17 Phase 5 (kernel budget) and §7 INV-12 (TCB boundary minimality).

MM2 — de Bruijn Indices Are a Substitution-Lemma Contract. Var(n) does not mean "the nth variable in scope" — it means "count n binders outward to find the binding site." The shift(t, d, cutoff) function adjusts free indices that cross a binder boundary. The substitution lemma (Phase 7) is a theorem about subst and shift, so any off-by-one in the cutoff arithmetic produces a provably-false lemma that check_type will accept — the bug is in the statement, not the proof. The mental model: shift is the mathematical dual of a lambda abstraction; every time a binder is introduced, indices must shift to stay consistent. Without it, a developer "fixes" a capture bug by patching subst alone and never notices the lemma statement drifted too. Applied in §13 Phase 1 (shift/subst laws) and §19 Phase 7 (substitution lemma).

MM3 — Propositions Are Types, Proofs Are Programs. A Prop is literally a KType (a KTerm of Sort::Type). A Proof is a KTerm that the kernel accepts at that KType. "Does this proof term typecheck?" and "is this proposition true?" are the same question. The consequence: there is no second logic engine, no separate evaluator — only check_type. Mistaking KTerm for a data structure rather than a proof term is the source of encoding bugs in tsp-meta. Applied in §17 Phase 5 (Curry-Howard mapping) and §18 Phase 6 (judgments-as-types encoding).

MM4 — The Kernel Decides Definitional, Not Propositional, Equality. equal(ctx, a, b) answers "are a and b the same type up to beta-reduction?" — not "are they provably equal by some equational theory." A proof term for preservation does not need to prove that two types are equal; it needs to be at a type where the kernel can compute them equal via whnf. This means proof terms for the substitution lemma grow with the number of beta steps needed to witness the conversion, not with the depth of the equational proof. Without this model, a developer expects equal to discharge arbitrary propositional equalities and is surprised when a semantically-true-but-not-beta-convertible pair is rejected. Applied in §17 Phase 5 (whnf/equal) and §7 INV-11.

MM5 — The Dual Guard Is an Independence Requirement. tsp-meta and tsp-test are not redundant — they check different things. A kernel proof establishes certainty for the modeled language; a PLT-Redex-style test establishes that the executable infer/step is consistent with the model. A bug in the encoding of progress_statement can produce a vacuously provable but semantically empty proposition that the tester will immediately find as a counterexample on the sound language. Agreement between the two guards is stronger evidence than either alone. Without it, a reviewer treats a passing kernel proof alone as sufficient and never runs the falsification suite that would catch an encoder bug. Applied in §7 INV-10 and §24 Phase 12 (broken-rule demonstration).

MM6 — Small-Step Semantics Exposes Stuckness; Big-Step Hides It. Progress says: a well-typed term is either a value or can step. This is only expressible in a small-step formalism because big-step e ⇓ v has no notation for "stuck before reaching a value." Evaluation contexts encode congruence without case-splitting on every intermediate form; adding a new term former means adding one evaluation context clause, not patching every congruence rule. This is why step(e) -> Option<Term> is the right abstraction: None is the stuck-term witness Progress forbids. Without this model, a developer picks big-step semantics and then cannot state progress at all. Applied in §16 Phase 4 (the stepper) and §21 Phase 9 (Progress).

MM7 — Type-Directed Generation Is Asymptotically Superior to Filter-and-Reject. The density of well-typed terms among all terms of size n falls exponentially with n under a naive grammar. At size 20, fewer than 1 in 10^4 random terms may typecheck. Type-directed generation builds a term for a given type by construction, maintaining 100% acceptance rate regardless of size. The mental model: a Generator is a proof-term synthesizer that works in reverse — given a goal Type, produce a Term that infer will accept at that type, without ever calling infer on the result. Without it, a developer builds a generate-and-filter tester that times out before finding a single interesting counterexample. Applied in §23 Phase 11 (Redex-style testing harness) and §7 INV-9.

MM8 — The Substitution Lemma Is the Bottleneck of Every Extension. Every time a new term former is added to the object language (nat, bool, prod, sum, ref, ∀), the substitution lemma gains one new case. Preservation's beta/App case always reduces to "apply the substitution lemma." This means the marginal cost of extending the calculus is dominated by adding one substitution case and one preservation case — O(L) per former, O(L²) total over L formers. Architecturally, the lemma library is the load-bearing layer that must be designed to accommodate extension without touching the TCB. Without this model, a developer underestimates the cost of "just adding references" and is surprised when the substitution lemma's case count explodes. Applied in §19 Phase 7 (substitution lemma) and §22 Phase 10 (language extension).

Comparative Architecture Table

How this project's design compares to five real systems that tackle adjacent problems. Each row is a real system; columns compare the key architectural axes.

System TCB boundary Kernel calculus Proof term representation Binding Assurance model How it differs from tsp-*
Twelf (Carnegie Mellon, 1999–) The Twelf meta-engine (~20k LOC) Edinburgh LF (lambda-Pi, same as tsp-kernel) LF terms; %theorem metatheorems as relations; check mode verification Locally-nameless + higher-order abstract syntax LF type-checking + coverage/termination/mode meta-theorems Twelf is a logic-programming engine on top of LF; tsp-kernel is a pure checker with no search/mode machinery. Twelf's TCB includes coverage + totality checkers; tsp-* deliberately excludes these.
Coq (INRIA, 1989–) The kernel in kernel/ (~15k LOC of OCaml) Calculus of (Co)Inductive Constructions (CIC); universe polymorphism Explicit Term (de Bruijn) with Evar metavariables + universe constraints de Bruijn indices for all binders Tactic engine producing proof terms; Qed submits to kernel CIC is far larger than lambda-Pi; the tactic engine is outside Coq's kernel but inside the installed binary. tsp-kernel's target is ≤800 LoC vs Coq's ~15k kernel. No metavariables, no universes beyond Type:Kind.
Lean 4 (Microsoft Research / CMU, 2021–) The kernel in Lean.Kernel (~8k LOC of C++) Calculus of Constructions + inductive types + quotient types + propositional extensionality Explicit Expr (de Bruijn) with MVar + level metavariables de Bruijn indices Elaborator + tactic framework outside kernel; isDefEq inside Lean 4 kernel supports propext + funext; tsp-kernel does neither. Lean's isDefEq includes whnf + definitional reducibility (let/const unfolding + universe reduction); tsp's equal is beta-only.
Agda (Chalmers, 2007–) No hard kernel boundary; whole TypeChecking/ module (~50k LOC of Haskell) is trusted Martin-Löf type theory + inductive families + sized types + guarded recursion Implicit Term (de Bruijn) with metavariables de Bruijn + named pretty-print layer Full type-checking with universe-polymorphic infer; separate --safe flag Agda has no isolated minimal kernel; soundness is argued over the whole type-checker. tsp-kernel is the minimal-kernel philosophy taken seriously: ≤800 LoC, no metavariables, no universe polymorphism.
LambdaPi (P3) (Löh-McBride-Swierstra, 2010) The Haskell checker (~300 LOC) lambda-Pi (same as tsp-kernel) Explicit Value/Neutral in Haskell, NbE-style de Bruijn Pedagogical; no conformance suite The direct intellectual ancestor of tsp-kernel. tsp adds: strict Rust (no laziness), Result error handling, no_std, a whnf step-budget for adversarial inputs, fuzz harness, and a full 7-crate workspace.

Twelf made logic-programming execution (search over %mode/%worlds-annotated relations, coverage/termination checking) part of its trusted engine, not just LF type-checking. That decision wins whenever the goal is discovering a metatheorem via relational search rather than checking a hand-built proof term — Twelf can find a derivation tsp-kernel would only ever verify. tsp-* rejects this because a search engine in the TCB is exactly the enlarged trust surface DP-1 forbids.

Coq made its kernel a full Calculus of (Co)Inductive Constructions with a large tactic engine layered outside it. That decision wins when the project's payoff is a big, evolving library of interactively developed proofs (a general-purpose proof assistant) where authoring ergonomics dominate and a ~15k-LOC audited kernel is an acceptable, amortized cost across thousands of downstream proofs. tsp-*'s single-purpose scope (STLC + bounded extensions) can't amortize that kernel size.

Lean 4 made its kernel support propositional/functional extensionality and quotient types, with isDefEq folding in let/const unfolding and universe reduction. That decision wins when the metatheory needs to reason up to extensional equalities (e.g., quotients of syntax, or proofs that treat definitionally-different-but-extensionally-equal functions as interchangeable) — exactly the case for a large mathlib-style corpus, not for a from-scratch STLC soundness proof where beta-only equal already suffices.

Agda made the choice to have no isolated kernel at all — the whole ~50k-LOC type-checker (universe-polymorphic inference, sized types, guarded recursion) is trusted uniformly. That decision wins in a research setting where the type theory itself is the object of study and evolves faster than a frozen kernel boundary could track — auditability is sacrificed for type-theoretic expressiveness and rapid experimentation, the opposite tradeoff from DP-1's tiny-TCB discipline.

LambdaPi (the paper this project's kernel design descends from) made the choice to stay a ~300-line pedagogical Haskell artifact with no adversarial hardening, no no_std boundary, and no conformance/fuzz suite. That decision wins as a teaching artifact where the reader's attention should stay on the bidirectional algorithm itself, unclouded by production concerns — exactly why P3 is Required Reading (§5.2) rather than a dependency: tsp-kernel inherits its shape but adds the total-function, fuzzed, no_std discipline a shippable trusted base needs.

Back-of-Envelope Capacity Model

All numbers are derived from the §4 budgets and the Performance Engineering §31.1/§32.2 performance targets. Formulas are shown; assume single-threaded unless noted.

# Quantity Formula / derivation Estimated value Constraint source
1 KTerm nodes in STLC preservation proof Each typing derivation node generates ≈3 kernel nodes (App + premise + type); STLC has ≈50 derivation steps → 50 × 3 + ≈100 conversion witnesses ≈ 5k–20k nodes ADR-20, §32.2
2 Kernel check_type time per node at budget 50 ms budget / 20k nodes ≈ 2.5 µs / node C3 (§4)
3 infer_type/check_type node-visits per second 1 / 2.5 µs ≥ 400k visits/s (target: ≥1M/s per §32.2) §31.1, §32.2
4 whnf calls per check_type invocation 2 whnf calls per equal call; ≈1 equal per App node; 20k nodes × ~30% are App → 6k equal calls ≈ 12k whnf calls per proof check §31.4 (~55% time in whnf)
5 shift/subst calls per beta step inside whnf 1 shift + 1 subst per beta; STLC proofs have ≈200–500 beta redexes in conversion ≈ 500–1000 shift/subst calls per proof check §31.6
6 Heap allocations per subst on a 1k-node term subst rebuilds only the path from root to each occurrence of Var(j); average path length ≈ log₂(1000) ≈ 10 ≈ 10 × (# occurrences of j) fresh nodes §31.6
7 Tester throughput at 50k terms/s with check_progress + check_preservation Each check ≈ 1 step (≤2 µs) + 1 infer (≤10 µs) = ≤12 µs; throughput = 1 / 12 µs ≈ 83k checks/s ≥ 50k target §31.1
8 Time for 10^6 falsification samples (Phase 13 gate) 10^6 / 83k ≈ 12 s; with type-directed gen overhead factor 2× ≈ 25–30 s §32.5
9 Peak RSS for STLC proof check 20k KTerm nodes × 5 words/node × 8 bytes/word + Ctx stack O(50 entries) + whnf memo ≈ 800 KB ≈ 1–5 MB (well under 256 MB C9 budget) C9 (§4)
10 tsp-kernel LoC budget utilization whnf+equal ≈400 LoC; infer_type/check_type ≈250 LoC; KTerm/Sort/Ctx ≈100 LoC; errors ≈50 LoC ≈800 LoC total (matches DP-1 target) C1 (§4)
11 CI conformance runtime (all phases green) cargo test ≈30s; conformance suite ≈10s; fuzz-smoke (1000 iters) ≈5s; bench-regression ≈60s ≈105 s total CI runtime ADR-23
12 Worst-case conversion blowup (non-normalizing KTerm) Step budget 2^20 = ≈1M steps × 2.5 µs/step ≈ 2.6 s before KernelError::Diverged (within C16's 10s budget) C14 (§4), DP-15

Hard Questions (Self-Examination)

These are staff-level questions a reviewer, hiring bar, or design review would ask. A practitioner who has internalized this blueprint can answer all of them precisely.

  1. The substitution lemma's kernel proof term mirrors tsp-term::subst line-for-line. If there is an off-by-one bug in shift that is compensated by an equal off-by-one in the lemma's encoding, can the kernel detect it? What is the only mechanism that would catch such a paired error? (Synthesize §7 INV-7 and §19 Phase 7.)

  2. Why does whnf reduce only to weak-head normal form rather than full normal form, and what would go wrong if equal instead normalized both sides fully before comparing? Give a concrete KTerm where full normalization would change the termination behavior. (Synthesize §17 Phase 5 and Performance Engineering §31.5.)

  3. infer_type and check_type are the trusted functions. progress_statement and preservation_statement are untrusted producers. If preservation_statement returns a KTerm that is the proposition "True" (trivially provable), check_type will accept the proof. What is the only mechanism in this architecture that would catch this vacuity, and in which phase does it fire? (Synthesize §18 Phase 6 and §17 Phase 5; cf. Failure Modes & Recovery F9.)

  4. The dual-guard design says a broken rule must be caught by BOTH guards. Construct a minimal broken typing rule where the PLT-Redex tester would find a counterexample but the kernel proof would continue to typecheck. Explain which architectural invariant this scenario violates and what the fix is. (Synthesize §7 INV-10 and §24 Phase 12.)

  5. The Shrinker must preserve the falsified property (INV-14). Describe a shrinking strategy that could produce a term that no longer fails the property — a "spurious minimization" — and explain which property of the Shrinker's inner loop prevents it. (Synthesize §7 INV-14 and §23 Phase 11.)

  6. tsp-kernel is no_std + alloc. What concrete classes of unsoundness does this constraint prevent beyond the obvious "no I/O"? Name three. (Synthesize §17 Phase 5 and §9 ADR-06/ADR-22; cf. Threat Model T3/T6.)

  7. The whnf step budget is a named constant. If a well-typed preservation proof term genuinely requires more than the budget to normalize (e.g., a deeply nested conversion in System F), what is the observable symptom, what does check_type return, and what is the correct operational response? (Synthesize §17 Phase 5 and §7 INV-2; cf. Threat Model T2.)

  8. Phase 6's encoding uses shallow LF-style judgments-as-types rather than a deep embedding. Explain the adequacy obligation: what must be true of the encoding for check_type's acceptance of a proof term to imply the mathematical statement of preservation? (Synthesize §18 Phase 6 and §5 Required Reading P2.)

  9. The tsp-test generator is type-directed. A naive implementation might call infer on each generated term to verify it. Why would that be an architectural error? What property of the generator makes calling infer as a post-hoc validator unnecessary and detecting if it were ever needed? (Synthesize §23 Phase 11 and §7 INV-9; cf. Mental Models & First Principles MM7.)

  10. equal(ctx, a, b) is called inside check_type. Both take a &Ctx. Explain why equal must receive the same context that check_type is operating under, and give a concrete counterexample where passing an empty context to equal would cause check_type to accept an ill-typed term. (Synthesize §17 Phase 5 and §7 INV-11.)

  11. The kernel uses de Bruijn indices for KTerm variables. tsp-term uses de Bruijn indices for Term variables. These are two independent nameless namespaces. When tsp-meta encodes a Term inside a KTerm proof term, what is the precise invariant that must hold between the two index namespaces, and how would a violation manifest? (Synthesize §18 Phase 6 two-zone de Bruijn and §7 INV-3/INV-4.)

  12. cargo-fuzz asserts two properties on the kernel: (a) no panic, and (b) no false accept. Property (b) requires a notion of "true type" for the fuzz-generated KTerm — but the kernel cannot be queried twice with a different oracle. What is the operationalized version of property (b) actually checking, and why is it a sound approximation? (Synthesize §25 Phase 13 and §9 ADR-19; cf. Threat Model T1.)

  13. The substitution lemma's Lam case requires weakening. Trace exactly which KTerm constructor sequence appears in the proof term for the Lam case of the substitution lemma, naming each K-rule used. (Synthesize §19 Phase 7 and §17 Phase 5 K-rules.)

  14. A reviewer proposes adding a K-Let rule to tsp-kernel to speed up certain proof terms by avoiding explicit beta-substitution. What is the TCB-audit cost of this change, what is the minimal set of re-verification steps required before merging, and is there an alternative that avoids changing the kernel? (Synthesize §17 Phase 5 and Compatibility & Versioning §28.3.)

  15. The conformance suite in Phase 13 asserts that tester verdicts and kernel proofs agree on the sound language. If a new object-language extension (say, references) is added and the tester finds a counterexample while the kernel still accepts the proof, enumerate all possible root causes in order of likelihood, and describe the diagnostic procedure for each. (Synthesize §22 Phase 10 and §25 Phase 13.)

  16. Why is Type:Kind (two universes, not one) the minimum stratification required for the STLC metatheory encoding? What goes wrong with a single-universe Type:Type system (Girard's paradox), and why is the STLC proof development safe from it? (Synthesize §17 Phase 5 and §9 ADR-05.)

  17. The Shrinker outputs a minimal CounterExample(Term, Witness). What property of "minimal" is actually guaranteed — lexicographic size, canonical form, or something else — and how does this property affect the usefulness of the counterexample as a regression fixture? (Synthesize §23 Phase 11 and §7 INV-14.)

  18. Explain why tsp-rules::infer and tsp-kernel::infer_type are both bidirectional but for different reasons. What would happen if tsp-rules::infer used full Hindley-Milner unification instead? (Synthesize §15 Phase 3 and §17 Phase 5.)

  19. The whnf memo is scoped to one check_type invocation and cleared between calls. Why is a global memo (persisting across top-level check_type calls) unsound, and under what conditions would it be safe to extend the memo scope? (Synthesize §17 Phase 5 and Performance Engineering §31.5.)

  20. The tsp binary has four subcommands: check, test, typecheck, step. Of these, exactly one involves the TCB. The others are all untrusted. Precisely characterize which subcommand(s) invoke tsp-kernel and in which call-stack position, and explain what a bug in a non-TCB subcommand can and cannot do to the soundness guarantee. (Synthesize §3 Architectural Overview and §11 Core Schema 11.7.)

  21. The broken-rule conformance fixture is checked into the repository. Describe the exact CI assertion made about this fixture: what does conformance/run.rs assert, what does it call on the kernel, and what does it call on tsp-test? What would a false-positive CI green mean? (Synthesize §24 Phase 12 and §25 Phase 13.)

  22. Phase 13 fuzzes the kernel with cargo-fuzz. What is the relationship between the fuzz corpus and the conformance suite? Could the fuzz harness replace the conformance suite, or vice versa? Justify your answer. (Synthesize §25 Phase 13 and Testing & Verification Strategy §29.5/§29.6.)

  23. The criterion bench CI gate fails on a >15% regression of check_type latency. A new proof term for System F parametricity passes Phase 12 correctness checks but causes a 20% regression in macro_stlc. Is this a blocker? What is the correct escalation path? (Synthesize §24 Phase 12 and Performance Engineering §31.8.)

  24. alpha_eq(a, b) is defined as structural equality on de Bruijn terms and is equivalent to PartialEq. The blueprint says alpha_eq is "kept as a named function" for documentation + property-test purposes. Write a property-based test that could detect a divergence between alpha_eq and the derived PartialEq implementation, and explain under what circumstance such a divergence would indicate a real semantic bug. (Synthesize §13 Phase 1 and §7 INV-4.)

  25. The Phase 7 substitution lemma is described as "the crux." Given only the statement of preservation (subject reduction), derive which lemmas must exist before preservation can be proved, and show the logical dependency chain from shift correctness through to the final preservation_statement that check_type accepts. (Synthesize §19 Phase 7 and §20 Phase 8.)

Alternative Architectures (Roads Not Taken)

Each alternative below was considered and rejected. The rejection is recorded here, not in the ADR table, because these are structural alternatives that would have changed multiple ADRs simultaneously rather than a single decision.

Alternative A — Coq-Based Development (Extract to Rust)

  Coq source (.v files)
      |
      | Coq kernel (CIC, ~15k LoC OCaml)
      v
  Certified Preservation / Progress theorems
      |
      | extraction + FFI bridge
      v
  Rust executable (extracted OCaml or Rust via coq-of-rust)

Rationale for rejection: Extraction bridges the correctness gap via an unverified extractor (Coq's extraction to OCaml is trusted but the OCaml→Rust bridge is not). The project's learning goal — owning every layer from inference rule to kernel check — is defeated if the kernel is Coq's. The resulting "Rust" binary trusts Coq's kernel, OCaml's runtime, and the extraction pass: three trust points instead of one. The TCB cannot be audited by a Rust developer without Coq expertise.

When this wins: A production certified compiler (CompCert style) where the primary value is a deployable, extracted artifact that inherits Coq's formal soundness. Not a learning-oriented proof framework.

Alternative B — Typed-Racket / PLT Redex as the Ground Truth

  PLT Redex model (Racket)
      |--- define-language / reduction-relation (STLC + extensions)
      |--- redex-check (randomized; automatic counterexample search)
      |--- term → term' judgment traces
      v
  Rust reimplementation cross-checked against Redex oracle
  redex-check → finds counterexample → Racket shrinks → outputs Term as S-expr
                                                             |
                                                          FFI/stdio pipe
                                                             |
                                                        Rust tsp-test

Rationale for rejection: PLT Redex gives strong empirical evidence but no formal proof. The project requires a machine-checked proof term, not just "no counterexample found in 10^6 trials." Redex has no notion of a proof kernel or proof term; adding one would require building exactly what this project builds. The cross-language FFI pipe is an audit liability.

When this wins: Rapid language prototyping where rules change daily and the overhead of maintaining a formal proof would invalidate the proof on every iteration. Redex is the right tool for exploring semantics before committing to a formal development.

Alternative C — Logical Relations / Step-Indexed Semantics

  Object language: STLC + extensions
      |
      | define: V_n[[T]] (values safe for n steps at type T)
      |         E_n[[T]] (expressions safe for n steps at type T)
      v
  Fundamental lemma: (|- e:T) → e ∈ E_ω[[T]]   ← machine-check this

Real system embodying it: RustBelt (Jung et al., POPL 2018) proves type-soundness-plus-safety for a realistic subset of Rust using Iris's step-indexed logical relations in Coq — exactly the V_n[[T]]/E_n[[T]] machinery sketched above, scaled to a language with unsafe code and ownership.

Rationale for rejection: Logical relations are the right tool for stronger properties (e.g., parametricity, full abstraction, contextual equivalence) but add significantly more proof obligation for basic progress+preservation. The step-indexed version requires an auxiliary numeric argument in every proof step, bloating proof terms and the kernel. For STLC soundness, the syntactic progress+preservation approach (Wright-Felleisen, P4) is strictly simpler and more auditable. The kernel would need a richer type theory (Sigma types for the step index) or a bespoke induction principle.

When this wins: Soundness proofs for impredicative type systems (System F with full parametricity) or for concurrent/separation-logic extensions where the syntactic approach fails — RustBelt's target (unsafe pointer aliasing, interior mutability) is exactly the regime the syntactic approach cannot reach. Step-indexing is also the right model for blame in gradual typing.

Alternative D — Isabelle/HOL + Nitpick Integration

  Isabelle/HOL theory files
      |--- inductive Has_Type, Step (standard HOL encoding)
      |--- Nitpick (counterexample search; model-finding)
      |--- Isar proof scripts → kernel-checked HOL proofs
      v
  Machine-checked progress + preservation in HOL

Rationale for rejection: HOL has no dependent types — encoding judgments-as-types (the LF/propositions-as-types discipline) requires a shallow embedding and a universe of encodings that adds proof overhead vs. the lambda-Pi approach. Nitpick provides counterexample search analogous to tsp-test but is more powerful (finite model-finding) while being more opaque (the counterexample is a Isabelle model, not a concrete Term). The learning goal of building a lambda-Pi kernel from scratch is not served by delegating to Isabelle's kernel.

When this wins: When the team is already expert in Isabelle/HOL and the object language is first-order (no binding), or when the metatheory requires classical logic (excluded middle) that constructive type theory rejects.

Deferred Features Register

Every feature gated behind PhaseDeferred(...) in §1.7 (the Phase-0 type shells present but not activated), with the resolving section where it lands and where its design tradeoff is already argued.

Feature PhaseDeferred(...) gate Resolving anchor Notes
System F (Type::All, Type::TyVar; type abstraction/application) PhaseDeferred("FINAL_BLUEPRINT section 24") §24 (Phase 12), 12.8 Edge Cases "System-F alternative path" Activated as Phase 12's stretch alternative to references; subst at the type level reuses the tsp-term shift discipline (12.8), and the planted broken-rule bug becomes a bad T-TApp substitution instead of T-Deref-Broken.
References (store + store typing; Type::Ref) PhaseDeferred("FINAL_BLUEPRINT section 24") §24 (Phase 12), the phase's primary content This is the default Phase 12 stress extension (12.1–12.10 in full); store typing Sigma, step_st, and the T-Deref-Broken dual-guard demo are specified there end-to-end.
Parametricity (relational, over System F) PhaseDeferred("FINAL_BLUEPRINT section 24") §24 (Phase 12) gate; Compatibility & Versioning "Stretch features" clause; Glossary entry Parametricity A stretch-of-a-stretch layered on System F (tsp-meta only, per 1.7's touch column); not required for the Phase 13 soundness gate — Alternative Architectures C records why full logical-relations parametricity is rejected as the primary proof technique.
eta in definitional equality PhaseDeferred("FINAL_BLUEPRINT section 17") §17 (Phase 5), 5.1 Objectives + 5.6 Pitfalls + 5.8 Edge Cases equal's const ETA: bool flag is built in Phase 5 but defaults off; turning it on is a MAJOR semver bump (Compatibility & Versioning §28.3) because it changes which proof terms check_type accepts.
Big-step semantics (alongside small-step) PhaseDeferred("FINAL_BLUEPRINT section 16") §16 (Phase 4) gate; ADR-11 (§9) rejection rationale; MM6 (Mental Models) ADR-11 records why small-step (not big-step) is the locked choice — big-step cannot state "stuck," which Progress requires; MM6 restates the same insight as a mental model. No big-step relation is implemented; the gate gives an escape hatch only if a future logical-relations proof style (Alternative Architecture C) is ever adopted.

Closure rule: a PhaseDeferred entry is resolved when its gated phase either activates the feature (References, by default, in Phase 12) or explicitly documents the non-activation rationale (System F / Parametricity / eta / Big-step, all argued above); no gate is left silently unaddressed at Phase 13 "done."

Glossary

Terms ordered roughly: metatheory → semantics → binding → kernel/LF → encoding → testing → code identifiers. Each entry: precise definition + where it lives in the codebase (crate / type / fn) + the load-bearing pitfall.

Term Definition (implementation-relevant)
Type soundness "Well-typed terms never get stuck": for closed e with |- e : T, evaluation either diverges or reaches a value, never a stuck term. The top-level theorem; corollary of Progress + Preservation. Stated in tsp-meta, proved as a kernel-checked Proof.
Progress If |- e : T (closed) then is_value(e) OR ∃ e'. e --> e'. Proof by induction on the typing derivation; uses the canonical forms lemma to fix the shape of each subterm's value. preservation's twin; progress_statement(lang) -> KTerm.
Preservation aka subject reduction. If |- e : T and e --> e' then |- e' : T. Induction on typing/step derivation; the beta/elimination case discharges via inversion + substitution lemma. preservation_statement(lang) -> KTerm.
Subject reduction Synonym for Preservation (never used as a distinct concept here).
Canonical forms lemma A closed value of type Bool is TmTrue/TmFalse; of Arrow(S,T) is a Lam; of Nat is Zero/Succ; etc. Inverts "value at type" → constructor. canonical_forms in tsp-meta; feeds Progress.
Inversion lemma From a typing judgment for a compound term, recover typings of its subterms (e.g. |- App(f,a):T ⇒ ∃ S. |- f:Arrow(S,T) ∧ |- a:S). inversion in tsp-meta; feeds Preservation.
Substitution lemma THE crux: Γ,x:S |- e:T and Γ |- v:S ⇒ Γ |- [v/x]e : T. Its kernel proof mirrors tsp-term::subst/shift index arithmetic exactly — an off-by-one in shift breaks the lemma. substitution in tsp-meta.
Weakening Adding an unused binding to the context preserves typings: Γ |- e:T ⇒ Γ,x:S |- shift(e,1,0):T (under de Bruijn, weakening shifts indices). weakening in tsp-meta; prerequisite of substitution.
Small-step semantics Reduction as a single-redex relation e --> e'; iterate to evaluate. Implemented by step(e: &Term) -> Option<Term> in tsp-rules (None = value or stuck). The canonical operational model here.
Big-step semantics Evaluation-to-value relation e ⇓ v in one judgment. NOT the primary model; mentioned only as the alternative to small-step. Soundness here is phrased over small-step.
Evaluation context A term with one hole [·] marking the next redex (E ::= [·] | App(E,e) | App(v,E) | If(E,e,e) | Succ(E) | ...). Encodes congruence/search rules so step reduces left-to-right, call-by-value. tsp-rules, Phase 4.
Value A fully-reduced term: Lam, TmTrue, TmFalse, numerals (Zero/Succ of values), pair/inj of values. Decided by is_value(t: &Term) -> bool in tsp-term. Progress's left disjunct.
Stuck term A closed, non-value term with step(e) == None (e.g. If(Zero, ...)). Type soundness ⇒ no well-typed closed term is ever stuck. The failure state soundness forbids.
Simply-typed lambda calculus (STLC) The base object language: Term/Type with Var/Lam/App + Arrow. The textbook calculus whose soundness is proved first (Phases 1–9), then extended.
System F Polymorphic lambda calculus (universal types). Stretch object language: Type::All/TyVar, type abstraction/application. Phase 12 stress option; brings parametricity as a further stretch.
de Bruijn index Nameless variable: Var(usize) = number of binders between use and its binder. Eliminates alpha-renaming. The representation in tsp-term::Term::Var; indices are usize.
Shift shift(t,d,cutoff) -> Term: add d (an isize) to every free index ≥ cutoff; cutoff rises by 1 under each binder. Adjusts indices crossing a binder. The off-by-one hotspot.
Capture-avoiding substitution subst(t,j,s) -> Term = [s/j]t: replace free index j with s, shifting s under each binder it descends. Capture is automatic given correct shift. Mirrored by the substitution lemma.
Alpha-equivalence Equality of terms up to bound-variable renaming. Under de Bruijn it is structural equality — alpha_eq(a,b) is a plain recursive Term/Type comparison. tsp-term::alpha_eq.
Judgment An atomic assertion in an inductive relation, e.g. Has-Type(ctx,e,T) or Step(e,e'). Judgment { form: Atom, args: Vec<Term> } in tsp-rules.
Inference rule Premises ⊢ conclusion, named: Rule { name, premises: Vec<Judgment>, conclusion: Judgment }. Object-language typing/step rules are Rule data; parsed by tsp-syntax.
Derivation A finite proof tree: a Rule instance with subderivations for each premise. Derivation { rule, subderivations, conclusion }; validity decided by check_deriv(d,rules) -> Result<(),DerivError>.
Inductive relation A relation defined by a finite set of inference rules, closed under those rules (smallest such). Typing and --> are inductive relations realized as Vec<Rule>.
Logical framework (LF) The Edinburgh LF: a dependently-typed metalanguage for encoding judgments and derivations as types and terms. tsp-kernel is an LF-style lambda-Pi core; object judgments are LF inductive signatures (Phase 6).
lambda-Pi The dependently-typed lambda calculus (Π-types) underlying LF. Realized by KTerm { Var, Sort, Pi, Lam, App } with Sort { Type, Kind } (Type : Kind). The kernel's term language. Written "lambda-Pi", never with Greek.
Pi type / dependent function type Pi(A, B) = the type of functions from x:A to B where B may mention x. Encodes implication (non-dependent Pi) and universal quantification (∀). KTerm::Pi(Box<KTerm>,Box<KTerm>).
Proof term A KTerm whose type (a KType) is the proposition it proves. "Valid proof" ≡ check_type(ctx, term, prop) succeeds. A Derivation is reified into a proof term in Phase 6.
Proposition (Prop) A KType viewed logically. Proof { goal: KTerm, term: KTerm } packages term : goal for the kernel to check.
Propositions-as-types The discipline mapping logic to types: implication→Pi/Arrow, ∀→Pi, conjunction→pair, etc.; a theorem is a KType, its proof a KTerm. The encoding strategy of tsp-meta.
Curry-Howard The proofs-as-programs correspondence underpinning propositions-as-types; "proving" = "constructing a well-typed proof term the kernel accepts."
Trusted computing base (TCB) The code whose correctness is assumed, not checked: here exactly tsp-kernel (infer_type/check_type/whnf/equal). Kept small + audited (target low-hundreds of lines); a TCB bug that accepts an ill-typed proof term silently validates a false theorem — the gravest failure.
Bidirectional type checking Split into infer_type(ctx,t) -> KTerm (synthesize a type) and check_type(ctx,t,ty) (check against a given type). Avoids guessing Pi domains; the kernel's checking algorithm (Phase 5).
Definitional equality / conversion equal(ctx,a,b) -> bool: types/terms equal up to beta- (and optionally eta-) reduction. Used by check_type to compare expected vs actual types. Decided via whnf.
Weak-head normal form (whnf) whnf(t) -> KTerm: reduce only until the outermost constructor is exposed (don't normalize under it). The engine of equal and bidirectional checking; lazy enough to terminate on the propositions we encode.
Eliminator / induction principle The recursion/induction scheme for an LF inductive signature; lets a proof case-split and recurse over a derivation. Generated in Phase 6; how Progress/Preservation "do induction on the derivation."
Property-based testing Generate many inputs, assert a property holds (PLT-Redex style). tsp-test: Generator of size-bounded well-typed terms feeds check_progress/check_preservation; complements proofs with fast falsification.
Counterexample shrinking On failure, the Shrinker minimizes the offending Term to a smallest still-failing witness. TestResult::CounterExample(Term, Witness) where Witness records which property broke + how.
Store typing A typing context for the mutable store (heap) Σ mapping locations→types; needed so Ref soundness is preserved across allocation/update. Phase 12 references stretch.
Parametricity Uniform behavior of System F polymorphic functions ("theorems for free"). Stretch goal layered on the System F extension; not required for the soundness gate.
LangDef The parsed object-language definition: { sorts, grammar, judgments, rules } in tsp-syntax. The .lang input the CLI checks/tests against.
Atom An interned identifier used for sorts, judgment forms, and rule names (Judgment.form, Rule.name).
Type Object-language types in tsp-term: Base, Arrow, Prod, Sum, Ref, plus All/TyVar (System F).
Term Object-language terms in tsp-term: Var (de Bruijn), Lam/App, TmTrue/TmFalse/If, Zero/Succ, growing with products/sums/refs.
Context The object typing context Γ (a de Bruijn list of Type) consumed by infer(ctx,e) -> Result<Type,TypeError>. Distinct from the kernel's Ctx.
KTerm / KType Kernel terms and their types (a KType is itself a KTerm). The lambda-Pi syntax checked by the trusted infer_type/check_type.
Ctx The kernel context: a de Bruijn list of KTerm types. Distinct from object Context.
tsp (binary) The tsp-cli executable. Subcommands: check (a LangDef + its soundness proof through the kernel), test (randomized soundness), typecheck (object infer), step (object reduction).
Dual-guard design A deliberately broken typing rule must be caught by BOTH tsp-kernel (its Proof no longer check_types) AND tsp-test (a shrunk CounterExample). The cross-check validating Phase 12.

Every leaf file from the Section-10 repository layout, one purpose-line each. Tree order = workspace root → governance → seven tsp-* crates → benches → conformance corpus → fuzz → examples → docs → CI. Paths relative to repo root. TCB files (the load-bearing trusted base) flagged [TCB].

docs/performance/targets.md Kernel check time, test throughput, proof-term size, peak memory budgets (§4/§31.1).
docs/performance/results/ Committed criterion baselines (JSON) diffed by the bench-regression gate.
docs/failure-analysis/substitution-offbyone.md Canonical bug study: a shift off-by-one breaking the substitution lemma.
docs/failure-analysis/kernel-unsoundness.md Post-mortem template for any accepted ill-typed proof term (F3).
docs/glossary.md Locked terminology (type soundness … parametricity); single source.

A.14 CI workflows (.github/workflows/)

Path Purpose
.github/workflows/ci.yml fmt + clippy -D warnings + build + full test on push/PR.
.github/workflows/audit-tcb.yml Gate: fail PR if tsp-kernel exceeds line budget or gains a dependency.
.github/workflows/conformance.yml Run conformance/run.rs; proofs check, broken variants rejected, oracle matched.
.github/workflows/fuzz.yml Scheduled cargo-fuzz on kernel/parser/subst targets; upload crash artifacts.
.github/workflows/bench.yml Criterion benches; compare against committed targets; regression report.
.github/workflows/audit.yml cargo-deny (licenses/advisories/bans) per deny.toml.
.github/workflows/release.yml Tag v* → build tsp, attach binaries, verify dual-license headers, publish 7 crates in locked order.

A.15 Templates, scripts, tools, and reference/legal directories

Path Purpose
templates/language.lang.tmpl Skeleton .lang: sort decls, grammar, judgment forms, rules — scaffold for a new object language.
templates/lemma.proof.tmpl Proof-term scaffold for authoring a new tsp-meta lemma.
templates/adr.md.tmpl ADR authoring template (mirrors the docs/adr/000N-*.md shape).
scripts/tcb-count.sh Counts tsp-kernel/src lines; fails CI if the TCB budget (C1/DP-1) is exceeded.
scripts/conformance-run.sh Drives conformance/ via tsp; asserts manifest.toml expectations.
scripts/repro.sh Rebuilds from Cargo.lock inside Docker; reproducibility check.
tools/proofdump/ Binary: pretty-prints a KTerm proof term (debugging the kernel).
tools/derivviz/ Binary: renders a Derivation tree as ASCII (debugging check_deriv).
notes/ Working notes: de Bruijn worked examples, design scratch — not normative, no build/CI dependency.
papers/ Cited PDFs/links: TAPL, Software Foundations, PLT Redex, HHP/LF, LambdaPi, de Bruijn 1972 (§5 Required Reading).
legal/NOTICE Third-party attribution notice accompanying the dual MIT/Apache-2.0 license.
legal/ (third-party license texts) Upstream license texts for any vetted [dev-dependencies] (per deny.toml allow-list).
brand/ Logo + ASCII banner rendered by tsp --version.

Appendix B — Milestone Summary

Single source of truth mapping each LOCKED phase (doc headings ## 12..## 25) to its deliverable, duration, key risks, and measurable exit criteria. Durations sum to 22 weeks, matching the Section 1 timeline. Risk severity uses G = gravest class (TCB unsoundness / silent acceptance of an ill-typed proof term), H = high, M = medium.

Phase Heading Deliverable Duration Key Risks Exit Criteria (measurable)
0 — Skeleton ## 12 7 tsp-* crate shells (tsp-syntax/tsp-term/tsp-rules/tsp-kernel/tsp-meta/tsp-test/tsp-cli); Term/Type/Judgment/Rule/Derivation/KTerm type shells; error enums; CI + Makefile + Dockerfile 1 wk M: workspace dep-cycle (tsp-meta→tsp-rules+tsp-kernel); M: no_std+alloc misconfig on lib crates cargo build + cargo test green in CI + Docker; smoke test: infer types Lam(Bool, Var 0) : Bool->Bool, step((Lam x. x) true) = Some(TmTrue); clippy -D warnings clean
1 — Object terms + binding ## 13 de Bruijn Term/Type; shift(t,d,cutoff), capture-avoiding subst(t,j,s), structural alpha_eq, is_value 1.5 wk H: off-by-one in shift cutoff → silent capture, later breaks substitution lemma (Phase 7); M: isize underflow on negative shift 50+ shift/subst unit fixtures pass; subst∘shift round-trip property (10k proptest cases) holds; alpha_eq reflexive/symmetric/transitive on fixtures; zero capture in nested-binder corpus
2 — The metalanguage ## 14 LangDef parser (sorts, term/type grammars w/ binders, JudgmentForm, Rule); object-term parser; ParseError/ParseErrorKind diagnostics 1.5 wk M: ambiguous grammar (binder vs application precedence); M: poor error spans hide MalformedRule STLC .lang round-trips parse→print→parse identically; all 4 ParseErrorKind variants exercised by negative fixtures w/ correct span; 30+ object terms parse to expected Term
3 — Inductive rule engine ## 15 typing+step Rule data; executable infer/check (STLC); Derivation + check_deriv validating tree vs rules 1.5 wk H: check_deriv accepts an invalid tree (premise/conclusion mismatch) → false confidence; M: infer divergence from declared rules infer agrees with hand-typed fixtures (40+); check_deriv rejects every mutated subderivation in mutation corpus; valid STLC derivation trees accepted; TypeError/DerivError paths covered
4 — Small-step semantics ## 16 reduction rules, values, evaluation contexts / congruence, executable step; (opt) determinism of step 1 wk M: missing congruence rule → premature stuck; M: redex/value overlap breaks determinism step reaches a value on all closed well-typed fixtures; None iff value-or-stuck; determinism property (step ⇒ ≤1 result) holds on 10k proptest cases
5 — Proof-kernel core ## 17 KTerm (lambda-Pi), Sort(Type:Kind), Ctx; bidirectional infer_type/check_type (TRUSTED); whnf; definitional equal (beta, opt eta) 2.5 wk G: infer_type/check_type accepts an ill-typed KTerm → unsoundness of TCB; H: whnf non-termination on adversarial term; H: equal unsound conversion TCB ≤ 800 LOC (audited); positive/negative kernel fixtures (60+) all correct; whnf terminates on every fixture; equal decides beta(/eta) correctly; zero ill-typed term accepted in seed corpus
6 — Propositions-as-types encoding ## 18 object judgments (typing, step) as LF inductive signatures; induction/elimination principles; Derivation→kernel proof term 1.5 wk H: encoding admits a proof of False (mis-stated eliminator); M: signature/Rule drift Each STLC rule has a kernel signature that check_type accepts; round-trip Derivation→KTerm→check_type succeeds; eliminators type-check; no signature proves a non-derivable judgment
7 — Structural lemmas ## 19 machine-checked canonical_forms, inversion, weakening, substitution lemma (Γ,x:S⊢e:T ∧ Γ⊢v:S ⇒ Γ⊢[v/x]e:T) 2 wk G: substitution-lemma proof term mis-checks an off-by-one yet kernel passes (mirrors tsp-term::subst bug); H: inversion cases missed All 4 lemmas: Proof{term:goal} accepted by check_type; substitution lemma's de Bruijn arithmetic matches tsp-term::subst (cross-checked on shared fixtures); canonical-forms exhaustive over Type constructors
8 — Preservation ## 20 preservation_statement(lang) + machine-checked proof: ⊢e:T ∧ e-->e' ⇒ ⊢e':T (via inversion + substitution lemma) 1.5 wk H: beta/elimination case relies on a not-yet-proven lemma; G: proof term checks but goal is mis-stated preservation_statement is the exact LOCKED Prop; check_type accepts the proof at that goal; every step rule has a covered case; reuses Phase 7 lemmas with no admit/hole
9 — Progress ## 21 progress_statement(lang) + machine-checked proof: ⊢e:T ⇒ value(e) ∨ ∃e'. e-->e' (via canonical_forms) 1 wk H: stuck-term case unhandled (e.g. If on non-bool) → progress false; G: mis-stated disjunction progress_statement is the exact LOCKED Prop; check_type accepts proof; canonical-forms invoked at each elimination form; no closed well-typed fixture is stuck
10 — Soundness + extension ## 22 type-soundness corollary (well-typed ⇒ never stuck); extend object language (naturals, booleans, products, sums); re-prove progress + preservation 1.5 wk H: new constructor breaks canonical-forms exhaustiveness; M: extension regresses STLC proofs Soundness corollary check_type-accepted; progress+preservation re-checked for extended calculus; STLC proofs still pass (no regression); is_value/step/infer cover all new forms
11 — Redex-style testing ## 23 Generator of size-bounded well-typed Term; check_progress/check_preservation; Shrinker→minimal CounterExample; cross-validate vs proofs 1.5 wk M: generator skewed (low type coverage) misses bugs; M: Shrinker produces non-minimal/ill-typed witness Generator emits only well-typed terms (validated by infer); ≥10⁵ terms/run yield only TestResult::Pass on sound language; shrinker output minimal + still triggers Witness; throughput ≥ 50k terms/s
12 — Stress + broken-rule demo ## 24 references (store + store typing) OR System F extension; soundness proof extends; one deliberately broken typing rule caught by BOTH kernel (proof fails) AND tester (shrunk counterexample) 2 wk H: store-typing invariant subtlety (Ref aliasing); G: broken rule still "proves" — kernel false-accept; M: tester misses the bug Stretch calculus: progress+preservation check_type-accepted; broken rule ⇒ proof term rejected by kernel AND tester emits a shrunk CounterExample(Term, Witness); dual-guard demonstrated end-to-end
13 — Validation ## 25 conformance suite (proofs check; broken variants rejected; tester⇔proofs agree); kernel fuzzing; benchmarks vs targets 2 wk G: fuzzer finds a KTerm the kernel false-accepts; H: fuzzer panics kernel (DoS); M: perf below target Conformance 100% green; ≥ 10⁷ fuzz iters: zero ill-typed proof accepted, zero panic on adversarial KTerm; kernel check ≤ 10 ms/proof; test throughput ≥ 50k/s; all Section-1 targets met → "done" gate

Duration roll-up: 1 + 1.5 + 1.5 + 1.5 + 1 + 2.5 + 1.5 + 2 + 1.5 + 1 + 1.5 + 1.5 + 2 + 2 = 22 weeks, equal to the Section 1 schedule. Critical path runs through the TCB: Phase 5 (kernel) → Phase 7 (substitution lemma) → Phases 8–9 (preservation/progress); these four carry every G-class risk and gate all downstream proofs.

"Phase-0 done" vs "full done." Phase-0 done means the seven tsp-* crates build and test green in CI/Docker with all core type shells (Term/Type/Judgment/Rule/Derivation/KTerm) present and the two smoke checks passing (infer types Lam(Bool, Var 0) : Bool->Bool; step((Lam x. x) true) = Some(TmTrue)) — a compilable skeleton with no metatheory yet. Full done (Phase 13 gate) means the type-soundness metatheory is machine-checked: progress_statement and preservation_statement are stated as the LOCKED Props and their proof terms are accepted by the trusted lambda-Pi kernel (check_type) atop the audited TCB (≤ 800 LOC); the Redex-style tester finds only Pass on the sound calculus; the deliberately broken rule is caught by both guards (kernel rejection + shrunk CounterExample); and fuzzing (≥ 10⁷ iters) shows the kernel never accepts an ill-typed proof term and never panics — all consistent with the Section 1 targets.
